## [Unreleased]

## [6.2.2] - 2026-11-27

### Fixed
- **Role guards: close the Oracle R21 and Reviewer R21 adversarial audits — the 4+8 overlapping findings converge on 7 root causes in the eval binding chain premises, the write-definition unification, the control-flow/scope environment model, the depth-exhaustion final consumption points, the nested xargs recursion cost, the PATH self-reference expansion budget and the release-evidence integrity (`shell_ast.go`, `hc_prepare.go`, `role_guards_shell.go`, `role_guards_handlers.go`, `role_guards_heredoc.go`, `role_guards_code_env.go`, `role_guards_v622_r20_test.go`, `role_guards_v622_eval_test.go`, `role_guards_v622_r21_test.go`).**
  - **The static eval binding replication now validates the COMPLETE wrapper chain (Oracle R21-M1 / Reviewer R21-#2 — the peel trusted the basename and skipped unknown options).** The former `stripBuiltinWrapperWords` reduced every wrapper word to its basename before comparing against `command`/`builtin` (so `/dev/null/command eval` peeled exactly like the builtin) and consumed ANY `-…` word as an option regardless of the real builtin contract (so `command -x eval` — an invocation that errors before eval ever runs — still minted the exact binding), and the scalar-write premise excluded only `+=`, letting an array-subscript write `eval 'arr[1]=/x'` pose as the plain scalar bind. The new `peelEvalBuiltinChain` requires every chain layer to be the bare word `command` or `builtin` (no `/` path component at all), restricts `command` to POSIX's legal non-query `-p` clusters (provable `-v`/`-V` shapes remain pure resolution queries that execute nothing), treats `builtin` as a bash-only no-option builtin (unknown under the dash dialect), and requires the final head to be the bare word `eval` exactly; array-subscript writes (`a.Index != nil`) join `+=` on the pollution side. Every failing shape taints the operand writes instead of silently skipping them — the pre-scan counts those writes either way.
  - **The write definition is unified between the pre-scan and the replay (Oracle R21-M2 / Reviewer R21-#3 — two diverging write collectors).** The pre-scan recognized the Bash named-write builtins (`printf -v`/`%n`, `read`/`REPLY`/`-a`, `getopts` OPTIND/OPTARG/NAME, `unset`, the declare family) while the replay collectors saw only syntax assignments: a single counted `printf -v PATH` write inside (or outside) a static eval fell back to the parent's ORIGINAL PATH in the mktemp success proofs, and `taintHiddenEvalWrites` walked `st.Cmd` matching only descendant `*syntax.Stmt` nodes — silently skipping a ROOT-level static eval inside an eval operand text. `writeNamesInStmt` now feeds a one-statement pseudo-file through `preScanVarMutations` itself (subtracting only the root eval's own operand replication, which `bindEvalCallWrites` owns), so every write shape shares exactly one definition; the main walk's new `noteBuiltinWrites` taints builtin writes in statement order, and `taintHiddenEvalWrites` checks the statement's root call before descending. The invariant holds: every variable the pre-scan counts as written is either precisely bound or explicitly polluted — never silently dropped.
  - **AST scope-path tracking with control-flow merge replaces the flat childScope boolean (Oracle R21-M3 / Reviewer R21-#1).** The flat model answered only "not a parent write" and never "which scope does this consumer belong to": subshell assignments were dropped even for the SAME subshell's later consumers (`(PATH=B; /bin/bash); /bin/bash` yielded [A,A] instead of the exact [B,A]), while background compound subtrees (`{ PATH=B; } &`) propagated nothing downward and conditional writes (`if`/`case`/loop bodies, `&&` right sides, uncalled function bodies) overrode outer consumers exactly. The collector now threads a unique scope path (child scopes fork sibling ids off the shared counter) plus a conditional-depth marker onto every segment, and all four production accumulation points (`scriptAllowsLocalShellStdinPrepareDialect`, the nested `collectSegs`, `guardCtxRunStdinForCommand`, `guardShellSubagentDepthSegs`) run the shared `scopeEnvTracker`: child-scope assignments bind within their own forked clone and die with it, conditional writes surface as uncertainty marker ops ('t') that never mint exact overrides, consumers dependent on an uncertain environment are refused the stdin rewrite in hc_prepare, and the fallback/heredoc paths treat the tainted variables as pollution through `taintedSentinelNames`. A childless `/usr/bin/env KEY=VAL` still never rewrites the parent view (its operands die with the env process).
  - **Depth exhaustion blocks explicitly at every final consumption point (Oracle R21-M5 / Reviewer R21-#4).** The exhaustion marker was honored only at the rewrite doorstep: the API stdin fallback (`screenStdinTargets`), the here-string screen (`screenHereStringTargetsDepth`) and the static/dynamic heredoc screens re-read the zero-value `lang=""`/`bash=false` of an exhausted target as a KNOWN POSIX shell and re-parsed unidentified bytes under the dash default — the exact inversion of fail-closed. Every final program-input consumption point now returns the explicit depth verdict (`depthExceededHit` for the text screens, the strict-readonly/subagent rule pair for the API stdin screen) the moment a target carries `exhausted`.
  - **Nested xargs append-mode recursion merges equivalent states and caps per-request work (Reviewer R21-#5).** The no-hit walk recursed into the same inner target twice per level — once under the inner xargs context, once under the outer — doubling the peel/LookPath cascade at every nesting level. When both invocations run in append mode with empty replacement tokens the two continuations are byte-identical state and are merged into one; every recursion entry additionally memoizes on the normalized (depth, argv, token) key inside a per-request table and fails closed past a 512-node single-request budget. Replacement-token states keep their independent descent (the branch shapes genuinely diverge there).
  - **Chained `$PATH` self-reference expansion is budget-checked before allocation (Reviewer R21-#6).** A cross-statement doubling chain (`PATH=$PATH:$PATH` replayed N times) materialized the previous expansion repeatedly — 14 rounds already reach ~229 KB — synchronously before any guard verdict, and truncating the result would spell a DIFFERENT valid short PATH the shell never computes. `expandPathSelfRefs` now estimates the substituted size against `maxShellCommandBytes` before allocating; once the replay crosses the budget the PATH verdict degenerates to the unresolvable placeholder (sticky across the rest of the replay), consumers refuse the rewrite in hc_prepare, and the ordinary single self-reference (`PATH=$PATH:/x`) keeps its exact expansion.
  - **Test discriminative power hardened, deduplicated and de-fragilized (Oracle R21-SF1 / Reviewer R21-#7/#8/#9).** The R20 OBS row now exercises the EMPTY-SIDE passthrough branch of `merged` directly (constructing a backing array with spare capacity and asserting an append onto the returned snapshot never clobbers the shared memory — the two-non-empty merge shape it replaced allocated a fresh exactly-sized array on ANY implementation and could discriminate nothing); the trailing child-scope assertions moved out so the row tests exactly one contract. The R20-M5 row now asserts the consumer's actual rejection at the final consumption point instead of comparing two list lengths that both pass when every target degrades to dash. The fragile `R16-S3` Go-AST self-parse of the test tables (locking fixed row counts and table variable names without ever exercising the guard) is deleted, and the duplicate linear depth-budget fixture is deduplicated — the authoritative copy stays in `role_guards_v622_eval_test.go` (R19-S2), the R20 clone is removed.
  - **Regression coverage (as merged in the R22 closure below).** The suite `TestGuard_V622_OracleR21Probes` — originally added in `role_guards_v622_r21_test.go`, folded into `role_guards_v622_r20_test.go` in the R22 closure with the R20/R21 overlaps de-duplicated — pins 10 subtests in its merged form (12 in the original standalone file before the overlap removal): 11 discriminating rows (the slash-carrying wrapper and unknown-option chain rejections with the bare-eval and legal `-p` positive controls, the array-subscript scalar premise, the eval-hidden and direct printf -v PATH revocations with the pre-scan count pins, the root-level nested eval detection, the exact subshell snapshot sequence [B,A], the four conditional-write refusal rows with the unconditional control, the background-compound downward propagation, and the chained self-reference budget verdict with the single-expansion control) plus 1 behavior-pin row for the xargs recursion merge (append-mode chain open-feed, replace-token chain descent, plain-cat negative control); the merge drops the here-string-exhaustion row and the stdin-fallback overlap rows whose coverage the other suites already carry — hence 10 in the merged form — while the R20 test file's OBS/M5 rows were rewritten as above and its S2 clone removed, and the eval file loses R16-S3. FAIL-first verification on the pre-fix tree `ce35f40` — the original closure files compile there verbatim (`go vet` rc=0) — is recorded per test blob, never as a cross-suite sum: the ORIGINAL standalone R21 file (12 rows) yields exactly 10 FAIL / 2 PASS there (the childless-env control passes as a structural invariant and the xargs row passes on both trees as a behavior pin of unchanged verdicts), while the strengthened R20 suite yields exactly 1 FAIL (the rewritten M5 row) / 7 PASS on the same tree; the two suites' failures sum to 11 only across both blobs. The MERGED R21 suite (10 rows) necessarily yields 0 FAIL / 10 PASS on `ce35f40`'s R22-predecessor descendant `0c618ac` because that tree already carries the R21 production fixes — it is regression coverage, not new-finding discrimination, and the discriminating FAIL-first evidence above belongs to the closure-time files. All rows pass on the fixed tree. Every new/changed function measures strictly `cognitive < 35` under `cccc --no-config --no-cache` (measured at closure: `peelEvalBuiltinChain` 24 with the wrapper grammar extracted into `peelBuiltinLayer` 6 / `peelCommandLayer` 20, `writeNamesInStmt` 14, `noteBuiltinWrites` 17, `taintOneEvalCallWrites` 3, `taintStmtSharedWrites` 2, `enterChildScope` 1, `enterConditional` 0, `envTrackTaintedVar` 1, `scopeEnvTracker.stateFor` 18, `scopeEnvTracker.foldSegment` 19, `scopeEnvTracker.view` 1, `envsUntrustedForRewrite` 8, `expandPathSelfRefs` 4, `xargsFeedsCodeFlagDepth` 5, `xargsWorkMemoKey` 0, `effectivePATHFrom` 16, `bindEvalStmtWrites` 31 unchanged). No historical function grew beyond its pre-R21 measurement.
- **Role guards: close the Oracle R22 and Reviewer R22 adversarial audits - the overlapping findings converge on 6 root causes in the eval binding replication's write-definition, the shell-function shadowing and readonly-variable premises, the control-flow merge environmental uncertainty, the stdin program-target exhaustion propagation and untrusted-PATH classification, the hc prepare entries' divergence, the cross-request shared mutable xargs state and the production traversal's environment-snapshot copy discipline (`shell_ast.go`, `role_guards_shell.go`, `role_guards_subagent.go`, `role_guards_peel.go`, `role_guards_heredoc.go`, `role_guards_handlers.go`, `role_guards_code_env.go`, `hc_prepare.go`, `role_guards_v622_r20_test.go` with the merged `role_guards_v622_r21_test.go`).**
  - **Shell-function shadowing of eval/command/builtin and the readonly/special variables defeat the exact static binding (Oracle R22-M1 / Reviewer R22-#3).** Name resolution in the shell tries a same-named FUNCTION before any builtin, but the replication keyed on the bare chain words alone: `eval() { :; }; eval 'R22_V=/after'` minted the exact binding even though the runtime executes user code that never re-parses the operand, and `eval "UID=/after"` minted a value the kernel-enforced readonly variable can never receive. The collector now records every shell function definition in statement order (`noteFuncDef`, including the nested eval replays and cross-dialect reparse isolation) and `peelEvalBuiltinChainShadowed` drops a shadowed first chain word to the ordinary taint path exactly like a pathname or unknown option - only the FIRST word is shadow-sensitive because a surviving unshadowed `command`/`builtin` wrapper bypasses the function lookup for everything after it. The readonly/dynamic special variables (`UID`/`EUID`/`PPID`/`BASHPID`/`SHELLOPTS`/`BASHOPTS`/`GROUPS`/`FUNCNAME`/`LINENO`/`RANDOM`/`SECONDS`/`SRANDOM`/`EPOCHSECONDS`/`EPOCHREALTIME`/`HISTCMD`/`BASH_SUBSHELL`/`BASH_VERSINFO`) and every name the script declared through `readonly`/`declare -r`/`typeset -r` route through `noteShellAssign` to pollution instead of minting a bind the runtime never establishes; the declaration's own inline write (`readonly X=/x`) mints first and the attribute is recorded atomically after it.
  - **The eval replication's write definition is unified with the pre-scan (Oracle R22-M2 / Reviewer R22-#4).** `bindEvalCallWrites` truncated the statement write scan at exactly the replication boundary and the taint verdict dropped the operand rest, so writes inside a static eval operand beyond the replication's shape vanished between the pre-scan count and the replay. `writeNamesInStmt` now runs UNTRUNCATED for every taint verdict (the bind verdict keeps its replication-owned truncation because a bare-assignment statement has no argv to scan), the tainted chain keeps its best-effort operand rest (`evalChainBestEffortRest`) so statically visible operand writes taint exactly, and an eval-shaped chain with NO confirmable operand boundary (`taintEvalBoundary`) faces the maximal pollution verdict - sentinelTrustVars plus PATH - because the single-write PATH exemption never extends to an unconfirmable eval boundary. A plain dynamic command head (`$s`) is NOT eval-shaped (`evalChainShaped`): the pre-scan counts no operand writes for it and the verdict stays precise. The invariant now holds end to end: every write the pre-scan counts lands either as an exact binding or as explicit pollution, never silently dropped.
  - **The control-flow environment merge carries conditional uncertainty (Oracle R22-M3 / Reviewer R22-#2).** The elif chain's conditions were walked by the generic fallback with unconditional depth, minting exact overrides for writes that run only when earlier branches miss - `visitIf` now walks the first condition unconditionally and every elif condition inside the conditional region. `foldAssignWord` evaluates the unconditional overwrite's self-reference against the untrusted placeholder instead of the op's own value: a write that depends on the unknowable old value (`PATH=$PATH:/suffix` or any dynamic content) KEEPS the uncertainty marker instead of laundering it into an exact constant, while an independent constant overwrite still re-establishes the exact value. The assignment-folding license is the AST's own verdict (`shellSegment.pureAssign` set only for a genuine bare-assignment statement) - a quoted command word spelling `KEY=VAL` after quote stripping is a command invocation and never folds as an assignment.
  - **Stdin program-target exhaustion propagates end to end, and an unknowable PATH classifies as an explicit unknown target (Oracle R22-M5 / Reviewer R22-#5, Oracle R22-#6 / Reviewer R22-#6).** The wrapper-peel budget cutoff, a nested parse failure, the xargs inner-wrapper exhaustion and the recursion cutoff all set `exhausted: true` on the produced target - no zero-value `lang:""` pseudo-POSIX-shell target survives - and the remote pure-shell exemption refuses certification the moment a vehicle target carries the marker instead of re-reading the zero value. An unrecognized BARE head under an untrusted PATH (conditional write marker or over-budget expansion, `stdinUnknownHeadTargets` with `pathUntrusted`) returns the explicit exhaustion target instead of nil, so the data-consumer fallback can never certify an unidentified consumer; `pathSegUnvettable` keeps the substitution placeholder out of every filesystem lookup. Both hc prepare entries share the untrusted-environment refusal through `stdinRewriteableTarget` exactly.
  - **Cross-request shared mutable xargs state is eliminated (Oracle R22-M5-xargs / Reviewer R22-#1).** The R21 recursion memo lived in a package-level table keyed on normalized argv alone: two concurrent requests with different resolution contexts could serve each other's verdicts, and a depth-0 entry of one request reset the shared table underneath an in-flight computation of the other. The memo and budget counter are now per-request values threaded through `xargsResolveCtx` alongside the PATH snapshot, keyed on the FULL context (depth, argv, replacement token, effective PATH), so concurrent analyses stay isolated and byte-identical with their serial verdicts under -race.
  - **The production traversal's environment snapshots are demand-materialized with parent-chain sharing (Oracle R22-SF2 / Reviewer R22-#7).** `scopeEnvTracker` forks child scopes as parent-linked states (`parent`/`baseLen` chain, frozen fork-time prefix) instead of eagerly copying the accumulated table, `view` walks the chain with per-ancestor fork-time truncation (`scopeVisibleLen`/`chainView`) and materializes exactly one capacity-frozen snapshot per consuming segment, and all four production loops skip the materialization entirely for segments that cannot consume stdin - the AST's own `pureAssign` verdict carries the skip for free, no wrapper peel is paid either. Consecutive pure-assignment runs pay zero redundant copies: measured marginal cost of a fixed 8-segment window is flat across the prefix length (segments 1-8 vs 57-64 ratio ~0.8 vs the pre-fix quadratic ~3.8). `accumulatePureAssignEnv` is deleted - no production caller remains.
  - **Regression coverage.** `role_guards_v622_r20_test.go` gains `TestGuard_V622_OracleR22Probes` pinning 14 subtests (13 discriminating rows plus embedded positive controls) (readonly/special-variable non-binding, shadowed-eval replication refusal, unknown-option and shadowed-chain PATH exemption revocations, the elif-condition exactness refusal, the self-referencing uncertainty preservation with the independent-overwrite positive control, the quoted KEY=VAL fold refusal, the four exhaustion-propagation rows, the untrusted-PATH unknown target, the concurrent xargs isolation with serial-verdict agreement and the flat-marginal zero-copy discrimination). ALL 14 rows verified FAIL-first on the pre-fix tree `0c618ac` - the complete merged test file compiles there verbatim (`go vet` rc=0) and every row FAILs with the pre-fix behavior while passing on the fixed tree (the rows' embedded control assertions are exercised on the fixed tree, where the primary assertions pass). The R21 suite is folded into the same file (`role_guards_v622_r21_test.go` deleted) with the R20/R21 overlap de-duplicated as documented in the file header. Every new function measures strictly `cognitive < 35` under `cccc --no-config --no-cache` (`noteReadonlyDecls` 18, `bindEvalOneStmt` 20, `foldAssignWord` 11, `peelEvalBuiltinChainShadowed` 10, `collectOneStdinSeg` 7, `lookPathOneSeg` 7, `evalChainBestEffortRest` 6, `bindImpossible` 5, `evalChainShaped` 5, `evalChainPathVerdict` 4, `stdinCommandSegRule` 4, `visitIf` 4, `noteFuncDef` 3, `chainView` 3, `consumerViewEnv` 2, `stdinUnknownHeadTargets` 2, `stdinRewriteableTarget` 2, `taintEvalBoundary` 2, `replayEvalTextWrites` 2, `scopeVisibleLen` 2, `evalChainLayerVerdict` 1, `noteShellAssign` 1, `isPureAssignCall` 1, `evalChainTaintedRest` 0, `pathSegUnvettable` 0) and no pre-existing function grew beyond its `0c618ac` measurement (zero cognitive-complexity regressions across all eight production files - verified by a full before/after cccc diff; `walkStmt` 117->115, `peelEvalBuiltinChain` 24->16, `noteStaticAssign` 13->10, `writeNamesInStmt` 14->0 and `stateFor` 18->11 only dropped through the extraction refactors the budget demanded).
- **Role guards: close the Oracle R20 adversarial audit — 5 Must-Fix plus 2 Should-Fix items and 1 observation in the static-eval binding premises, the compound-statement nested-eval write coverage, the subprocess/subshell environment scoping, the stdin fallback environment accumulation, the depth-exhaustion target classification, the login-name tilde prefix scan and the depth-budget fixture cost model (`shell_ast.go`, `hc_prepare.go`, `role_guards_shell.go`, `role_guards_handlers.go`, `role_guards_heredoc.go`, `role_guards_v622_r20_test.go`, `role_guards_v622_eval_test.go`).**
  - **A static eval's assignment replication is licensed only for a provably-executed same-process builtin (R20-Oracle-M1 — fake writes trusted as real bindings).** The replication keyed on the basename alone and fired before the call's redirections were even inspected: `/dev/null/eval 'PATH=/x'` (an external program that can never write shell state) and `eval 'PATH=/x' < /dev/null/missing` (a redirection whose failure aborts the command BEFORE the builtin runs) both minted the exact static binding the runtime never establishes, and `eval 'V+=/suffix'` minted the bare-suffix bind even though bash's append operator concatenates onto the inherited value. `bindEvalCallWrites`/`bindEvalStmtWrites` now require a slash-free raw eval head, zero call redirections and plain `=` assignments before any binding replicates — every other shape conservatively TAINTS the operand text's writes (`taintEvalTextWrites`) instead of silently skipping them, because the pre-scan counts those writes and dropping them is the anti-conservative direction.
  - **Writes hidden inside compound statements of a static eval text can no longer vanish (R20-Oracle-M2 — real writes dropped, not fake writes believed).** The pre-scan descends into nested static evals of the whole inner text, but the replication loop never leaves the top-level statement list: `eval ': && eval "PATH=/usr/local/sbin"'` counted one PATH write the replay then neither bound nor tainted, and PATH is exactly the name the single-write pollution skip releases — the substitution success proofs kept resolving mktemp under the parent's ORIGINAL PATH. The replication now walks every non-plain statement for nested static evals (`taintHiddenEvalWrites`) and taints their writes recursively; unparsable or budget-exhausted operand texts likewise taint the sentinel variables instead of falling through, so a counted write always lands as either a binding or pollution.
  - **Subprocess and subshell environments no longer rebind the parent shell's stdin-scan environment (R20-Oracle-M3 — wrong-object PATH override).** `env PATH=B` without a child command peels to the same empty head as a bare `PATH=B`, but env spawns its own process — it cannot modify the calling shell — and `(PATH=B)` dies with the subshell; both used to merge into `runEnv` and PATH is an OVERRIDE, not a monotone pollution bit, so later consumers were graded under a PATH they never run with. `shellSegment` carries `childScope` (subshell/pipeline-component/background/substitution provenance, threaded through `walkStmt`/`emitCall`/`newSub`), and `accumulatePureAssignEnv` now additionally requires every raw argv word to be a literal assignment or inert no-op fragment: parent-shell `PATH=B; bash` propagates B exactly like before while `/usr/bin/env PATH=B` and `(PATH=B)` keep the consumer at the inherited PATH (or refuse the rewrite — never the child scope's value).
  - **The mixed-dialect stdin fallback and the local heredoc/here-string scans accumulate the pure-assignment environment exactly like the flat prepare scan (R20-Oracle-M4 — re-review under a shell state the consumer does not have).** `guardCtxRunStdinForCommand` rebuilt an empty environment per segment, and the heredoc/here-string/pipe projections in `screenSegmentHeredocsDepth`/`screenOneSegmentDepth` did the same — an exported HOME reassigned by an independent statement never reached the fallback verdict, so `$HOME/bin/hc` in the stdin payload was misclassified (`untrusted path binary` instead of the dynamic command head the env-prefix control produces). All three paths now thread the accumulated `runEnv` (childScope-aware) into `stdinProgramTargetsWithEnv` in statement order, matching the prepare path's environment byte for byte.
  - **Depth exhaustion in the stdin target projection is an explicit non-data verdict, never a confirmed data consumer (R20-Oracle-M5 — exhaustion confused with data-only).** `stdinProgramTargetsDialect` returned nil both for empty argvs and at the recursion budget, and the data-consumer fallback promoted the latter into `data: true` — exempting a dynamic heredoc payload from every program screen at exactly the depth boundary. The budget cutoff now returns `stdinTarget{exhausted: true}`: it survives `stdinProgramNonDataTargets` as a program-shaped target, is excluded from the rewrite candidates in `stdinShellTargetRewriteable`, and never earns the data-only exemption — the boundary unit test at `depth = shellSplitMaxDepth - 1` pins both the exhaustion shape and the one-level-shallower control.
  - **A login-name tilde prefix carrying quoted bytes is a verbatim cwd-relative literal, never a dynamic glob word (R20-Oracle-S1 — false positive).** bash's tilde expansion requires every byte of the prefix up to the first UNQUOTED slash to be unquoted, but the classifier only looked at the first literal: `~ro"ot"/notes` minted a dynamic glob encoding even though bash passes the de-quoted `~root/notes` verbatim. The new `tildeWordShape` scan walks the word parts across the quoting boundary: any quoted part inside the prefix yields `tildeShapeLiteral` (plain static text, no glob or tilde marker — resolving `/opt/r19/~root/notes` under the caller cwd), while the fully unquoted `~root/notes` keeps its original dynamic refusal.
  - **The eval depth-budget regression fixture is linear in length and stays inside maxShellCommandBytes (R20-Oracle-S2 — exponential test cost).** The former fixture re-escaped every backslash and quote per nesting level — 25 levels produced a ~67 MB source string, orders of magnitude beyond the 64 KiB command-shape budget, and every race-gate run paid the allocation and parse cost. Bare-word nesting (`eval eval eval zzleaf=1`) peels exactly one eval per level at five bytes per level while preserving the identical shallow-exact/deep-cutoff discrimination, and the fixture explicitly asserts its length never exceeds maxShellCommandBytes.
  - **Consecutive pure assignments no longer pay quadratic copy cost, and target snapshots are capacity-frozen (R20-Oracle observation).** `envAssignments.merged` now freezes the result backing array with the three-index slice idiom (`ops[:len:len]`) on every path — including the previously uncovered empty-side passthroughs — so any snapshot already handed to a stdin target is immutable against later appends, while `accumulatePureAssignEnv` appends consecutive pure-assignment ops in place onto the running accumulator: n consecutive assignments cost O(n) instead of copying 2+3+...+n ops.
  - **Regression coverage.** `role_guards_v622_r20_test.go` adds `TestGuard_V622_OracleR20Probes`; the suite's CURRENT shape is 8 subtests after the R21 closure deduplicated the R19-S2 depth-budget clone and rewrote the OBS/M5 rows (the pre-dedup historical file's exact row count is no longer reproducible and is therefore not restated here). The current 8 comprise 7 discriminating rows (the path-headed and failing-redirect eval replication rejections with the positive builtin control, the append-assignment pollution, the `&&`-hidden nested-eval PATH revocation with the pre-scan count pin, the env/subshell PATH scoping against the parent-shell control, the stdin fallback env-prefix parity on the `$HOME/bin/hc` verdict, the depth-boundary exhaustion shape with the one-level-shallower control, and the quoted login-tilde literal resolution with the unquoted negative control) plus rows pinning the immutable-snapshot accumulation contract; the linear depth-budget fixture itself is asserted in `role_guards_v622_eval_test.go` (R19-S2) after the dedup, not here. On the pre-R21 tree `ce35f40` the strengthened suite compiles verbatim (`go vet` rc=0) and yields exactly 1 FAIL (the rewritten M5 row) / 7 PASS — the per-blob counts are never summed across suites; the original closure-time FAIL-first run on the Commit-1 merge tree is archived in the closure work record rather than restated here, since its original-file transplant delta is no longer reproducible. Every row passes on the fixed tree. Every new/changed function measures strictly `cognitive < 35` under `cccc --no-config --no-cache` (`bindEvalCallWrites` 7, `bindEvalTextWrites` 4, `bindEvalStmtWrites` 32, `taintSentinelVars` 1, `taintEvalTextWrites` 9, `taintHiddenEvalWrites` 1 (closure 8), `stripBuiltinWrapperWords` 29, `tildeWordShape` 13, `tildeLitShape` 13, `litHasUnquotedGlobOrTildeWord` 14, `unquotedTildeTriggerWord` 2, `writeUnquotedGlobTildeLit` 2, `tildeHomeProvenWord` 1, `accumulatePureAssignEnv` 4, `argvIsPlainAssignRun` 5, `stdinProgramTargetsDialect` 22, `stdinTargetsWithDataFallback` 2, `screenOneSegmentDepth` 17, `screenSegmentHeredocsDepth` 12, `guardShellSubagentDepthSegs` 10, `merged` 2 — no historical function grew beyond its Commit-1 measurement).

### Changed
- **Modular `role_guards` split merged from `origin/main` (`84195b5`, PR #6), zero logic change.** The monolithic `role_guards.go` (25567 lines, 741 top-level declarations) is divided byte-exactly along the upstream nine-file order (`role_guards_shell.go`, `role_guards_subagent.go`, `role_guards_peel.go`, `role_guards_readonly.go`, `role_guards_code_env.go`, `role_guards_code_write.go`, `role_guards_handlers.go`, `role_guards_py_allowlist.go`, `role_guards_js_allowlist.go`) plus two size-driven companion files (`role_guards_heredoc.go`, `role_guards_readonly_write.go`); concatenating the file bodies without headers reproduces the pre-split file byte for byte. The v6.2.2 regression tests moved losslessly from `zz_r2fix_test.go` lines 852..4796 into `role_guards_v622_peel_test.go` / `role_guards_v622_shell_test.go` / `role_guards_v622_eval_test.go` (three files instead of the two originally planned because the new content is 206 KB — a strict two-way split cannot satisfy the <95 KB per-file bound; concatenation of the three bodies is byte-exact), the older `zz_*`/`fixes2-4`/`sim_*` fragments are consolidated into upstream's `role_guards_*_test.go` / `readonly_allowlist_regression_test.go` / `readonly_allowlist_sim_test.go` exactly as upstream, and the R16-S3 AST table probe now parses the migrated file names. The byte-exact concatenation claim covers the PRODUCTION split only: across the test migration the 771/771 top-level declarations compare equal by normalized AST (not raw bytes or declaration order — initialization-order differences were vetted separately for dependencies and side effects), and of the 38 `zz_r2fix_test.go` test functions 37 moved byte-identically while 1 carries a located 5-line file-name adaptation (38 is that file's function count, not a denominator of all historical tests); two further branch-side test differences (a version assertion and the NUL fixture) are retained in the merge result. No production logic changed in this merge commit.

### Fixed
- **Role guards: close the Oracle R19 adversarial audit — 4 Must-Fix plus 3 Should-Fix items and 1 observation in the eval operand decoding, the static-eval binding replication, the tilde expansion classification, the pure-assignment stdin-target environment, the data-only heredoc payload screen, the eval recursion budget and the adjacent double-glob byte contract (`role_guards.go`, `shell_ast.go`, `hc_prepare.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`).**
  - **Bash's builtin eval consumes its own leading `--` option terminator before re-parsing (R19-Oracle-M1 — same-input opposite verdict).** `scanShellBuiltinWrites` joined `args[1:]` verbatim: for `eval -- 'IFS=:'` the inner parser received a `--`-led command text whose assignment spelling degrades into an ordinary argument of a `--` command head, never a `*syntax.Assign` — the write scan counted 0 IFS writes while the plain spelling counted 1, and the same unquoted `$HOME` graded as one operand slot under the runtime-polluted IFS. The shared `evalStaticOperandParts` helper strips the terminator before the operands are joined, so `eval -- 'IFS=:'` and `eval 'IFS=:'` count the IFS write identically; the two argv-layer re-parse joins (the stdin target scan and the peeled eval re-entry) strip it the same way.
  - **A static eval's assignment writes replicate into the collector's bindings exactly like direct writes (R19-Oracle-M2 — wrong effective PATH at the substitution success proof).** `preScanVarMutations` counts writes, but a single write count is SSA bookkeeping, not a bind: `eval 'PATH=/custom/dir'` never reached `staticVars["PATH"]`, so `canResolveMktempInPath` fell back to the caller's ORIGINAL PATH and `"$(mktemp -d)"` earned the absolute-dir grant under a PATH the running shell no longer has. The new `bindEvalCallWrites` hook (fired from `walkStmt`'s CallExpr case) decodes the static operands through the same `evalStaticOperandParts`/`stripBuiltinWrapperWords` machinery and re-parses the inner text under the collector's own dialect: pure top-level assignments replicate through `staticAssignVal`+`noteStaticAssign` — literally the path a direct assignment statement takes — nested static evals recurse, and every other statement's written names are conservatively tainted because their execution is conditional. `eval 'PATH=/usr/local/sbin'` now revokes the mktemp grants exactly like `PATH=/usr/local/sbin`, and `eval 'PATH=/usr/bin:/bin'` keeps them exactly like its direct spelling.
  - **The tilde provenance marker requires a REAL tilde expansion shape, not just a leading `~` byte (R19-Oracle-M3 — encoding/decoding semantic inversion).** Bash expands `~` only when the tilde prefix up to the first UNQUOTED `/` carries no quoted character (and resolves as empty or a login name): `~\/notes` (backslash-quoted slash) and `~` followed by another word part are verbatim cwd-relative literals the shell never expands, yet the encoder keyed on `HasPrefix(rawLit, "~")` alone and minted the unforgeable HOME marker for both — the consuming shell receives `~/notes` as a relative path while the guard restored the service HOME into it. The new `tildeLitShape` classifier scans the prefix bytes exactly like bash: quoted-prefix spellings encode as plain STATIC literals (no glob or tilde marker at all — nothing expands), lone `~`/`~/` prefixes mint the HOME provenance, and login-name shapes (`~user`, `~+`/`~-`) stay dynamic with a glob marker but never the HOME marker. `~/"notes"` still resolves `/home/r19/notes` while `~\/notes` resolves to the cwd-relative `/opt/r19/~/notes`.
  - **Pure-assignment segments propagate their environment into later stdin consumers (R19-Oracle-M4 — rewrite authorized under the wrong environment).** `HOME=/home/r19; bash` skips the pure assignment as a non-consumer, but the assignment re-binds the exported variable in the RUNNING shell: the later `bash` inherits the new HOME exactly like the env-prefix spelling `HOME=/home/r19 bash`. Both the nested `collectSegs` and the flat `scriptAllowsLocalShellStdinPrepareDialect` scan now accumulate a skipped pure-assignment segment's ops (`pureAssignEnv`) into the inherited env in statement order, so the consumer target's `taintedSentinelNames()["HOME"]` agrees across the two spellings; neutral assignments (`X=1; bash`) touch no sentinel and keep their rewrite.
  - **A data-only heredoc consumer never faces the dynamic payload screen (R19-Oracle-S1 — false positive).** `screenSegmentHeredocsDepth` checked `hb.dynamic || isDynamicArg(hb.text)` BEFORE establishing that any program consumer exists, so `cat <<EOF` with a plain `$HOME` parameter expansion in the body was refused as `dynamic code payload` even though cat reads bytes, not programs — the parent behaviour returned without judging payload dynamics in exactly this case (substitutions inside the body are already emitted as nested segments by the collector itself). The screen now projects `stdinProgramNonDataTargets` first and skips both the dynamic check and the per-target program screen when no non-data program target exists; a shell reading its program from the stream keeps the refusal.
  - **The static-eval recursion shares the peel depth budget (R19-Oracle-S2 — unbounded pre-verdict parsing work).** `scanShellBuiltinWrites` recursed into nested static evals with no depth metering, so a self-nesting operand text could outparse the budget the outer guard peels under before any verdict. `preScanVarMutationsDepth`/`scanShellBuiltinWritesDepth` thread one `peelChainMaxDepth` step per nested re-parse and fall back to the conservative sentinel accounting at the budget boundary — a nesting of `peelChainMaxDepth+1` levels leaves the leaf write unparsed (write count 0) while a 3-level nesting replays exactly (write count 1).
  - **The test file compiles verbatim on pre-fix trees, and the FAIL-first records are honest (R19-Oracle-S3).** `zz_r2fix_test.go` no longer references the HEAD-only `cmdSubPlaceholderTilde` constant — the R15 exactly-two-glob assertion and every R19 probe use the literal `"\x00__ctx_tilde__\x00"` bytes — so the complete file compiles unchanged on both `8c9447a` and `496b81f` (verified with `go vet` rc=0 in isolated clones). The R18 FAIL-first claim below is now backed by a verbatim full-file overlay re-verified in this closure: exactly 12 discriminating rows FAIL and 11 control rows PASS on `8c9447a` (`/tmp` logs removed after transcription — no dangling paths are referenced in this record).
  - **The tilde brace encoding carries the adjacent double-glob pair in its RAW bytes (R19-Oracle observation).** The R18 encoder wrote the glob marker BEFORE the tilde provenance marker, so `~/{red,blue}` ended in `glob,tilde,glob` in the raw stream while `writeBraceExpansionMarkers`' stripping check minted the adjacency invariant only in the normalized form. The encoder now emits tilde provenance first and the glob marker second: the raw encoding ends in a glob byte group, so the appended brace marker forms the consecutive `__ctx_glob____ctx_glob__` pair at the word tail in the RAW byte stream the consumers actually key on — verified end-to-end on `~/{red,blue}`.
  - **Regression coverage.** `zz_r2fix_test.go` gains `TestGuard_V622_OracleR19Probes` pinning 12 subtests — 9 discriminating rows (the `eval --` IFS parity, both eval/direct PATH grant pairs, the quoted-slash verbatim tilde resolution, the login-name dynamic tilde, both pure-assignment HOME propagation shapes, the data-only heredoc payload exemption, the beyond-budget leaf cut-off and the raw adjacent-pair byte contract) plus 3 positive/negative control rows (the with-mktemp PATH pair, the neutral `X=1` rewrite and the program-consumer payload refusal). All 9 discriminating rows verified FAIL-first on the pre-fix tree `496b81f` in an isolated `/tmp` clone overlaid with only the test file (production code untouched, rc=1); the control rows pass on BOTH trees. Every new small function measures strictly `< 35` cognitive under `cccc --no-config --no-cache` (`scanShellBuiltinWritesDepth` 10, `evalStaticOperandParts` 8, `stripBuiltinWrapperWords` 29, `writtenNamesInNode` 1 (closure 16), `bindEvalCallWrites` 5, `bindEvalTextWrites` 4, `bindEvalStmtWrites` 25, `tildeLitShape` 11, `unquotedTildeTrigger` 2, `litHasUnquotedGlobOrTildePart` 14, `stdinProgramNonDataTargets` 3, `sshHeredocVerdict` 3, `pureAssignEnv` 3, `evalJoinOperands` 2, `accumulatePureAssignEnv` 1, `collectStdinSegTargets` 3), and no historical function grew beyond its `496b81f` measurement — verified by a full before/after `cccc` diff of all three files (`preScanVarMutations` closure 259, `wordToArgWithSubst` 57, `emitCall` 86, `walkStmt` 116, `stdinProgramTargetsDialect` 22, `stdinIndirectionTargets` 19, `peelResolvedSegDepth` 289, `scriptAllowsLocalShellStdinPrepareDialect` 25 — all unchanged; `screenSegmentHeredocsDepth` 15→11 only dropped, and the R18 `scanShellBuiltinWrites` was renamed into the depth-metered `scanShellBuiltinWritesDepth` (10) with its operand decoding shared through `evalStaticOperandParts`).
- **Role guards: close the Oracle R18 adversarial audit — 5 Must-Fix plus 6 Should-Fix items in the PATH lookup semantics, the multi-consumer stdin screens, the eval write propagation, the tilde provenance model, the TMPDIR resolution review, the `-c` slot state machine, the nested stdin classification and the release record (`role_guards.go`, `shell_ast.go`, `hc_prepare.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`, `readonly_allowlist_alfix7_test.go`).**
  - **PATH lookups reproduce the kernel's per-component resolution instead of lexically cleaning `..` away (R18-Oracle-M1 — wrong-object admission).** `pathHasExecutable` stat'd `filepath.Join(dir, name)`, whose lexical cleanup turns `/usr/bin/true/..` — a spelling the kernel rejects with ENOTDIR because `/usr/bin/true` is a regular file — into `/usr/bin`, so a PATH segment that can never be traversed yielded the executable `mktemp` and armed the substitution success grants. The lookup now requires the directory entry to stat as a real directory first and concatenates `dir` + `/` + `name` VERBATIM (no cleaning), so the kernel performs its own per-component resolution and the ENOTDIR spelling reports false exactly like the runtime.
  - **Local heredoc / here-string payloads are screened against EVERY program target, order-independently (R18-Oracle-M2 — lost consumer across input channels).** A mutually exclusive branch pair (`if …; then dash; else bash; fi`) sharing one fd0 lists `[dash, bash]` as its stdin targets, but `screenSegmentHeredocsDepth`/`screenOneSegmentDepth` projected only the FIRST non-data target — the dash target screened the brace-expanding `printf tag{red,blue}` payload as a POSIX literal while the API stdin channel judged the SAME target list per consumer, and swapping the branch order flipped the verdict. Both screens now iterate `stdinProgramTargetsWithEnv`'s full list through the extracted `screenHereStringTargetsDepth`/`screenHeredocBodyTargetsDepth` helpers: every non-data consumer faces `screenStdinProgram` under its own env ops and dialect, and any single rejection rejects the payload, so `[dash, bash]` and `[bash, dash]` agree exactly with each other and with the API stdin screen.
  - **A static eval's variable writes ride back into the enclosing collector's write scan (R18-Oracle-M3 — same-process re-parse state loss).** `eval` re-parses its arguments in the SAME shell process, yet the pre-scan only conservatively tainted PWD/OLDPWD and dropped every other write: `eval 'IFS=:'` polluted the runtime IFS while `isSingleWordOptionValue` kept grading a later unquoted `$HOME` under the default splitting rules. The new `scanShellBuiltinWrites` joins static eval operands exactly like eval does, re-parses them under the script's own dialect and feeds them through `preScanVarMutations` itself (nested static evals recurse); every inner write count ADDS onto the outer scan. A dynamic operand, an unparseable operand or an all-tainted inner scan conservatively counts every sentinel variable as written — so `eval "$X"; echo $HOME` degrades the unquoted HOME exactly like an unvettable direct write, while the double-quoted spelling is unaffected either way.
  - **Tilde restoration demands the unforgeable NUL-provenanced marker (R18-Oracle-M4 — provenance forged by printable bytes) while the word-count proof survives a tainted HOME independently (R18-Oracle-S5).** The printable `cmdSubPlaceholderGlob` can arrive VERBATIM inside a single-quoted literal (`'~__ctx_glob__/notes'`), and the restoration keyed on it alone restored the service process HOME into bytes the consumer receives unexpanded. The word encoder now mints `cmdSubPlaceholderTilde` (`\x00__ctx_tilde__\x00` — impossible to produce from shell text or argv) ONLY for a real unquoted word-start tilde under a known HOME, `tildeExpansionSpelling` requires it, and any glob-placeholder byte without it (`globTildePathUnproven`) fails the path operand closed outright. Because the second-glob-marker revocation is gone, a tainted HOME's `~/banner` keeps exactly one glob marker: `resolveNodeRunnerPathOpt` still refuses the concrete path (R17-M4-b preserved) while `isSingleWordOptionValue`/`printfFmtWriteSlots` correctly report ONE word (`printf ~/banner` stays dyn=false) — bash's tilde expansion never field-splits, so losing the concrete-path proof must not revoke the word-count proof.
  - **The TMPDIR pseudo-root review is component-exact and re-checked after symlink resolution (R18-Oracle-M5/S3 — wrong object both ways).** `HasPrefix(td, "/dev")` misrejected ordinary development directories sharing the `/dev` prefix (`/development/cache`) — the comparison now runs component-exact on `filepath.Clean` output: `/proc`/`/sys` outright, `/dev` entries except `/dev/shm` (and children). Independently, the lexical spelling `/dev/shm/..` inherited the tmpfs exception while `Stat`/`Access` acted on the RESOLVED object `/dev` (and `/dev/shm/../mqueue` resolves to the mqueue pseudo filesystem, never a tmpfs) — `inheritedTmpdirValid` now additionally requires `filepath.EvalSymlinks` to succeed AND the resolved path to pass the same component-exact review before the `tmpdirChildCreatable` Stat/perm/access tail.
  - **The `-c` slot scanner keeps a pending `-c` across a later independent cluster (R18-Oracle-S1 — prepare/guard slot desync).** `scanShellCClusterWord`'s `newSawC` defaulted to false, so `bash -c -e 'echo ok'` dropped the pending state at the `-e` word and `locateShellCSlot` reported ok=false — production prepare then skipped a rewrite the guard models correctly. The scanner now carries the incoming `sawC` forward exactly like `unwrapShellArgv`'s caller-side tracking, locating slot 3 for the probe argv.
  - **Nested pure-assignment segments consume no stdin and never mint a data consumer (R18-Oracle-S2 — flat/nested classification divergence).** The flat prepare scan skips segments whose peeled head is empty (`X=1`), but the nested `collectSegs` marked every no-program-target segment as a data consumer, so `bash -c 'X=1; bash'` refused the stdin rewrite the flat `X=1; bash` permits. `stdinTargetsWithDataFallback` now distinguishes the two shapes through the same `peelWrappersWithIndex` emptiness check: pure assignments vanish from the summary exactly like the flat scan while real data consumers (`cat`, `echo`) keep their `data: true` marker (R17-M6 preserved).
  - **The dash dialect rides into command-substitution collectors (R18-Oracle-S4 — cross-input-channel dialect loss).** `newSub` never copied `posixNoBraces`, so `echo $(printf tag{red,blue})` under dash encoded the inner format word with bash brace markers and the zero-argument printf review fired `env dynamic printf` — even though the substitution re-parses in the SAME dash process that performs no brace expansion. The field is inherited now, keeping the inner word the static literal dash really passes; the bash payload keeps its brace encoding.
  - **Release-record correction (R18-Oracle-S6).** The R17 entry below miscounted its own regression table: `TestGuard_V622_OracleR17Probes` carries 12 discriminating rows (M1/M2/M3/M4/M5/M6/S1/S2 = 2/2/1/2/2/1/1/1) plus 9 positive/negative control rows — not 10/11 (`R17-M5-pos` cannot count as a both-trees control: the pre-fix tree mislocates the terminator slot). The corrected counts are recorded in the R17 entry.
  - **Regression coverage.** `zz_r2fix_test.go` gains `TestGuard_V622_OracleR18Probes` pinning 23 subtests — 12 discriminating rows (the `/usr/bin/true/..` ENOTDIR lookup, both heredoc and here-string branch-order pairs, the eval/direct IFS parity plus the dynamic-eval conservative pollution, the forged-marker refusal, the `/dev/shm/..` + `/dev/shm/../mqueue` spellings with the direct `/dev/mqueue`, the `-e` cluster slot, the nested `X=1; bash` parity, the `/development/cache` false pseudo-root, the dash substitution printf shape and the tainted-HOME printf word-count proof) plus 11 positive/negative control rows. All 12 discriminating rows verified FAIL-first on the pre-fix tree `8c9447a` in an isolated `/tmp` snapshot overlaid with only the test file (production code untouched, rc=1); the control rows pass on BOTH trees. `readonly_allowlist_alfix7_test.go`'s fuzz harness strips NUL bytes when shaping placeholder text into symlink fixture names (the encoding evidence is never executable text) and the R15 exactly-two-glob assertion judges the encoding after stripping the provenance marker. (R19-Oracle-S3 clarification: the original FAIL-first overlay could not have been the complete HEAD test file verbatim — it referenced the HEAD-only `cmdSubPlaceholderTilde` constant — so the exact transplant delta of the original run is unrecoverable. The verification is REPRODUCED in the R19 closure with the constant reference replaced by literal bytes: the complete file then compiles verbatim on `8c9447a` and exactly 12 discriminating rows FAIL with 11 control rows PASS.) `stdinSingleShellTargetDialect` drops to 5 cognitive (`cccc --no-config --no-cache`, eliminating the 6→7 growth), `parseSubagentShellASTEnv` stays 47, `resolveNodeRunnerPathOpt` drops 27→19, `inheritedTmpdirValid` 7→6, every new small function measures strictly `< 35` (`scanShellBuiltinWrites` 19, `restoreTildePathOpt` 7, `tmpdirPseudoCleanedRoot` 7, `tmpdirChildCreatable` 3, `statExecutableFile` 1, `pathIsRealDirectory` 1, `tildeHomeProven` 1, `globTildePathUnproven` 1, `screenHereStringTargetsDepth` 5, `screenHeredocBodyTargetsDepth` 5, `stdinTargetsWithDataFallback` 2, `stdinShellTargetRewriteable` 1), `pathHasExecutable` drops 4→2, and no historical function grew beyond its `8c9447a` measurement (`peelResolvedSegDepth` 289, `awkProgramFrags` 68, `wordToArgWithSubst` 57, `locateShellCSlot` 56, `prepareCtxRunShellTargets` 31, `scanShellCClusterWord` 21, the `preScanVarMutations` closure 259 — all unchanged).
- **Role guards: close the Oracle R17 adversarial audit — 6 Must-Fix plus 2 Should-Fix items in the mktemp exec premise, the bash-dialect stdin continuation, the IFS/HOME single-word proofs, the production `-c` slot alignment, the nested stdin classification, the brace-shape encoding and the tmpfs TMPDIR compatibility (`role_guards.go`, `shell_ast.go`, `hc_prepare.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`).**
  - **The mktemp grants additionally require the effective PATH to resolve an executable `mktemp` (R17-Oracle-M1 — exec failure mistaken for success).** A writable valid TMPDIR never proved the child RUNS at all: a caller whose effective PATH carries only directories without `mktemp` (verified `/usr/local/sbin` is root-owned 0755 without it) makes the shell report "command not found", the substitution collapses to EMPTY stdout inside the double quotes, yet `dqSubstPlaceholder` still minted the absolute-dir class and `staticAssignVal` the non-empty `/tmp/__ctx_mktemp_dir__` bind. Both producers now share `mktempGrantOK` — untainted TMPDIR + valid inherited TMPDIR + `canResolveMktempInPath` — which walks the collector's effective PATH (static `PATH=` bind first, then the caller's replayed pathEnv, then the process PATH) and requires an absolute directory containing an executable non-directory `mktemp`; a tainted PATH is unvettable.
  - **Local bash heredoc / here-string stdin re-parses keep the bash dialect (R17-Oracle-M2 — argv-cardinality check lost across input channels).** `stdinProgramHeadWithEnv` projected only `lang,isProg,envAs` and dropped `stdinTarget.bash`, so a heredoc or here-string into a local `bash` re-parsed the SAME program text under the dash POSIX default: `printf tag{red,blue}` became a zero-argument literal-format ALLOW while the byte-identical `bash -c` payload and the API stdin channel blocked with `env dynamic printf`. The projection now carries the dialect flag, `screenStdinProgram` threads it into `guardShellSubagentDepthDialect` for the subagent screen, and the heredoc/here-string/pipe consumers pass it along — the dash consumers keep their POSIX literal ALLOW.
  - **A polluted IFS revokes the unquoted-`$HOME` single-word proof (R17-Oracle-M3 — field-splitting desync).** The project itself allows `:`/`,` as IFS separators, and the write pre-scan already taints a script-level IFS write — yet the unquoted grant never consulted it: `HOME=/home/red:blue` with `IFS=:` splits into TWO argv words at runtime while `isSingleWordOptionValue` judged the literal text one operand slot. The unquoted encoder now degrades whenever `ifsClean()` is false (script-tainted IFS, a non-default exported process IFS, or a static bind carrying non-default separators) exactly like the whitespace/glob downgrade — `cmdSubPlaceholderAbsDq + cmdSubPlaceholder + cmdSubPlaceholderGlob` — while the double-quoted spelling is unaffected (double quotes suppress field splitting entirely).
  - **`~/...` restoration demands both the real tilde-expansion marker and an untainted expansion environment (R17-Oracle-M4 — wrong-object admission).** Criterion A: `resolveNodeRunnerPathOpt` keyed the HOME restoration on a bare leading `~` byte alone, so a direct argv or single-quoted word `~/notes` (which reaches the consumer VERBATIM) silently resolved to the service HOME — the tilde branch now fires only for `tildeExpansionSpelling` (leading `~` plus the shell's glob marker that only an unquoted word-start tilde can mint); a bare `~/notes` resolves cwd-relative or fails closed. Criterion B: a collector whose HOME is tainted now mints a SECOND glob marker after a word-start tilde (`tildeGrantRevoked`), revoking the pure-tilde single-path exemption (exactly-one-marker count) so the consuming shell's unknowable HOME can never be replaced by the service process's.
  - **`locateShellCSlot` skips the `--`/`-` terminators after a pending `-c` exactly like `unwrapShellArgv` (R17-Oracle-M5 — production prepare rewrote a slot the guard never screened).** The slot walker read the terminator word itself as the script operand: for `["bash","-c","--","echo ok"]` it returned `(2,"--")` while the unwrap decoded `"echo ok"`, so production prepare replaced one argv word and the guard re-screened another. The walker now mirrors the unwrap word for word — bare `--`/`-` (and the dash-only `+`) end option parsing with the NEXT word as the command string, a second `c` in a later cluster is a plain flag, dynamic option-NAME operands after a pending `-c` fail closed, and glued cluster tails (`-ce`/`-co`) keep their unrewritable verdict; `argv[idx] == rawScript == unwrapShellArgv(argv).text` holds for every clean slot.
  - **Nested `-c` stdin classification no longer drops plain data consumers (R17-Oracle-M6 — mixed stream rewritten as pure shell program).** `collectSegs` only appended recursive program targets, so a plain `cat` inside `bash -c "cat; bash"` vanished from the summary and `stdinSingleShellTargetDialect` saw only the inner bash — the outer `scriptAllowsLocalShellStdinPrepareDialect` then permitted rewriting a stream that is partly DATA into a `<selfExe> --hc-gate` program. Segments that consume the shared stream without yielding a program target now surface as `stdinTarget{data: true}`, which refuses the rewrite (matching the flat `cat; bash`) while the pure-program screens skip them exactly like before.
  - **A multi-glob path with quoted literal braces is never a brace expansion (R17-Oracle-S1 — false positive).** `docs/*/'{v1}'/*.txt` legitimately carries TWO glob markers (one per unquoted `*` Lit) separated by the static infix `/{v1}/*.txt`, yet `isSafeDynamicDataOperand` keyed the brace refusal on the mere two-marker count. `writeBraceExpansionMarkers` now guarantees CONSECUTIVE ADJACENT markers at the word tail for real brace expansions (`{docs/a,-blue}`, `~/{red,blue}` both end in `__ctx_glob____ctx_glob__`), and the consumer keys on the adjacent pair — the separated two-glob path stays a safe data operand while every real brace word keeps failing closed.
  - **`/dev/shm` is a valid tmpfs TMPDIR again (R17-Oracle-S2 — false positive).** The `/dev/…` prefix exclusion was a blanket refusal, but `/dev/shm` is a real tmpfs (mode 1777) the repository itself lists as an allowed scratch root — `inheritedTmpdirValid` now excludes only `/proc`/`/sys` outright plus `/dev` entries other than `/dev/shm` (and its children), so `TMPDIR=/dev/shm` keeps the mktemp success proof while `/proc`, `/sys` and `/dev/null` stay rejected.
  - **Regression coverage.** `zz_r2fix_test.go` gains `TestGuard_V622_OracleR17Probes` pinning 21 subtests — 12 discriminating rows (M1/M2/M3/M4/M5/M6/S1/S2 = 2/2/1/2/2/1/1/1: the mktemp-less-PATH grant revocations, the bash heredoc/here-string dialect continuations, the IFS=: unquoted degradation, the unmarked-literal and tainted-HOME tilde refusals, the `--` slot alignment, the nested cat+bash refusal and the separated-glob safe-operand proof) plus 9 positive/negative control rows. (R18-Oracle-S6 correction: this entry previously miscounted the table as 10 discriminating / 11 control rows — `R17-M5-pos` cannot count as a both-trees control because its terminator spellings exercise the very slot mislocation the fix removes, so it belongs to the discriminating side, yielding 12/9.) All discriminating rows verified FAIL-first on the pre-fix tree `841473c` in an isolated `/tmp` snapshot overlaid with only the test file (production code untouched, rc=1); the control rows pass on BOTH trees. `parseSubagentShellASTEnv` drops to 47 cognitive (`cccc --no-config --no-cache`, baseline 57 on `d3efa30`, 58 on `841473c`), `awkProgramFrags` stays 68 (≤ 73), every new small function measures strictly `< 35` (`mktempGrantOK` 2, `canResolveMktempInPath` 7, `pathHasExecutable` 4, `ifsClean` 7, `unquotedHomePlaceholder` 4, `tmpdirPseudoRoot` 5, `tildeGrantRevoked` 1, `writeUnquotedGlobTildeLit` 2, `validateParsedShellFile` 3, `walkSecondaryShellFile` 2, `firstProgramTarget` 3, `tildeExpansionSpelling` 1, `scanShellCClusterWord` 21), and no historical function grew beyond its `841473c` measurement (`unquotedParamPlaceholder` 14→5, `inheritedTmpdirValid` 10→7, `resolveNodeRunnerPathOpt` 27→27, `screenStdinProgram` 6→6, `stdinProgramHeadWithEnv` 1→1, `stdinIndirectionTargets` 19→19, `locateShellCSlot` 59→56, `prepareCtxRunShellTargets` 31→31, `wordToArgWithSubst` 57→57).
- **Role guards: close the Oracle R16 adversarial audit — 4 Must-Fix plus 3 Should-Fix items in the mktemp/HOME grant premises, the bash-dialect continuation, the brace-glob data-operand review, the dash write pre-scan and the release record (`role_guards.go`, `shell_ast.go`, `hc_prepare.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`).**
  - **The `mktemp -d` directory proof rejects pseudo filesystems and the variable-assignment binding path applies it too (R16-Oracle-M1 — empty-expansion positional-layout desync).** `os.Stat` + `IsDir()` never proved a mktemp child can create a subdirectory: `/proc` is a real directory (mode 0555) on a pseudo filesystem, so `TMPDIR=/proc` inherited the non-empty absolute-dir grant even though GNU mktemp always fails there with EMPTY stdout. `inheritedTmpdirValid` now additionally refuses the `/proc`/`/sys`/`/dev` pseudo-filesystem prefixes and requires a write-permission bit plus a real `unix.Access(td, W_OK|X_OK)` probe. Independently, the `staticAssignVal` assignment-binding producer (`d=$(mktemp -d)`) never applied even the original directory check, so a variable bound under `TMPDIR=/dev/null` still minted the non-empty `/tmp/__ctx_mktemp_dir__` static text and fed it into the same downstream positional-layout proofs — it now carries the identical `!isTainted("TMPDIR") && inheritedTmpdirValid()` gate as the direct double-quoted substitution path, so both producers agree for `TMPDIR=/dev/null` and `TMPDIR=/proc`.
  - **eval/trap/stdin re-parses of a bash-family shell keep the bash dialect instead of falling back to dash (R16-Oracle-M2 — argv-cardinality check lost across input channels).** The explicit `sh -c` edge already resolved the real dialect, but the dialect never continued with the running shell: `eval` operands and `trap` actions re-parsed in the SAME interpreter yet re-entered the guard with the dash POSIX default, and the stdin-program scan (`stdinShellTargetsScan`) plus the hc prepare rewrite (`collectHcGateEdits`/`guardPreparedShellEnv` in `prepareCtxRunShellTargets`) always chose non-bash — so `bash` reading `printf tag{red,blue}` from stdin kept the zero-argument literal-format exemption ALLOW while the byte-identical `bash -c` payload blocked with `env dynamic printf`. `shellSegment` now carries `bashDialect` from the parse, the eval/trap branches re-enter through the new `guardShellSubagentDepthDialect`, `stdinTarget` marks a bash-family consuming shell (computed exactly like the `-c` path through `hcShellDialectBash`), the pure stdin screens dispatch on it, and the prepare pipeline refuses the rewrite for a MIXED bash/dash consumer set while threading the shared dialect into the edit walk and the prov-aware guard pass. Top-level `/bin/sh` (dash) keeps `posixNoBraces` everywhere: the dash stdin/eval/trap spellings stay ALLOW.
  - **An unquoted `$HOME` carrying whitespace or glob bytes is never a single static operand (R16-Oracle-M3 — positional-layout desync through field splitting).** A legal absolute HOME is not safe as unquoted static text: `HOME="/home/Team Blue"` field-splits into TWO argv words at runtime and `HOME="/home/te*am"` pathname-expands, but the unquoted encoder emitted the inherited literal path and `isSingleWordOptionValue` judged it one operand slot — single-value options and the zero-argument printf review could then vet two real arguments as one. The double-quoted encoding is unchanged: `"$HOME"` suppresses field splitting entirely and keeps the static literal.

The encoders now top up every REAL brace expansion word to at least TWO glob placeholders (`writeBraceExpansionMarkers` — `~/{red,blue}` keeps exactly two, tilde + brace, and `{docs/a,-blue}` reaches two), so `isSafeDynamicDataOperand` keys the refusal on the two-marker brace shape: real brace words still fail closed while the single-marker quoted-literal glob keeps its data-operand grant.

- **Role guards: close the Oracle R15 adversarial audit — 2 Must-Fix plus 4 Should-Fix items in the brace-expansion encoding consumers and the non-empty absolute-path grant premises (`role_guards.go`, `shell_ast.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`).**
  - **Brace-expansion words can no longer pass the merged path-prefix/slash review of `isSafeDynamicDataOperand` (R15-Oracle-M1 — cross-branch option/data authorization hole).** Both word encoders keep the brace list's SOURCE text (`{docs/a,-blue}__ctx_glob__`), but a leading `{` byte is not a path property of ANY expansion result and a `/` carried by one branch (`docs/a`) says nothing about its siblings (`-blue`, which expands to an OPTION spelling) — yet `isSafeDynamicDataOperand` judged the whole merged encoding: the first byte `{` is not in its reject table and any branch's `/` passed the entire word, so `find` skipped it as a start path, `awkDynWordStep` reused the same proof in the option zone and `tar` skipped it as a member path.   - **The `"$(mktemp -d)"` absolute-dir grant requires an actually valid inherited TMPDIR, and `"$HOME"` requires an actually inherited non-empty absolute HOME (R15-Oracle-M2 — empty-expansion positional-layout desync).** A spelling match plus untainted TMPDIR never proved the mktemp child SUCCEEDED: with `TMPDIR=/dev/null` (a regular file) GNU mktemp fails, writes its diagnostic to stderr and stdout inside the double quotes is EMPTY — the new `inheritedTmpdirValid` helper requires an unset TMPDIR (the `/tmp` default) or an absolute NUL-free path that stats as a directory before `dqSubstPlaceholder` may emit `cmdSubPlaceholderAbsDq`. `childEnv` copies existing keys verbatim and never re-populates HOME: an inherited `HOME=""` makes `"$HOME"` expand EMPTY, but `safeHomeDir` fell back to `/root` and both encoders returned it as STATIC text — the new `inheritedHomeDir` helper grants the static encoding only for a non-empty absolute inherited value, otherwise the expansion degrades to the ordinary (empty-able) placeholders, so `dynGluedTailNonEmpty` and `dynWordCanSpellDashDash` correctly model the collapse. `safeHomeDir` is retained for NON-shell-variable consumers (configuration search paths) where a fallback directory is the documented semantics.
  - **Quoted literal braces keep the pure-tilde single-path exemption, and the dash top-level dialect performs no brace expansion (R15-Oracle-S1 — two distinct compatibility regressions).** Bash expands only the tilde in `~/'{red,blue}'` (the braces are single-quoted LITERAL text), but the unconditional `{`-byte exclusion in `isSingleWordOptionValue` could not distinguish it from the expanding `~/{red,blue}`; the encoders now append the brace hit's glob placeholder UNCONDITIONALLY, so `~/{red,blue}` carries TWO placeholders (tilde + brace) and is denied by the exact-one-placeholder count while the quoted spelling carries exactly one with `{` only AFTER the marker — the tilde branch now excludes `{` only in the text BEFORE the glob placeholder. Separately, `/bin/sh` is dash on the deployment topology and performs NO brace expansion at all: the `subCollector` gained a `posixNoBraces` field (set whenever `useBashVariant` is false) that skips the bash brace scan for the primary-dialect encoding, so a top-level `printf tag{red,blue}` is the static literal format word dash actually produces and stays ALLOW, while the explicit `bash -c 'printf tag{red,blue}'` keeps blocking with `env dynamic printf` (bash really multiplies the word).

  - **Nested separator-less literal braces are rejected by a single-pass budget scan with zero `SplitBraces` replay (R15-Oracle-S3 — self-inflicted quadratic copy cost).** `wordSplitBraceExpHit`'s recursive replay copied the already-restored inner Parts at every nesting level for words that never expand (1+3+5+... items: 16 copied items at depth 4, 64 at depth 8 — quadratic in depth, synchronously before the guard verdict). The helper is deleted: `wordBraceExpHit` now single-pass scans the literal parts first — a valid expansion must carry a closing `}` AND a separator (`,` or `..`), so words like `{{{{a}}}}` return false in O(N) with ZERO WordPart copies; pathologically deep nesting WITH separators (> 8 opening braces) conservatively reports a hit without replaying anything, and only shallow separator-carrying words pay one `SplitBraces` copy whose result is checked for a top-level `*syntax.BraceExp` node.

- **Role guards: close the Oracle R14 adversarial audit — 3 Must-Fix plus 4 Should-Fix items in the word-cardinality / write-set / placeholder-grant boundary (`role_guards.go`, `shell_ast.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`).**
  - **Unquoted brace expansion can no longer encode as a static single word (R14-Oracle-M1 — printf `%n` write-target hole).** The locked `mvdan.cc/sh v3.14.1` parser keeps brace expansions inside `*syntax.Lit` — the `*syntax.BraceExp` node only ever materializes through `syntax.SplitBraces` — so `litHasUnquotedGlobOrTilde`'s `~ * ? []` scan never saw `tag{red,blue}`: both `wordToArg` encoders returned the word as STATIC text, and `printfFmtWriteSlots(s, nil)` concluded the zero-argument exemption applied even though bash expands one such word into MULTIPLE argv words (`match[0]` becomes the real format string honouring any `%n`, `match[1]` the first write-target argument). The new `wordBraceExpHit`/`wordSplitBraceExpHit` helpers replay `SplitBraces` on a throwaway copy of the word — including brace text SPANNING quoting boundaries (`{'%n',TAR_OPTIONS}`, which bash still expands into two words and which a per-literal scan could never catch) — and force the dynamic encoding with exactly one split-safe glob placeholder. The single-quoted spelling `'tag{red,blue}'` still encodes as the static literal `tag{red,blue}`; malformed expansions (`{a`, `{1..2..3..4}`) keep their literal text exactly like bash.
  - **An unbound `"$OLDPWD"` no longer receives the never-empty absolute-dir placeholder class (R14-Oracle-M2 — positional-layout desync).** The child environment only copies existing keys: when the parent process never exported a non-empty `OLDPWD` (no prior `cd`), the expansion is the EMPTY string, but `dqParamPlaceholder` granted `cmdSubPlaceholderAbsDq` unconditionally — so `dynGluedTailNonEmpty` proved a glued tail non-empty and `dynWordCanSpellDashDash` ruled out the `--` spelling for a value that can collapse to nothing. The grant is now limited to `PWD` (which bash itself always re-populates to a non-empty absolute path); `"$OLDPWD"` falls back to the ordinary quoted placeholder unless the script statically bound it (which `resolveParamForSubst` already handles upstream), so both consumer predicates correctly treat it as empty-able.
  - **Derived `$$` operators are no longer mistaken for raw PID digits in the context-free encoder (R14-Oracle-M3 — AST write-set undercount).** `wordToArg` keyed the never-dash PID grant on the bare `Param.Value=="$" && Exp==nil` check, but the parser stores `Slice`/`Repl`/`Index`/`Length`/`Excl`/`Names` in SEPARATE fields: `"--${$:0:0}"` (a zero-length PID slice CAN expand empty, spelling the `--` terminator exactly) inherited the digit-prefixed encoding, so `preScanVarMutations` never vetted the standby write-target slot of `getopts "--${$:0:0}" first second` (`writes["second"]` stayed 0 — the collector encoder already excluded these operators through `simpleParamName`, so the two encoders disagreed). Both sites now use `simpleParamName`, matching the collector exactly: the sliced PID spells the terminator (`dynWordCanSpellDashDash` true, standby slot vetted) while the bare `$$` keeps its correct decimal-digit exclusion.
  - **Explicit-path printf is exempt from the AST builtin write scan too (R14-Oracle-S1).** `preScanVarMutations` stripped the directory prefix before dispatching on the basename, so `/usr/bin/printf "label=$LABEL %s" value` entered the `printf` builtin scan and all-tainted the parent script even though the external child process can never write shell variables — `cmd=/bin/true; /usr/bin/printf "label=$LABEL %s" value; "$cmd"` lost its static bind and blocked with `dynamic command head`. The scan now skips the builtin write walk whenever the head carries `/` (mirroring the argv layer's path exemption), keeping the combination script ALLOW.
  - **printf's zero-argument exemption keeps pure-tilde format words (R14-Oracle-S2 — false positive reversal).** `~/banner` expands to exactly ONE path word under any normal absolute HOME (tilde expansion is not pathname expansion), but the glob placeholder encoding is indistinguishable from a real wildcard word, so `printf ~/banner` blocked with `env dynamic printf`. The zero-argument guard now reuses `isSingleWordOptionValue` — the same single-runtime-word predicate plus its proven pure-tilde exemption — with the exemption tightened against brace/quote bytes (`~/{a,b}` expands into MULTIPLE words even without glob metacharacters). `printf ~/banner` stays ALLOW while `printf ~/banner*`, `printf lit*` and `printf tag{red,blue}` keep failing closed.
  - **awk's `--source=` explicit empty program fragment survives collection (R14-Oracle-S3 — data file misjudged as code).** `gawk --source= -- "$F"` supplies an empty program (semantically identical to `-e ""`), but the long-option helper returned a plain string and the caller dropped empty fragments, so `len(frags) == 0` misjudged the post-`--` input data file as the positional program text and reported `dynamic code payload`. `awkProgramLongStep` now returns a `[]string` fragment channel in parity with `awkClusterFragsEnd`, and the caller appends unconditionally — `--source=` and `-e ""` produce identical results (cognitive complexity of `awkProgramFrags` 71→68, ≤ 73).
  - **Regression coverage (R14-Oracle-S4).** `TestGuard_V622_OracleR13Probes` is corrected to exactly 3 block + 10 allow end-to-end rows (the missing 10th allow row is the `bash -c` combination-script counterpart of the external-printf exemption) plus the two R13 discriminating assertions that were never landed (`printfFmtSlots("%10(%Y)T%n") == [false,true]` positional=false proving the strftime conversion consumes exactly one non-write slot, and `readDynClusterHit("-Zptext"+dq) != ""` pinning unknown-letter fail-closed with a non-empty static tail). The new `TestGuard_V622_OracleR14Probes` pins 14 pure-function/parser rows (the brace SplitBraces contract, both `wordToArg` encoders on brace words plus the quote-spanning shape, the unbound-`$OLDPWD` empty-collapse predicates, the sliced-PID dashdash spelling in both encoders, the getopts standby write count, the `/usr/bin/printf` pre-scan exemption, the pure-tilde/glob/brace printf shapes and the `--source=`/`-e ""` parity) plus 4 block and 6 positive end-to-end guard rows. All discriminating R14 rows verified FAIL-first on the pre-fix tree `27eaccb` in an isolated `/tmp` snapshot overlaid with only the test file (production code untouched, rc=1 — including the missing R13 10th allow row), and the full table passes on the fixed tree. Positive rows split into two evidence classes: REGRESSION positives are inputs the pre-fix tree wrongly refused and the fix must release (the `bash -c` external-printf combination script misjudged on `27eaccb` — the static-format positive shape the tree all-tainted; the class belongs to the fix, not to both trees), while INVARIANT positives are control rows that pass on BOTH trees unchanged. (R15-Oracle-S4: this entry previously miscounted the end-to-end table as 3 block rows — it is 4 — and the R14 allow row duplicating R13's combination-script row verbatim has since been replaced by an independent positive, since two identical rows cannot count as two independent behavior evidences.)
