- **Role guards: close the Oracle R13 adversarial audit — 4 Must-Fix plus 4 Should-Fix items in the printf/read/getopts write-target boundary (`role_guards.go`, `shell_ast.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`).** *(Partially supersedes the R14 closure below: the zero-argument `printf lit*` positive and the `read -p"$PROMPT" answer` positive are both REVERSED — each is now refused, see R13-MF-1/R13-MF-4. The R14 claim that bash printf honours `q` as a length modifier was factually wrong — `'q'` never was one; `%q` is the shell-quoting CONVERSION, see R13-MF-2.)*
  - **A zero-argument dynamic/glob printf format word is no longer automatically safe (R13-MF-1 — zero-pipe local tar/git RCE).** The R14 exemption reasoned "no arguments means nothing can be written", but that only holds when the format word arrives as exactly ONE runtime argv word: an unquoted glob or word-splitting expansion produces MULTIPLE argv words, where `match[0]` becomes the real format string (bash honours any `%n` inside it — filenames can legitimately be named `lit%n`) and `match[1]` the first write-target argument. `printfFmtWriteSlots` now requires `isSingleWordSplitSafe` for the zero-argument exemption: `printf lit*` blocks with `env dynamic printf` (the R14 positive is reversed) while the split-safe single-word spelling `printf x"$FMT"` and the quoted literal `printf 'lit*'` stay ALLOW.
  - **`%q` is the shell-quoting conversion, never a length modifier (R13-MF-2 — printf write-slot confusion both ways).** Bash printf's `LENMODS` is `"hjlLtz"` — `'q'` was never in it: `%q`/`%Q` are conversion specifiers emitting a shell-quoted string. Treating `'q'` as a length modifier made `printf "%q%n" x TAR_OPTIONS` parse `%` as a malformed conversion and truncate the scan before the real `%n` write (a hole), while `printf "%qn" "$X"` invented a phantom `%n` write conversion (a false positive). The length-modifier case no longer includes `'q'`: `%q%n` scans as `[non-write, write]` (blocked through `env TAR_OPTIONS`) and `%qn` scans as a single non-write conversion (ALLOW).
  - **read's static option-cluster end-letter judgment is positional, not a byte-value coincidence (R13-MF-3 — AST write-scan undercount).** `preScanVarMutations` decided whether a value letter consumes the next argv word by comparing the cluster's LAST BYTE against the value letter itself — `read -eii sample` glues the `-i` initial-value text `i` after the option letter, so the trailing `i` coincidentally matched and `sample` was silently swallowed as the phantom consumed value, never counted as a write (`sample=/bin/true; read -eii sample <<< pig; "$sample"` kept its static bind through the runtime overwrite). The scan now uses the hit POSITION (`k == len(s)-1`): only a value letter sitting at the end of its cluster consumes the next word.
  - **A glued dynamic read prompt/initial tail must be provably non-empty (R13-MF-4 — positional-layout desync).** `read -p"$PROMPT" answer` with `PROMPT=''` collapses to `read -p answer` at runtime: bash consumes the NEXT argv word as the prompt text, so the positional write targets diverge from the static scan (`answer` is swallowed as the prompt and the write lands on `REPLY`/nothing). The new `dynGluedTailNonEmpty` helper strips every placeholder spelling from the glued tail: at least one static literal byte (`read -p"Prompt: $PROMPT" line`) or a guaranteed non-empty placeholder class (`cmdSubPlaceholderAbsDq`) must survive, otherwise `readDynClusterHit` returns `env dynamic read` in both the argv layer and the AST pre-scan. The separated spelling `read -p "$PROMPT" answer` stays ALLOW — the quoted word arrives as exactly one argv even when empty (measured in R14).
  - **printf/read/getopts AST pre-scan alignment (R13-SF-1).** `preScanVarMutations` now feeds dynamic-but-encoded printf words into the shared `printfOptionScan` instead of tainting the whole script on the `ok=false` conversion flag (the encoding carries the exact letters — only a truly empty conversion taints), delegates read's dynamic cluster words to the same `readDynClusterHit` the argv layer uses (the old inline walk was dead code — context-free `wordToArg` always reports `ok=false` for dynamic words, and it never applied the R13-MF-4 non-empty-tail rule), and mirrors `screenGetoptsEnvWrite` for the dynamic first operand: `envDynSlotHit` plus the `dynWordCanSpellDashDash` standby-slot vetting at `args[3]`. The combination scripts `cmd=/bin/true; printf '%s' "$DATA"; "$cmd"`, `cmd=/bin/true; read -p"Prompt: $PROMPT" answer <<< ok; "$cmd"` and `cmd=/bin/true; getopts ":$OPTS" opt; "$cmd"` stay ALLOW — their data arguments never blind the whole-script write scan.
  - **`dynWordCanSpellDashDash` exactness and the `$$` PID encoding (R13-SF-2).** Expansion can only grow or shrink the DYNAMIC part: a static remainder of three or more dashes (`---$X`) can never shrink back to exactly `--`, and absolute-path/PATH-self placeholders always yield `'/'`-leading text — both now return false immediately instead of vacuously matching the all-dash walk. `$$` (a decimal PID — never `--`, never `-`-leading) is digit-prefixed in all four encoding sites (`wordToArg` quoted/unquoted paths, `unquotedParamPlaceholder`, `dqParamPlaceholder`) so the placeholder strip no longer vacuously matches a bare `"$$"`: `getopts "$$" opt "$arg"` stays ALLOW while `H="--"; getopts "$H" opt TAR_OPTIONS` keeps vetting the standby write-target slot.
  - **Explicit-path printf execs the external binary, which cannot write shell variables (R13-SF-3).** `/usr/bin/printf` (or any `/`-carrying spelling) runs in a child process whose output can never mutate the parent shell — the `screenPrintfEnvWrite` write-target scan is skipped for path spellings, so `/usr/bin/printf "label=$LABEL %s" value` and `/usr/bin/printf 'label=%s' value` stay ALLOW while the bare builtin keeps its full scan.
  - **Regression coverage (R13-SF-4).** `zz_r2fix_test.go` gains `TestGuard_V622_OracleR13Probes` pinning 8 pure-function/parser unit rows (`printfFmtWriteSlots` zero-argument glob/quoted pairs, `printfFmtSlots` `%q%n`/`%qn` shapes, the `preScanVarMutations` `read -eii sample` write count, `readDynClusterHit` empty-able/static-prefix tails, `dynWordCanSpellDashDash` precision) plus 3 block and 10 positive end-to-end guard rows; inside `TestGuard_V622_ReviewerR4Probes` the `printf lit*` zero-argument positive moves to the block table, `read -p"$PROMPT" answer` moves to the block table (the static-prefix and separated positives stay), and the positional-designator probes are corrected to `%2\$s%n`/`%*2\$n` (inside bash double quotes an unescaped `$s`/`$n` expands empty — the probes were testing the wrong format string). - **Role guards: close the Reviewer R14 acceptance audit — 2 Must-Fix plus 2 Should-Fix boundary holes found in the R4/R12 closure (`role_guards.go`, `shell_ast.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`).**
  - **The glob-only printf format exemption is removed (R14-MF-1 — zero-pipe local tar/git RCE).** Linux filenames legitimately contain `%n`: when the current directory holds a file named `lit%n`, the unquoted glob in `printf lit* TAR_OPTIONS` expands the format word to exactly `%n`, storing the byte count (3) into `TAR_OPTIONS` outright — the R4 claim that "a filename can never spell a valid write target" confused the write TARGET (the aligned argument) with the format SHAPE (which a filename absolutely controls). `printfFmtWriteSlots` no longer has a glob-only safe branch: a dynamic format word — glob, variable or command substitution alike — with ANY trailing argument is unvettable (`env dynamic printf`), and only the zero-argument spelling (`printf lit*`) stays ALLOW because nothing can be written without arguments. The `printfGlobOnlyFmt` helper is deleted.
  - **printf length modifiers on `%n` are scanned, `%(...)T` consumes its argument under flags/width/precision, and `%*m$n` star-positional widths fail closed (R14-MF-2 — zero-pipe local tar/git RCE).** Bash printf honours `hh/h/l/ll/j/z/t/L/q` on EVERY conversion including `n` (measured: `printf "%ln" TAR_OPTIONS` writes 0, `printf "%hhn" TAR_OPTIONS` likewise, and the glob spelling writes 3), but the conversion parser compared the post-precision byte directly against `'n'`, so `printf "%ln" TAR_OPTIONS` / `printf "%hhn" TAR_OPTIONS` sailed through. `printfFmtConv` now skips the optional length modifier before judging the conversion letter, settles `%(...)T` strftime conversions that carry flags/width/precision in front (`%10(%Y)T` consumes exactly one argument slot), and the extracted `printfFmtMeasure`/`printfStarPositional` helpers report the `%*2$n` star-positional width shape as unvettable (fail closed). `printf "%10(%Y)T\n" -1` stays ALLOW.
  - **`readDynClusterHit` fails closed on unknown static option letters (R14-SF-1).** The dynamic-word cluster walk previously stepped over every byte that was not a value letter or `a`, contradicting `shell_ast.go`'s conservative `default` arm: only the known BOOLEAN letters `r/s/e` may precede the dynamic part now, everything else returns `env dynamic read` in parity with the static scanner.
  - **`preScanVarMutations` vets read's consumed separate value operand (R14-SF-2).** When a value letter (`d n N p t u i`) ends its cluster and the value arrives as the next argv, an UNQUOTED expansion (`read -p $X` with `X="p cmd"`) word-splits at runtime, shifting the positional layout so the REAL write targets diverge from the static operand scan — the operand is now vetted with `isDynamicArg && !isSingleWordSplitSafe` (tainting every static bind fail-closed) exactly like the argv layer's `envDynSlotHit`. A quoted expansion (`read -p "$PROMPT" x`) stays exactly one argv word at runtime even when empty (measured), so the layout can never shift and the slot is skipped like static text.
  - **Regression coverage.** `zz_r2fix_test.go` gains the `P-R14-1..4` block probes inside `TestGuard_V622_ReviewerR4Probes`, the `P-R14-5` direct `preScanVarMutations` unit pin (rtGuardCwd cannot discriminate SF-2 because `readClusterStep` already refused the shape at the argv layer — the AST taint is depth defense) plus its quoted positive, and the `R14-pos printf strftime width conv` positive; the glob-literal-prefix positive is narrowed to the zero-argument `printf lit*` spelling. All five discriminating R14 probes verified FAIL-first on the pre-fix tree `9139e0e` in an isolated `/tmp` snapshot (29 subtests failing including the pre-existing R4 rows, rc=1) and pass on the fixed tree; both positive rows pass on BOTH trees. Every new/changed small function measures strictly `cognitive < 35` under `cccc --no-config --no-cache` (`printfFmtConv` 22, `printfFmtMeasure` 4, `printfStarPositional` 3, `printfFmtWriteSlots` 16, `readDynClusterHit` 12) and `awkProgramFrags` stays at 71 (≤ 73).
- **Role guards: close the Reviewer R4 / Oracle R12 adversarial audit — 5 Must-Fix plus 2 Should-Fix items (`role_guards.go`, `shell_ast.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`).** *(Historical iteration — the glob-only format exemption described below is SUPERSEDED by the R14 closure above: a filename CAN spell `%n` as the expanded format text, so a dynamic format word with arguments is now unvettable regardless of shape.)*
  - **printf write-target scanning closes the format-string `%n` and glued/clustered `-v` holes (R4-MF-1/MF-3/MF-4 — zero-pipe local tar/git RCE).** `screenPrintfEnvWrite` previously stopped at the first format word or `--`, so the `%n` conversion — which stores printf's byte count into the shell variable NAMED by its aligned argument — was never scanned: `printf "%n" TAR_OPTIONS` / `printf "%s %n" x TAR_OPTIONS` / `printf -- "%n" TAR_OPTIONS` wrote the hook variable outright, and `preScanVarMutations` counted only the separate `-v VAR` spelling, so `cmd=/bin/true; printf -vcmd %s pig; "$cmd"` kept the static `/bin/true` bind through a second write. The scanner now walks the `internal_getopt(list, "v:")` cluster grammar in BOTH files (glued `-vVAR`, clustered `-v` and repeated `-v x` spellings) and then vets the format string through the new `printfFmtSlots`/`printfFmtWriteHit` helpers: a dynamic variable/command-substitution format text with arguments is unvettable (`env dynamic printf`), positional `m$` conversions fail closed, `%(...)T` consumes its argument without inheriting the parenthesized `%n` (strftime newline), `*`/`.*` width-precision arguments align the cycle, and Bash's format-reuse rule is replayed so every argument landing in a real `%n` slot is vetted through `envWriteTainted` — with every format argument required to stay a single argv word first, so an unquoted `$X` cannot field-split a fake alignment. ~~Glob-only format words (`printf lit* x`) stay ALLOW~~ *(superseded by the R14 closure: `printf lit* TAR_OPTIONS` writes TAR_OPTIONS when the expansion spells `%n` — a dynamic format word with arguments is now unvettable, and only the zero-argument `printf lit*` stays ALLOW)*. `printf '%s\n' $UNQUOTED` and `printf "%s" "$A" "$B"` stay ALLOW.
  - **Write-target array subscripts validate their base and reject arithmetic shapes (R4-MF-4).** The new shared `writeTargetBase` helper parses printf `-v`, `%n`, read positional and `-a` write targets: `arr[0]` reduces to the array base for the hook lookup (`printf -v TAR_OPTIONS[0] "%s" x` and `printf "%n" TAR_OPTIONS[0]` hit `env TAR_OPTIONS`), while any arithmetic/dynamic/unbalanced subscript (`x[1+1]`, `x[$(...)]`, `x[0`, a bare `]`) is unvettable and fails closed with `env dynamic printf`; `printf -v arr[0] "%s" x` stays ALLOW.
  - **printf first-word glob expansion into the option zone is refused (R4-MF-2).** `wordToArg` encodes unquoted globs as `<pattern>__ctx_glob__`; a format-position word whose FIRST byte is expansion-controlled (`*`, `?`, `[` or an extglob `@(`/`+(`/`!(` prefix) can expand into `-vNAME` at runtime, desynchronizing the statically scanned option zone — the new `printfGlobFormatHit` helper refuses it with `env dynamic printf` while a definite non-`-` literal prefix (`printf lit* '%s' x`) stays a format string.
  - **GNU tar `--occurrence` is an optional-argument option (R4-MF-5).** `--occurrence` is removed from `tarShortDataOpt`: the bare spelling consumes NO operand (`--occurrence[=N]` only), so `tar --occurrence --use-compress-program=pig -cf /dev/null /dev/null` can no longer swallow the exec hook as the phantom occurrence value — the hook is scanned and blocks.
  - **read's `-i` value letter and glued dynamic value tails (R4-SF-1).** `i` joins the value-letter table (`d n N p t u i`) in both scanners and `e` is boolean: a dynamic word whose static letter prefix already ended at a value letter carries a glued prompt/initial value, so `read -p"$PROMPT" answer` / `read -p"Prompt: $PROMPT" line` / `read -ei "$DEFAULT" answer` stay ALLOW, while a placeholder inside the option-letter zone (`read -p$P x` unquoted, `read "-$OPT"`) blocks with `env dynamic read`.
  - **getopts dynamic first operand standby slot narrowed to a real `--` shape (R4-SF-2).** The new `dynWordCanSpellDashDash` helper strips the placeholder markers and checks the surviving static bytes: only a dash-shaped remainder can expand to exactly `--` at runtime, so `H="--"; getopts "$H" opt TAR_OPTIONS` keeps vetting the standby write-target slot while `getopts ":$OPTS" opt TAR_OPTIONS` and `getopts ":$OPTS" opt "$arg"` — whose literal `:`/`o` survive every expansion — return ALLOW again.
  - **Regression coverage.** `zz_r2fix_test.go` gains `TestGuard_V622_ReviewerR4Probes` pinning 22 block rows and 16 positive guard rows — every probe verified FAIL-first (24 of 38 rows failing, rc=1) on the pre-fix tree `9139e0e` in an isolated `/tmp` snapshot overlaid with only this test file, and the full table passes on the fixed tree. Every new/changed small function measures strictly `cognitive < 35` under `cccc --no-config --no-cache` and `awkProgramFrags` stays ≤ 73.

- **Role guards: close the v6.2.2 Oracle R11 adversarial audit — 3 Must-Fix items plus observation R11-Obs-1 (`role_guards.go`, `shell_ast.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`).**
  - **Trailing dangling tar value options no longer panic the guard (R11-MF-1 — index-out-of-range crash).** `screenTarSubagent`'s operand-validation loop indexed `args[i+k]` for the statically derived consume count even when the words did not exist, so a trailing `tar -f` / `tar -C` / `tar --file` / `tar --directory` (and `tar -cf /dev/null -C`, shell AND argv forms) crashed the guard binary with `panic: index out of range`. The consume loop is bounded by `i+k < len(args)` and both `tarSubagentWordStep`/`tarSubagentLongStep` report consume 0 for a trailing dangling data option (tar itself errors at runtime — there is no operand word to validate).
  - **Quoted/escaped/enum-expanded/env -S-rewritten declaration builtins lose the field-splitting exemption (R11-MF-2 — zero-pipe local tar/bash RCE).** dash and bash gate POSIX declaration-utility status on an UNQUOTED, unescaped literal command token: `"export" FOO=$X` / `\export FOO=$X` / `'export' FOO=$X` / `for E in export; do $E FOO=$X; done` all run export as an ordinary command whose operands DO word-split, but the lenient R10 rule (only a peeled wrapper word flipped the check strict) let the smuggled `X='1 TAR_OPTIONS=--use-compress-program=pig'` through. `peelPreludeHasNonAssign` now vets four demotion channels — the peeled non-assignment prelude (channel 1), an `env -S` operand-stream rewrite detected by the loss of argv/head positional correspondence (channel 2), a quoted/escaped/concatenated command word proven through `seg.argWords`' parse shape (channel 3), and a for-loop enum-expanded head whose parse word is absent (channel 4) — while a genuine bash `DeclClause` segment (`shell_ast.go` now tags `seg.declClause`) is exempt because the parser itself only forms that node for a bare literal keyword. `export FOO=$X` / `A=1 export FOO=$X` / `command export FOO="$X"` stay `ALLOW`; every demoted dynamic spelling blocks with `env dynamic export`, including inside `bash -c` strings where the bash-dialect parse applies.
  - **printf/read/getopts write-target scanning models the real builtin option grammars and refuses dynamic option-zone words (R11-MF-3 — zero-pipe local tar/git RCE).** `screenEnvWriteTargets` is split into the per-command scanners `screenPrintfEnvWrite`/`screenReadEnvWrite`/`screenGetoptsEnvWrite` plus the shared `envWriteTainted`/`envDynSlotHit` slots: printf now walks `internal_getopt(list, "v:")` clusters exactly — glued `-vTAR_OPTIONS`, repeated `-v x -v TAR_OPTIONS` and clustered tails all vet their write-target names instead of only the first separate `-v`; read consumes `d n N p t u a` value letters cluster-wise and vets every separate value operand BEFORE skipping it (`read -p $X` can no longer word-split a forged write-target name past the static operand layout); getopts vets the OPTSTRING operand for word-splitting (`getopts $X -1` / `getopts p* -1` shift NAME into attacker-chosen slots). Quoted-safe dynamic OPTION-ZONE words are refused outright: `printf "$OPT"` / `printf "-$OPT"` pick their option letters at runtime even though the expansion never field-splits (`env dynamic printf`), `read "-$OPT"` picks its cluster letters at runtime (`env dynamic read`), and a dynamic getopts first operand that may expand to `--` at runtime shifts the positional layout so `getopts "$H" opt TAR_OPTIONS` / `getopts "$H" opt "$V"` vet the standby write-target slot. `printf '%s\n' "$X"` / `printf "val=$X\n"` / `printf -- -v TAR_OPTIONS` / `printf -v myvar` / `read -p "$PROMPT" x` / `getopts "$OPTS" opt` all stay `ALLOW`.

- **Role guards: close the v6.2.2 Oracle R9 adversarial audit — 4 Must-Fix items plus observations R9-Obs-3..4 (`role_guards.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`).**

The value now returns through the independent `aux` channel threaded `awkWOperandProgramLeaf` → `awkWOperandProgram`/`awkWCommaProgram` → `awkClusterWOperand` → `awkClusterFragsEnd` → `awkProgramFrags`, which appends it ONLY after the positional settlement — a library file name carrying an unclosed quote (`gawk -W i "a'b" 'BEGIN{system("pig")}'`) can never poison the REAL program text that precedes it, and all six `P-R8-MF1-*` probes block again while `gawk -W i /dev/null 'BEGIN{print 1}'` stays `ALLOW`.

  - **`MAWK_LONG_OPTIONS` joins the env-hook table (R8-MF-3 — local mawk zero-pipe RCE).** mawk(1) rewrites GNU `--` long-option handling at runtime (`ignore`/`warn` drop the option without consuming its operand, `allow` re-arms the `-W` boolean spellings), desynchronizing the guard's gawk-shaped parse from what the interpreter executes — `MAWK_LONG_OPTIONS=ignore awk --file=/dev/null 'BEGIN{system("pig")}'` and the `warn`/`--assign`/`allow --i` variants now block through the KEY=VAL prefix, `export`, `env` and `ctx_run env` channels with the env verdict.
  - **Comma-chained `-W` operands resolve the trailing item name (R8-SF-1).** The new `awkWLastItemName` helper extracts the LAST comma item's option name in both `awkClusterWOperand` and `awkOptWordTokenStep`: `-W interactive,exec` / `-W i,e` now retire the positional program word (`sawFile`) and end awk's option parse exactly like `-W exec`, so the legitimate read-only `awk -W interactive,exec parse.awk "$FILE"` and `xargs -a <p> -I{} awk -W interactive,exec parse.awk {}` are `ALLOW` again (in parity with `-W exec`) while `awk -W i,interactive 'BEGIN{system("pig")}'` keeps blocking.
  - **Regression coverage.** `zz_r2fix_test.go` gains `TestGuard_V622_OracleR8Probes` pinning all 18 `P-R8-*` probe rows (zero new symbol dependencies — `rtGuardCwd`, `guardCall.guardCtxRunEnv`, `awkProgramFrags` only) plus the quote-poison / `-W i,interactive` negative rows, the R8-Obs-1 `bash -c "bash --restricted <<< 'pig'"` row restoring real here-string discriminating power, and 9 positive guard rows. `awkProgramFrags` drops back to cognitive 71 (the dynamic-word and `-F`/`-v` steps are extracted as `awkDynWordStep`/`awkDataAssignOptStep`); every new/changed small function measures strictly `cognitive < 35` under `cccc --no-config --no-cache`.
- **Role guards: close the v6.2.2 Oracle R7 adversarial audit — 4 Must-Fix and 1 Should-Fix items, including two zero-false-positive R8 security regressions (`role_guards.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`).** *(Historical iteration — two mechanics described below are SUPERSEDED by the R8 closure above: the `-Wi` collision value now travels through an independent aux channel rather than the ordinary fragment list (the in-place merge described below regressed `gawk -W i /dev/null` positional-program extraction), and the `--assign`/`--field-separator` split validation in `awkProgramLongStep` claimed below was only wired in by R8 — the function body was never edited at this commit.)*
  - **`awkProgramFrags` restores the pre-terminator dynamic-word gate (R7-MF-1 — R8 security regression).** The R8 rewrite dropped the unconditional `isSafeDynamicDataOperand` check without keeping a `!endOpts` restriction, so every dynamic word BEFORE `--`/`-E` passed unreviewed once a program source existed. The dynamic-operand branch now blocks `!endOpts && (strings.HasPrefix(a, "-") || !isSafeDynamicDataOperand(a))`: `awk -f /dev/null "$X"` / `awk -f /dev/null $X` (mawk keeps parsing options after `-f`), `gawk '1' "$X"` / `gawk -e '1' "$X"` (getopt_long permutation) and `gawk '1' ./$X` block again with `dynamic code payload`, while `awk -f parse.awk -- "$FILE"`, `awk '1' -- "$FILE"`, `awk '1' "$PWD/data.txt"`, `awk '{print $1}' /tmp/*.log` and the static-variable rows stay `ALLOW`.
  - **Single-word `--` long options no longer fall into the short-cluster scanner (R7-MF-2 — R8 security regression).** `unwrapShellWordStep` gained an `isCluster` return: long-option words (settled or operand-consuming) report `isCluster=false` and `unwrapShellArgv` continues directly instead of falling through to `unwrapShellClusterWord`, whose byte-wise walk collided `--restricted`'s `c` and minted a bogus `("ted", true)` — bypassing `stdinProgramTargetsWithEnv` for `echo 'pig' | bash --restricted`, the here-string/heredoc spellings and the `guardCtxRunStdin` argv+stdin channel, and false-killing legitimate `bash --restricted "a'b.sh"` script names. All four entries block again and the script-file positive is `ALLOW` again; `unwrapShellLongWord`'s redundant `val != "/dev/null"` conjunction is simplified away.
  - **Bare `-c` boolean semantics and the POSIX `-`/`+` option terminators (R7-MF-3).** `interpDynWordStep` treats a bare shell `-c`/`+c` as a boolean letter that consumes NO operand (instead of the `valueExact` swallow that ate the following `-o` and hid the dynamic word) — `sh -c -o errexit -o $X 'echo hi'` / `bash -c -o errexit -o $X` / the `+o`/`-O` twins block with `dynamic code payload`. `unwrapShellWordStep` validates the bare `-o`/`+o`/`-O`/`+O` option-name operand for dynamic words when a `-c` is pending (depth parity with the cluster path). A bare `-` (and `+` under a pending shell `-c`) is settled exactly like `--` as an option terminator everywhere — `unwrapShellWordStep`, `scanOperandWord` (pending shell `-c`: the word AFTER the terminator is the command string, or an open `flagOnly` slot when none follows) and `interpOptionZoneHasToken` — so `sh -c - 'pig'` / `bash -c - 'pig'` / `sh -co errexit - 'pig'` / `sh -c - "$X"` and `xargs -a <p> sh -c -` block, while `sh -c 'echo "$1"' _ "$ARG"`, `sh -c -o errexit 'echo "$1"' _ "$ARG"`, `sh -c - 'echo "$1"' _ "$ARG"` and `xargs -I{} sh -c - 'echo "$1"' _ {}` stay `ALLOW`.
  - **mawk's `-W` grammar collision and comma-chained operands (R7-MF-4).** `-Wi`/`-W i`/`-W in` collide between gawk (`--include` library FILE consuming the next argv) and mawk (the zero-operand boolean `-W interactive` leaving the same word as the positional program): `awkWOperandProgram` keeps the `awkProgramFileHit` validation AND the `extraConsumed=1` consumption but also merges the value into the final program — *(the claim that this merge \"never suppresses positional extraction\" was factually wrong at this commit: the value entered the ordinary fragment list and DID suppress the `len(frags) == 0` positional gates — corrected in the R8 closure above through the independent aux channel)* — `awk -Wi 'BEGIN{system("pig")}'` / `awk -W i ...` / `awk -W in ...` block through the mawk interpretation while `awk -Wi 'BEGIN{print 1}'` and `gawk -W i /dev/null 'BEGIN{print 1}'` stay `ALLOW`. The new `awkWCommaProgram`/`awkWCommaTokenHit` helpers split comma-chained mawk operands (`-W interactive,exec` / `-W i,e`) item by item — only the trailing item may consume the next argv, the collision names chain as mawk booleans, and bare value-bearing items in non-final position fail closed — so `echo ... | awk -W interactive,exec /dev/stdin` blocks with `awk script from stdin`.
  - **Pure-data assignment slots validate unquoted word splitting (R7-SF-1).** The new `awkDataValSplitHit` helper intercepts `isDynamicArg(val) && !isSplitSafeDynamicWord(val)` in `-v`/`-F` (separate operands and glued cluster tails), `-W assign`/`field-separator` operands (separate AND inline `=` spellings, where the split re-parses the whole operand word) — *(the `--assign`/`--field-separator` long-step validation claimed in the original wording was factually wrong at this commit: `awkProgramLongStep` was never edited; the R8 closure above wires it in — R8-MF-2)*: `awk -v x=$VAR -f /dev/null`, `gawk -W assign=x=$VAR '1'` and `gawk -W assign $VAR '1'` block with `dynamic code payload` while the quoted spellings `-v ts="$val"` / `-W assign=x="$VAR"` / `-W assign "$VAR"` stay `ALLOW`.
  - **Regression coverage.** `zz_r2fix_test.go` gains `TestGuard_V622_OracleR7Probes` pinning all 25 `P-R7-*` probes (24 shell-command rows through `rtGuardCwd` plus the `guardCtxRunStdin` argv+stdin row for `bash --restricted`) and 15 positive guard rows. Note: the `bash --restricted <<< 'pig'` probe asserts the stable `bash-only syntax` verdict substring instead of `pig` because the outer dash-syntax gate blocks here-strings before stdin screening — the discriminating evidence is carried by probes a/c/d. Every new/changed small function measures strictly `cognitive < 35` under `cccc --no-config --no-cache`.
- **Role guards: close the v6.2.2 Oracle R6 adversarial audit — 4 Must-Fix and 2 Should-Fix items (`role_guards.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`).** *(Historical iteration — three mechanics described below are SUPERSEDED by the R7 closure above: the bare shell `-c`/`+c` is a boolean letter consuming no operand (not a `valueExact` code slot), the `-o`/`+o`/`-O`/`+O` operand is dynamic-validated rather than blindly skipped after a pending `-c`, and single-word `--` long options never reach the short-cluster scanner; the `!sawFile` dynamic-gate narrowing in R6-SF-1 is superseded by the restored `!endOpts` pre-terminator gate.)*
  - **POSIX shell pending `-c` modeled end-to-end as a boolean flag (R6-MF-1 — reverts the R7 regression).** POSIX shells parse `-c` as a BOOLEAN flag and keep scanning `-`/`+` options AFTER it: the command string is the first NON-OPTION word (or the word right after `--`), not the word following the `-c` cluster. `interpDynClusterStep` no longer lets shells share CPython's stop-on-first-code — the scan continues through the remaining option zone so `sh -co errexit -o $X 'echo hi'` / `bash -co errexit -o $X` / `sh -ce -o $X` block again with `dynamic code payload` (the R5-MF-1 repair had regressed them from `3ddc0a7`'s BLOCK to ALLOW). `unwrapShellArgv` is rebuilt around a pending-`-c` scan (extracted into `unwrapShellWordStep`/`unwrapShellLongWord`/`unwrapShellClusterWord`/`unwrapShellJoinTail`): after seeing `-c` it keeps skipping `-o`/`+o`/`-O`/`+O` operands, `--rcfile`/`--init-file` and short-option clusters until the first non-option word or the word after `--` — `bash -co errexit -o pipefail 'pig'` and `sh -co errexit -- 'pig'` now unwrap the REAL command string instead of the bogus `o\n-o` fragment. `scanLongWord` settles a pending flag-model code letter at the `--` terminator (next word as code, or an open `flagOnly` slot when no word follows — `xargs -a <p> sh -c --` blocks), and `interpOptionZoneHasToken`/`interpZoneWordStep`/`shellClusterZoneConsume` scan the pending `-c` cluster's zone through the remaining `-`/`+` words (consuming bare `-o` operands but never a `-c` operand) until the first non-option word or the post-`--` command word — `xargs -a <p> -I% sh -co errexit -- %` blocks while `sh -co errexit 'echo "$1"' _ "$ARG"` and `xargs -I{} sh -c 'echo "$1"' _ {}` stay `ALLOW`.
  - **`-W include`/`-W load` are library loads, not the program file (R6-MF-2).** `awkClusterWOperand` only sets `sawFile` for `-W file`/`-W exec` (and their GNU unique prefixes): `-W include=`/`-W include`/`-W i=`/`-W lo` keep the positional program word extractable, so `gawk -W include=/dev/null 'BEGIN{system("pig")}'`, `gawk -W include /dev/null '...'`, `gawk -W i=/dev/null '...'` and `gawk -W load=time '...'` all block on the real program text instead of discarding it.
  - **The `-W` option NAME slot fails closed on tokens/dynamic words, and the empty-program gate is gone (R6-MF-3).** `awkWOperandTokenStep` hits when the `-W` operand's name carries the token (`xargs -a <p> -I{} awk -W '{}' /dev/stdin` — mawk AND gawk — and `xargs -a <p> -I{} gawk -W '{}' '1'` block), and `awkWOperandProgram` fails closed on a dynamic or ambiguous-prefix name or a dynamic non-assignment value (`X='exec'; awk -W "$X" /dev/stdin` and `X='source=BEGIN{system("pig")}'; gawk -W "$X" '1'` block) while pure-data assignments `-W assign=x={}` / `-W assign '{}'` / `-W assign "$VAR"` stay exempt. `awkProgramTokenHit` scans the option zone UNCONDITIONALLY — the `if sawFile || program != ""` gate is deleted — so `xargs -a <p> -I{} gawk -e '' {}` / `gawk -e '' -l '{}'` / `gawk -e '' -i '{}'` can no longer hide the fed word behind an empty `-e` fragment.
  - **gawk optional-argument short options end their cluster (R6-MF-4).** `awkClusterFragsEnd` and `awkOptWordTokenStep` recognize getopt_long's `d::`/`D::`/`L::`/`o::`/`p::`: a glued tail is the option's own argument and ends the cluster immediately without consuming the next argv — the tail's letters can no longer reach the `e`/`f`/`v` cases, so `gawk -Lfatal 'BEGIN{system("pig")}'`, `gawk -Lno-ext '...'`, `gawk -p/dev/null '...'` and `xargs -a <p> -I{} gawk -d/tmp/v {} '1'` all block, while the legal `gawk -Lfatal 'BEGIN{print 1}'` lint row stays `ALLOW`.
  - **Post-terminator dynamic program word and the non-letter-tail pending parity (R6-SF-1/R6-SF-2).** `awkProgramFrags`' dynamic-operand gate uses `!sawFile` instead of `!endOpts` — `awk -- "$VAR"` blocks with `dynamic code payload` (mawk AND gawk execute it as the program) — while the dead unreachable `a == "--source"` case is removed and the input-data exemption is narrowed to words AFTER a program source so `awk -f parse.awk -- "$FILE"` / `awk '1' -- "$FILE"` are `ALLOW` again (the pre-existing unconditional `isSafeDynamicDataOperand` gate had blocked them on the clean baseline). `clusterConsumesOperand` keeps `hasFlagCode` through a non-letter cluster tail in lockstep with `scanShortCluster`'s pending model — `node -pCfoo-bar '1' -r $X` / `bun -pc/dev/null '1' -r $X` / `tsx -pP./tsconfig.json '1' -r $X` block again.
  - **Regression coverage.** `zz_r2fix_test.go` gains `TestGuard_V622_OracleR6Probes` pinning all 24 `P-R6-*` probes (xargs `-a` no-pipe entries for exact `dynamic code payload` verdicts per R3-Obs-2) plus the 10 positive guard rows through the shared `rtGuardCwd` entry. `unwrapShellArgv` drops from cognitive 87 to 13 (extracted steps 18/23/7/2), `interpZoneWordStep` 33→22 (long-word step extracted), and every new/changed small function measures strictly `cognitive < 35` under `cccc --no-config --no-cache`.
- **Role guards: close the v6.2.2 Oracle R5 adversarial audit — 4 Must-Fix and 3 Should-Fix items (`role_guards.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`).** *(Historical iteration — the `interpDynClusterStep` shell stop described below is SUPERSEDED by the R6 closure above: POSIX `-c` is a boolean flag there, not a stop-on-first-code slot.)*
  - **The R6 `flagCodes`+`valShort` cluster regression is reverted with correct two-slot consumption (R5-MF-1).** `clusterConsumesOperand` now returns the `(consumesVal, hasPendingCode)` pair: a bare value letter sets `consumesVal` (a glued value tail does not) and a pending flag-model code letter survives BOTH shapes as `hasPendingCode` — matching `scanShortCluster`'s pending model exactly. `interpDynClusterStep` checks the value operand for dynamic words BEFORE skipping it even when a pending code letter shares the cluster, then consumes the extra pending code slot for multi-code interpreters: `sh -co $X 'echo hi'` / `bash -co $X` / `node -pr $X` / `node -pC $X` / `bun -pc $X` block again with `dynamic code payload` (the R6 `(true,true)` mislabel had regressed them to `ALLOW`), and `node -pC default '1' -r $X` / `node -pCdefault '1' -r $X` / the `xargs -a` fed variants consume both slots so the fed word can no longer pose as the first script operand. `interpClusterZoneStep` consumes the value slot plus the pending code slot in the same shapes, and `unwrapShellArgv` surfaces a dynamic `-o` option-name operand (`sh -co $X`) as screened text instead of silently skipping it (depth defense). `clusterConsumesNext` keeps the `consumesVal && !hasPendingCode` projection so `stdinInterpTargetsScan` is unchanged.

An xargs-fed word can no longer expand into `-eBEGIN{system("pig")}` / `-f/dev/stdin` inside awk's permuted option zone — `xargs -a <p> -I{} gawk -e '1' {}` / `gawk -e '1' /dev/null {}` and the mawk-triggering `awk -f /dev/null {}` spellings all block — while `find . -exec awk -f parse.awk {} \;`, the `-v`/`--` and `./{}` rows stay `ALLOW`.
  - **gawk's GNU unique prefixes aligned and the library/assignment operands consumed (R5-MF-4 + R5-SF-1).** The long-option tables now match GNU gawk's real `optab[]` minimum unique prefixes (`exec:1`, `include:1`, `load:2`, `source:2`, `file:3`, `assign:1`, `field-separator:3`), with ambiguous short prefixes failing closed: `--e`/`-W e` (`--exec`), `--lo`/`-W lo`/`-l` (`--load`), `-W in` and `--as`/`--fie` all resolve. `--include`/`-i` and `--load`/`-l` operands now go through `awkProgramFileHit` in every walker (pipe, argv+stdin and cluster shapes) — `gawk --include=/dev/stdin '1'` / `gawk -i /dev/stdin '1'` block with `awk script from stdin` — without marking `sawFile` so the positional program still extracts. `-W assign` / `-W field-separator` / `--as` / `--fie` consume their separate value operand in both the program extraction AND the token zone scans, so the value word can no longer usurp the positional program slot: `gawk -W assign 'x=1' 'BEGIN{system("pig")}'` and `gawk --as 'x=1' '...'` block on the real program text. `awkProgramFrags`' short-option cases are `!endOpts`-gated (words after `--`/`-E` are awk's argv data — `gawk 'BEGIN{print ARGV[1]}' -- -f -` stays `ALLOW`), the dead `--file=`/`--exec=` case is removed (the long-step handles them), and `awkOptZoneHasToken` now actually sets `endOpts` after `-E`/`--exec`/`-W exec` instead of only claiming so in a comment.
  - **Test/changelog closure (R5-SF-2 / R5-SF-3).** `zz_r2fix_test.go` gains `TestGuard_V622_OracleR5Probes` pinning every `P-R5-*` probe (xargs `-a` no-pipe entries for exact `dynamic code payload` verdicts, echo-pipe and `guardCtxRunArgv` argv+stdin channels for the `awk script from stdin` rows) plus the positive rows and target-layer unit assertions; `P-R3-MF3-b` is tightened to `python inline code`, `P-R3-MF6-d/e` to the exact `piped stdin to ssh` verdict, `P-R4-MF4-k` to `awk script from stdin`, the duplicated `P-R4-MF2` rows are replaced with distinct php/tsx shapes, and the `R4-Obs-1` script append-mode regression row is added. Historical R2/R3 changelog entries that still describe since-superseded mechanics (`peelSingleWrapper`, `--exec-path=` in the exemption list, `clusterValueConsumesNext`, the node add-on table) are annotated as such. Every new/changed small function measures `cognitive < 35` under `cccc --no-config --no-cache`.

  - **tar's boolean `-h` removed from the cluster value table (R4-MF-1 / Reviewer R3-2).** `tarSubagentClusterStep` no longer treats the dereference BOOLEAN `-h` as a value letter, so it can no longer swallow a following `--use-compress-program=`/`--to-command=`/`--checkpoint-action=exec=` operand or truncate `-chI`: `tar -h`/`tar -ch`/`tar -xh` in front of an exec hook and `tar -chI pig -cf ...` all block. (The intermediate R3-era claim that the phantom `-h` was "gone from the data-option table" covered only `tarShortDataOpt`, not this cluster walker — corrected.)

  - **gawk's `-W` compatibility letter parsed under GNU getopt_long `"W;"` semantics, `-E`/`--exec` wired in, and positional operands no longer end the awk option zone (R4-MF-4 / Reviewer R3-3).** `-W opt[=val]` now resolves through `awkLongProgramName` exactly like `--opt[=val]` — inline `-W source=PROG`/`source PROG`, the file-source pair `-W file=`/`exec`/`include`/`load` (with GNU unique prefixes like `-W 'so=...'`), all sharing the `-f` stdin-device/dynamic-path validation, so `gawk -W file=-`/`/dev/stdin`, `gawk -W source 'BEGIN{system("pig")}'` and `xargs -I{} gawk -W exec '{}'` block. `-E`/`--exec` (and `--exec=`) consume their program-file operand with the same validation and end awk's own option scan — `echo ... | gawk -E /dev/stdin` / `gawk --exec /dev/stdin` block with `awk script from stdin` in the echo-pipe, argv+stdin AND heredoc entries. `awkOptZoneHasToken` skips positional operands instead of ending the scan (GNU gawk's getopt_long permutes options after operands), closing the post-positional `-f '{}'`/`--file='{}'`/`-i '{}'`/`-W 'file={}'` spellings while glued pure-data assignments (`-vf={}`/`-F{}`/`--assign=f={}`) stay allowed in parity with the separate-operand form (R4-SF-3).

- **Role guards: close the v6.2.2 R5 xargs replacement-token blind spot (`role_guards.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`).** *(Historical iteration — the positional rule below is SUPERSEDED by the R4 closure above: the `i == len(h)-1` last-word exemption it introduced was itself bypassable through glued/equals code options and no longer exists.)* `interpZoneWordStep` gained a `token` parameter replacing the hard-coded `isBareReplToken` spelling list, blocking the non-last-position token forms `xargs -I{} perl -e '1' {} 'system("pig")'` and `xargs -IFOO perl -e '1' FOO 'system("pig")'`; the now-unused `isBareReplToken` helper is deleted.

  - **tar long data options and the BSD first word (R3-MF-1 / Reviewer R2-1).** `tarSubagentLongStep` now consumes the separate operand of the plain-data long options (`--file`/`--directory`/...) instead of letting the value word pose as a BSD action word, the separate `--checkpoint-action exec` form arms the hook exactly like the glued one, and the phantom `-h` entry is gone from the data-option table (tar `-h` is the dereference BOOLEAN). The BSD legacy action scan is strictly limited to argv[0] with no preceding dashed option and walks the word letter by letter — `cIf`/`cfI` count the consumed value-letter operands so the `I` compress-program operand lands in `cmd` — restoring the baseline `BLOCK` on `tar -c --file foo.tar --use-compress-program=pig /dev/null`, `tar --directory /tmp/f --checkpoint=1 --checkpoint-action=exec=pig -cf /dev/null /dev/null` and closing `tar -cf /dev/null /etc/fstab --use-compress-program=pig` / `tar cIf pig /dev/null /dev/null`.

  - **flock's real long-option table with fail-closed unknowns (R3-MF-3 / Reviewer R2-3).** `flockMatchLong` now mirrors util-linux flock's real `getopt_long` table (`--no-fork`/`--nb` spelled correctly, the phantom `fd`/`lock-file`/`debug` entries removed so the legal `--f` abbreviation of `--fcntl` resolves uniquely), and `skipFlockLong` fails CLOSED on an unknown or ambiguous long option instead of guessing a two-word width that swallowed the lock file — `flock --no-fork /dev/null pig`, `flock --no-fork /dev/null python3 -c '...'`, `flock --f /dev/null pig` and `flock --nb /dev/null pig` are blocked again.
  - **script's permutation-accurate token zone plus `-E`/`--echo` (R3-MF-4 / Reviewer R2-4).** `scriptOptZoneHasToken` scans every word before the `--` terminator — positional operands included — because util-linux `script`'s `getopt_long` permutes options after operands, so a pre-`--` placeholder can expand into `-c`/`--command` from ANY position: the `xargs -a`, `find -exec` and `argv+stdin` spellings of `script % 'pig' /dev/null` / `script /dev/null % 'pig'` now block with the precise `dynamic code payload` verdict instead of depending on an upstream pipe rule. `scanScriptCluster`/`scanScriptLong` gain the real `-E`/`--echo` value option.
  - **Multi-code-slot cluster operands and the legitimate data-file boundary (R3-MF-5 / Reviewer R2-6 first row).** `clusterConsumesNext` is upgraded to `clusterConsumesOperand` returning a `(consumes, isCode)` pair: short clusters of the multi-code interpreters (`perl -we`/`ruby -we`/`php -nr`/`node -pe`/`bun -pe`) now consume their trailing CODE operand and keep scanning the option zone, so `perl -we '1' -I $X`, `ruby -we '1' -I $X`, `php -nr '1;' -c $X`, `node -pe '1' -r $X` and `bun -pe '1' -c $X` fail closed on the dynamic value operand, while option-position placeholders after a code slot (`perl -e '1' % 'system("pig")'`, `perl -e '1' -%`) still block. Conversely a STATIC post-code positional of a multi-code interpreter is input DATA (never re-parsed) and no longer counts as a zone hit, restoring `find . -exec perl -pe 's/a/b/' {} \;` to `ALLOW`.
  - **git/awk/xargs residual escapes (R3-MF-6 / Reviewer R2-5/R2-6).** `gitExecConfigValueHasToken` checks the KEY side of `key=val` for the token (`git -c '{}=pig' log` blocks), `--config-env` operands are screened with the same exec-capability predicate instead of being treated as pure data, and the glued pure-data path forms (`--git-dir={}`/`--work-tree=`/`--namespace=`/`--exec-path=` — *the `--exec-path=` exemption was removed again in the R4 closure above because it redirects git's subprogram lookup*) are explicitly exempted so `find . -name .git -exec git --git-dir={} log -1 \;` is `ALLOW` again while unknown token-carrying `--*` words still fail closed. `awkProgramFrags`' positional fallback is `!sawFile`-gated at the source (an input filename after `-f` is never folded into the program), `awkProgramTokenHit` drops the `!sawFile` mask so `gawk -f /dev/null /dev/null -e '{}'` blocks, `-W` is parsed again in both walkers (`gawk -e '1' -W 'source={}' /dev/null` blocks), and the pure-data assignment operands `-v`/`-F`/`--assign`/`--field-separator` are consumed without hitting on the value itself (`awk -v fname={} 'END{print fname}' {}` and `xargs -I{} awk -v k={} ...` stay `ALLOW` — R3-Obs-1). `xargsDerivedSeg` no longer lets a benign here-string/heredoc suppress the `-a` append risk — only a real pipe or file redirection counts as a direct stdin feed — so `xargs -a <payload> ssh dmit sh <<< 'echo hi'` and the heredoc twin block with the ssh verdict while the R12 precise verdicts (`piped stdin to ssh` / `file stdin to ssh stdin-program`) are unchanged; `xargsHasArgFile` resolves GNU unique-prefix abbreviations through `xargsMatchValueLong`/`xargsLongConsumesNext` so `--arg-f=` cannot drift apart from `--arg-file` (the verifier-side `xargs --arg-f=/tmp/items -I{} ssh dmit cat < /etc/passwd` exfiltration blocks with `ssh remote write`).
  - **Node spec single-source and dead-code cleanup (R3-SF-1/R3-SF-2).** Node's `-C` (`--conditions` short alias) and the readonly-plumbing long values (`--title`/`--env-file`/`--env-file-if-exists`/`--redirect-warnings`/`--diagnostic-dir`/`--watch-path`) now live in `lookupInterpOptSpec("node")` itself — `node --title x -e ...`, `node --env-file /dev/null -e ...`, `node -C default -e ...` and the `... | node -C default -` stdin spelling all block — and the `stdinInterpTargetsScan` add-on loop is gone so all four scanner families share the single spec source. The dead `interpOptSpec.longValueRe` field, the redundant `len(a) <= 2 && len(a) != 2` test and `xargsFindFeedsCodeSlot`'s stale `findExecSlotValueOpts` reference (now `isFindValueOpt`) are removed. Regression coverage: `TestGuard_V622_OracleR3Probes` pins every `P-R3-*` probe plus the Reviewer R2-6 legitimate-data `ALLOW` rows, asserts the target verdict substring (pipe/redirection masking avoided through `xargs -a` payload files and `argv+stdin` inputs per R3-Obs-2), and carries direct unit-level assertions for `wrapperChainEnvSplitHasToken`/`scriptOptZoneHasToken`/`gitExecConfigValueHasToken`/`flockMatchLong` so no upstream rule can mask a target-layer failure; every new/changed small function measures `cognitive < 35` under `cccc --no-config --no-cache`.
- **Role guards: close the `v6.2.2` Oracle R2 adversarial audit — 7 Must-Fix, 4 Should-Fix and 2 Observation items plus the Reviewer R1 confirmed items (`role_guards.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`).**
  - **Long-value options unified under `valueExact` (R2-MF-1).** `hasDynamicInterpOption`'s `--*` branch no longer `continue`s past the value table: every non-Python long value option (shell `--rcfile`/`--init-file`, node/bun/tsx `--conditions`/`--cwd`/`--input-type`/`--require`/`--import`/`--preload`/...) consumes its operand through the spec-derived `valueExact` table and a dynamic operand fails closed unless the slot is itself inline code — `bash --rcfile /dev/null "$OPT" 'pig'`, `bash --rcfile /dev/null --rcfile /etc/profile -c 'pig'`, `node --conditions default --import $X`, `bun --cwd . -c $X` and `node --input-type commonjs -r $X` all block again. The `spec.longValueRe` reverse-polarity entries (`--require`/`--loader`/`--preload` in `codeFlags`) are removed so `node --require $X` cannot launder through the code-flag exemption.
  - **Spec-driven short-cluster value consumption (R2-MF-2).** *(Historical iteration — `clusterValueConsumesNext` was superseded by `clusterConsumesOperand` in R3, whose `(consumes,isCode)` pair was itself corrected to `(consumesVal,hasPendingCode)` in the R5 closure above.)* The new `clusterConsumesNext`/`clusterValueConsumesNext` helpers derive cluster value arities from `interpOptSpec` in lockstep with `scanShortCluster` and are shared by `hasDynamicInterpOption` (dynamic operand checked BEFORE the skip — `python3 -uW $X`, `sh -xo errexit "$OPT" 'pig'`, `perl -wI /tmp -I $X` block) and `stdinInterpTargetsScan` (whose hard-coded `valueOpts` copy is replaced by the spec-derived table, closing the `bun --cwd . -` / `tsx --tsconfig tsconfig.json -` stdin escapes). `pyInterpScanStep` honours `consumesNext` again: a glued `-m<mod>` cluster carries its module name inside the word, so `python3 -mjson.tool "$HOME/a.json"` and `python3 -mpytest tests/*.py` are correctly allowed (R2 regression).
  - **sed/awk/git single-source parsers (R2-MF-3).** `sedScriptTokenHit`/`awkProgramTokenHit` reuse the repository's own `sedScripts`/`awkProgramFrags` grammars: once an explicit script source appeared the remaining positional words are input-file DATA — `find -exec sed -e 's/foo/bar/' {} \;`, `sed -n -e '1,5p'`, `sed -f rules.sed` and `find -exec awk -f parse.awk {} \;` are allowed again (R2 regression) — while the option-zone scans cover the glued/clustered and GNU-abbreviated spellings (`sed -ne'{}'`, `--expr='{}'`, `gawk -e'{}'`/`--source`/`--assign`). `gitConfigExecTokenHit` skips the global value options (`-C`/`--git-dir`/`--work-tree`/`--namespace`) as pure data (keeping `git -C {} status` allowed), intercepts tokens in the option zone itself (`git % 'core.pager=pig' log`) and in no-`=` `-c` values, and routes key capability through `gitKeyIsExecCapable` — `git -c 'merge.foo.driver={}'`/`diff.foo.command`/`credential.<url>.helper` block.
  - **`script`/`flock` GNU state machines (R2-MF-4).** util-linux `script`'s `-t`/`--timing` is an `optional_argument` — only the glued form carries a value, a bare `-t`/`--timing` never consumes the next argv (`script -t -c 'pig' /dev/null` blocks again, R2 regression); `scanScriptLong` gains `log-io`/`output-limit`/`logging-format` and GNU prefixes, and `scanScriptOpts` no longer ends the scan at the first positional typescript file because util-linux `script`'s `getopt_long` permutes options after operands (`script /dev/null -c 'pig'` blocks). `flockMatchLong` resolves util-linux `flock`'s real long options with GNU unique-prefix abbreviation across `skipFlockArgs`/`skipFlockLong`/`flockAfterLock` — `flock --com='pig' /dev/null`, `flock --time 5 /dev/null -c 'pig'` and post-lock-file `flock /tmp/lk --comm='pig'` block, while ambiguous prefixes stay conservative and the positive shapes `flock -xw 0 /dev/null true` / `flock -x -w 30 /dev/null true` parse the command head correctly.
  - **ssh/xargs stdin-tunnel closure (R2-MF-5).** `sshLocalZoneHasToken` consumes value-letter operands with `sshShortTakesValue` (checking the operand for the token first) so `-p 22` no longer poses as `<destination>` — `ssh -p 22 {} dmit ls` blocks; `xargsDerivedSeg` closes both stdin-tunnel escapes: `xargs -a <file> ssh dmit sh` (no outer stdin stream — the `-a` file feeds the appended words) and the bare `argv=["xargs","ssh","dmit","sh"]` + ctx_run-stdin spelling now derive `pipedStdin` for stdin-program targets while the precise R12 verdicts (`piped stdin to ssh` for real pipes, `file stdin to ssh stdin-program` for `-a` + file redirection) are unchanged.
  - **Spec-driven interpreter option-zone scan (R2-MF-6).** `interpOptionZoneHasToken` is driven by `lookupInterpOptSpec`: value options consume their operands (`python3 -W ignore % '...'`, `sh -o errexit % 'pig'` block), `bun -c`/`php -c` are value slots instead of premature zone ends, multi-code interpreters (perl/ruby/node/bun/tsx/php) keep scanning after each `-e`/`-r` slot (`perl -e '1' % 'system("pig")'`, `php -r '1;' % ...` block) while shells and CPython still stop at the first code slot (positional `$0`/`$1` data preserved), and `replTokenInVariant`'s `script` branch scans the option zone (`script % 'pig' /dev/null` blocks).
  - **Wrapper-chain-constrained `env -S` scan (R2-MF-7).** `peelWrappersWithPayloads` runs the new `wrapperChainEnvSplitHasToken` inside the alias-rewrite loop on every intermediate chain: the walk steps wrapper positions with `peelWrappers`' exact arities, screens any `env` position (including PATH-resolved aliases) for a token-carrying `-S` payload, and stops at the first real command head — `./myenv -S "perl -e '1' {}"` (symlink alias) blocks while `find -exec rg env -S {} \;` and `xargs -I{} echo env -S {}` are allowed again (R2 regression).
  - **Should-Fix/Observation closure (R2-SF-1..4, R2-Obs-1..2).** `peelExecSlotHead` writes the `canonicalResolvedBase` spelling back as a variant (`gfind`/`gxargs` face the canonical screens); `isFindValueOpt` adds `-amin`/`-atime`/`-cmin`/`-ctime`/`-context` and the `-newerXY` family (`find . -newermm -exec -exec pig ';'` blocks, `xargs -I{} find . -ctime {}` allowed); `xargsReplToken` treats `--replace=` (empty) as the default `{}` and `xargsMatchValueLong` resolves GNU abbreviations of the value long options (`--max-a`/`--arg-f*`); `tarExecOptTokenHit`/`screenTarSubagent` cover clustered `-I` (`tar -cI '{}'`) and GNU prefixes of `--checkpoint-action`/`--to-command`/`--use-compress-program`; `stdinInterpTargetsScan` derives its value table from the spec (plus node readonly-plumbing extras `--title`/`--env-file-if-exists`/`--redirect-warnings`/`--diagnostic-dir`/`--watch-path` — *the extras table itself was merged into `lookupInterpOptSpec("node")` in the R3 closure above*); `interpFileRunStep` uses `len(a) >= 2` so a bare two-character `-c` before `--version` stops the readonly file-run scan; `TestGuard_V622_OracleR2Probes`/`TestGuard_V622_OracleR2EnvCwd` pin every `P-R2-*` probe with positive and negative rows (the `xargs -a` probe pre-creates its payload file in `t.TempDir()` so a leading redirect cannot mask the target verdict, and the R12 exact-verdict rows are pinned alongside), including the previously missing `P-MF2-e` heredoc row; the `169→74` changelog figure is corrected to `169→22` (measured). All new/extracted small functions measure `cognitive < 35` under `cccc --no-config --no-cache` (peak `sshLocalZoneHasToken` 34 / `interpClusterZoneStep` 34); the post-R2 refactor split `screenTarSubagent`'s scan loop into the `tarSubagentWordStep`/`tarSubagentLongStep`/`tarSubagentClusterStep` word steps, cutting it from cognitive 51 to 12 with no behavior change.
- **Role guards: close the `v6.2.2` Oracle R1 adversarial audit — 9 Must-Fix, 5 Should-Fix and 2 Observation items across the interpreter option scanners, the structural-alias tables and the exec-slot descent (`role_guards.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`).**
  - **Python option-zone termination and short-cluster parity (MF-1/MF-2).** `hasDynamicInterpOption` now ENDS the option-zone scan at the first `-c`/`-m` (separate, glued `-c...`/`-m...` or clustered `-uc`/`-um`), matching CPython's own `_PyOS_GetOpt` semantics — `python3 -m pytest --cov=x`, `python3 -m json.tool --sort-keys` and `python3 -c 'print(1)' --foo` are correctly allowed again (regression introduced earlier in this release). The shared `pyShortClusterEnd` helper aligns all four Python scanners on `-uW`/`-uX`-style clusters: `-uW ignore` consumes `ignore` as the `-W` operand in `hasDynamicInterpOption`, `screenInterpFileRun`/`interpFileRunStep`, and the extracted `stdinInterpTargetsScan`, so `OPT=-c; python3 -uW ignore "$OPT" 'import os; os.system("pig")'`, the `--jit off` / `--check-hash-based-pycs=bogus` shuffles, the heredoc/pipe/`xargs -0` stdin variants and the `python3 -uX --version evil.py` readonly file-run all fail closed; `unwrapPythonSlots` no longer skips a `flagOnly` open code slot into a screening vacuum.
  - **Per-interpreter value/code flag separation (MF-3).** `hasDynamicInterpOption` derives its value table from `lookupInterpOptSpec(base)` instead of one cross-interpreter table — `bun -c` (bunfig file), `node`/`bun`/`tsx -r`/`--require`/`--loader`/`--preload` and `php -c` are plain VALUE slots whose dynamic operand fails closed, closing the `X='a.toml -e ...'; bun -c $X` security regression and the same-shape `node -r $X` / `php -c $X` escapes (the `longValueRe`-era reverse-polarity wording is superseded by the R2 closure ABOVE — the field itself is removed in the R3 closure).
  - **Structural-alias and shell rcfile tables (MF-4a/MF-4b, SF-2).** `npm`/`pnpm`/`yarn` join `isStructuralGuardHead` and `headBaseAliasHits` normalizes resolved target basenames through `canonicalResolvedBase`, so `bun -> npm-cli.js` / `node -> npm` symlinks hit `conflicting structural alias` instead of slipping `bun exec -- pig` past `screenNestedDispatch`; `peelExecSlotHead` canonicalizes the typed basename before family-conflict checks. `lookupInterpOptSpec` for shell heads gains `--rcfile`/`--init-file` as consumed value options and `shellRcfileDynamicHit` fails closed on any non-`/dev/null` or dynamic rcfile operand — `bash --rcfile /etc/profile -c 'pig'`, `xargs bash --rcfile /dev/null -c` (open slot) and `find -exec bash --rcfile ... -c '{}' \;` block, while `stdinShellTargetsScan` (extracted from `stdinProgramTargetsWithEnv`) consumes the rcfile operand so `echo pig | bash --rcfile /dev/null` can no longer hide the stdin-program verdict.
  - **`env -S` split-injection scan across the whole wrapper chain (MF-5).** `peelWrappersWithPayloads` now scans EVERY wrapper-chain position for an `env` head whose raw `-S`/`--split-string` payload carries the token (was: only `cur[0]` before peeling, which `nice`/`timeout`/`FOO=1` prefixes and nested `env -- env` hid behind a single-pass peel); `envHeadSplitHasToken` normalizes the head through `canonicalResolvedBase` (genv), treats bare `-` as `-i` and keeps scanning, and `envLongSplitHasToken` resolves GNU unique-prefix abbreviations through `envMatchLong` — `xargs -I{} nice env -S 'perl {}'`, `FOO=1 env -S`, `env - -S` and `env --split`/`--s=`/`--ch /tmp -S` all hit.
  - **Recursion caller re-anchor (MF-6).** `replTokenInVariant`'s `xargs`/`flock`/`watch`/`find` descents and `xargsVariantFeedsCodeSlot`'s `xargs`/`flock`/`watch` descents pass the env-`-C`-re-anchored `tCaller` instead of the pre-peel `outerCaller`, so `xargs -I{} env -C sub watch -x ./mysh -c '{}'` (and the flock/find/append-mode twins) resolve `./mysh -> /bin/sh` inside the chdir'd directory and block.
  - **`find` predicate alignment (MF-7, Obs-1).** `replTokenInFindPathWord` scans the whole remaining operand list for `;`/`+`/`-exec*` operators instead of bailing at the first non-`-` word — `xargs -I% find . % pig ';'` blocks while `xargs -I{} find {} -maxdepth 1 -name '*.go'` stays allowed; `findExecSlotValueOpts` gains `-fprint`/`-fprint0`/`-fls`/`-files0-from` and `-fprintf` consumes its real TWO operands (FILE FORMAT) in all three walkers — `xargs -I{} find . -fprintf /dev/null -exec -exec sh -c '{}' ';'` can no longer shift the `-exec` alignment; `xargsFindFeedsCodeSlot` treats append-mode (`repl == ""`) as an open injection into find's expression tail; `-H`/`-L`/`-P` no longer mark `pathDone` so `xargs -I{} find -L {} -name '*.go'` is allowed. `screenFindArgsSeg`'s `-exec` inner screen is extracted into `screenFindExecInner` and its unreachable duplicate readonly check dropped, cutting the function from cognitive 76 to 28.
  - **Placeholder injection beyond head position (MF-8, SF-1).** `interpOptionZoneHasToken` scans the shell/interpreter option zone (up to the first code slot or static script operand) for the token — `xargs -I% sh % 'pig'`, `xargs -I% python3 % '...'` and the `-e`/`perl` equivalents block; the inner `xargs` option zone (`xargs -a /tmp/p -% sh -c '{}'`) and `flock`'s lock-file/option zone (`flock % 'sh -c pig' /dev/null`) are scanned before descending; `sshLocalZoneHasToken` covers ssh's local parse zone (`xargs -I{} ssh {} dmit ls` — the token in `<destination>` becomes `-oProxyCommand=` at runtime), `replTokenInSSH` treats ANY token inside the joined remote command as remote `$SHELL -c` text, and `xargsSSHFeedsCodeSlot` covers append-mode joins (`xargs ssh dmit echo hi`) while deferring to the precise stdin-tunnel verdict when the remote command is itself a stdin-program consumer (`xargs ssh dmit sh` keeps the exact `piped stdin to ssh` rule). The `len(rseg.argv) == 0` / `parseErr` ordering in both ssh screens is corrected so parse failures fail closed instead of being dead code.
  - **`script`/`flock` option state machines (MF-9).** The new `scanScriptOpts`/`scanScriptLong`/`scanScriptCluster` model util-linux `script`'s real operand arity — valued letters `O/I/B/T/t/o/m` end their cluster (the `c` inside `-T/tmp/xc` is a filename byte, never a flag) and GNU `--com` abbreviations of `--command` count — so `xargs script -T/tmp/xc -c`, `script -T/tmp/c.log -c '{}' /dev/null` and `script -q --com='{}' /dev/null` all hit. `skipFlockArgs` is split into `flockClusterScan`/`skipFlockLong`/`flockAfterLock` (cognitive 35 → 33/16/4/7): `-F`/`--fcntl` are boolean again, short clusters (`-xc`, `-xw 0`, glued `-cCMD`) resolve letter-by-letter, and `--` lock-file consumption still checks for a following `-c`/`--command` — `flock -xc '{}' /dev/null`, `flock -- /dev/null -c '{}'` and `flock --fcntl /dev/null sh -c '{}'` block.
  - **Adjacent-gap Should-Fix items (SF-3/SF-4/SF-5, Obs-2).** `xargsReplToken` recognizes GNU `xargs`' unique `--r*` abbreviations (`--repl`/`--rep`/`--re`/`--r`); `replTokenInToolExecSlots` covers `awk`'s program-text operand, `sed`'s `-e`/`-f` script operands, `tar`'s `--checkpoint-action`/`--to-command`/`--use-compress-program` values and `git -c` command-exec config keys; `node` gains `--input-type`, `bun` gains `--conditions`/`--define`/`--target`/`--tsconfig-override` in `lookupInterpOptSpec.longValue` (consumed in lockstep by `hasDynamicInterpOption` through the spec-derived table); `TestGuard_V622_JSFamilySplit`'s `v622-2c` leg now restores the ORIGINAL `PATH` (the previous no-op `t.Setenv("PATH", os.Getenv("PATH"))` read back the already-modified value, so the direct `bun -p` leg never ran against the real PATH) and asserts the exact `javascript inline code` verdict; `scanLongWord`'s unreachable `stopOnFirstCode` tail is dropped (the only `stopOnFirstCode` interpreter — CPython — has no `longCode` table, so a long option can never emit a code slot there). Regression coverage: `TestGuard_V622_OracleR1Probes`/`TestGuard_V622_OracleR1EnvCwd` (`zz_r2fix_test.go`) pin every Oracle R1 probe `P-MF1-a`〜`P-OBS1`; every new/changed small function measures `cognitive < 35` under `cccc --no-config --no-cache`, and the two extracted red-line functions DROP (`screenFindArgsSeg` 76→28, `stdinProgramTargetsWithEnv` 169→22).
- **Role guards: close the `v6.1.1` Oracle-audit guard hardening backlog — Python `--` long options unified into one grammar, the JavaScript structural family split per runtime, and bounded recursive descent through xargs/flock/watch/find exec slots (`role_guards.go`).**
  - **Python `--` long options (`interpCodeSlots`, `unwrapInterpreterInline`, `pythonModuleArgvFrom`, `pythonModuleHit`, `screenInterpFileRun`, `hasDynamicInterpOption`).** Python's option grammar is now centralized in `interpOptSpec`/`lookupInterpOptSpec`: the glued `-c`/`-m` cluster model, the `-W`/`-X` value letters, the legal long options (`--help`/`--help-env`/`--help-xoptions`/`--help-all`/`--version` zero-arg queries plus `--check-hash-based-pycs` with a statically valid `default`/`always`/`never` mode in either `=mode` or separate-operand form), and a strict fail-closed rule for every other `--` word — its operand arity is statically unknowable (e.g. PyPy's `--jit off`), so an unknown long option yields an open `flagOnly` code slot that `xargsFeedsCodeFlag`/`replTokenInExecSlot` block and `hasDynamicInterpOption` reports as a dynamic payload. Before this change `python3 --check-hash-based-pycs=default -c '…pig…'` mis-scanned the long option as a short cluster and treated `heck-hash-based-pycs=default` as the `-c` payload, letting the real code escape direct-execution, `find -exec` and `xargs` screens; the space-separated form let `"default"` end the scan early so a trailing `-c`/`-m` was never seen; and `python3 -um pig` / `python3 --check-hash-based-pycs default -m pig` escaped the `-m` module screen entirely. CPython's stop-at-first-`-c`/`-m` semantics is modeled via `stopOnFirstCode` without dropping a later real `-c`/`-m` that still sits inside argv. Legitimate query spellings (`python3 --version`, `--help-all`, `--check-hash-based-pycs=default --version`) and post-script operands (`python3 app.py --custom-flag val`) stay allowed.
  - **JavaScript/TypeScript structural family split (`structuralFamilyKey`, `sameStructuralFamily`, `lookupInterpOptSpec` bun/ts-node/tsx specs).** `node`/`nodejs` now form their own family while `bun`, `ts-node` and `tsx` are independent single-member families: a cross-name symlink (`bun -> node`, `tsx -> node`) is a conflicting structural alias and fails closed in `peelResolvedSegDepth`, `replTokenInExecSlot` and `xargsFeedsCodeFlag` — previously the shared `heredocInterpLang == "javascript"` let `bun -p` keep the typed bun head while node's `-p`/`--print` grammar never applied. The real runtimes' own inline-code spellings are modeled directly: `bun -p/--print/-e/--eval` plus its value options (`-r`/`-c`/`--require`/`--preload`/`--import`/`--loader`/`--cwd`/`--config`/`--env-file`), and `ts-node`/`tsx` `-e`/`-p`/`--eval`/`--print` plus `-r`/`-P`/`-O`/`--require`/`--loader`/`--import`/`--project`/`--compiler-options`/`--tsconfig` — so a direct `bun -p 'require("child_process").execSync("pig")'` blocks even without any symlink. The legitimate `node <-> nodejs` distro pair stays same-family.
  - **Bounded recursive descent through command indirection (`replTokenInExecSlotDepth`, `xargsFeedsCodeFlagDepth`, `peelExecSlotHead`, `scriptHasOpenCommandFlag`).** Both exec-slot screens now descend through `xargs` / `flock` / `watch` / `find -exec*` / `ssh` with an explicit depth budget: reaching `peelChainMaxDepth` (24) fails closed. `xargs -I{} flock -x /dev/null -c '{}'`, `find . -exec xargs sh -c {} \;`, `xargs -I{} watch -x sh -c '{}'`, `xargs watch -x sh -c` (open `-c` fed by stdin words), `xargs -I% find . -okdir flock /tmp/lk --command='%' \;`, `xargs -I{} script -qc '{}' /dev/null` and the dangling `xargs env` wrapper (stdin words become the command head itself) all fail closed, as do `env -S` split payloads naming the token (`xargs -I{} env -S 'perl {}'` re-splits after substitution) and nested `hc`/`herdr-core`/ctxmode heads inside descended layers. Data-operand placeholders stay open: `find . -exec flock /tmp/lk grep foo {} \;`, `xargs -I{} watch -x grep foo {}` and `xargs -I{} find {} -maxdepth 1 -name '*.go'` remain allowed. Regression coverage: `TestGuard_V622_PythonLongOpts`, `TestGuard_V622_JSFamilySplit`, `TestGuard_V622_MultiLayerExecSlots` (`zz_r2fix_test.go`), all constructed from baseline `6f6a61d` entry points with zero new-symbol dependencies so the verifier can check them out onto the baseline for the FAIL→PASS proof; every new/changed function measures `cognitive < 35` under `cccc --no-config --no-cache`.

## [6.2.1] - 2026-11-24

### Fixed
- Clarified the `ctx_run` read-only tool description so strict read-only and sub-agent callers accurately see which `hc` subcommands are permitted (`hc send <addr> <text>`, `--help`/`-h`, `--version`/`-V`, and `hc <sub> --help`/`-h`); other verbs such as `ls`, `get`, `read`, `wait`, and `attach` remain denied.

### Changed
- Redesigned `README.md` with light/dark theme logo support, a quick-start guide, tool overview, and collapsible security/development sections, and added a matching Simplified Chinese translation (`README.zh-CN.md`).

## [6.2.0] - 2026-11-22

### Fixed
- **Restore the non-`sbx` SSH/PATH regression assertions dropped with `TestGuard_PeelSbxWrapper` (`readonly_allowlist_test.go`).** The peel-test deletion also removed five still-applicable assertions that only depend on the SSH PATH alias: the `xargs ssx dmit sh` piped-stdin tunnel, the `xargs ssx dmit sh -c` open code slot, `find -exec ssx dmit sh -c {} ;`, and the two stdin-probe PATH seeding boundaries (a remote `PATH=` op must not leak into the local resolution base, and `PATH=$PATH:<dir>` self-reference must not collapse). `TestGuard_SSHPathAliasRegressions` re-pins them verbatim under a neutral name with a fixture that plants ONLY `ssx -> /usr/bin/ssh` — no `sbx`/`foo` stand-in and no `PI_SANDBOX` logic returns.

### Removed
- **The `sbx` sandbox integration: all `sbx`-specific guard logic, env rules, test stand-ins and documentation (`role_guards.go`, `readonly_allowlist.go`, `hc_gate.go`, `hc_prepare.go`, `git_tools.go`, `scripts/ns-hc-gotest.sh`, `scripts/ns-hc-isolate.sh`, `audit_test.go`, `ns_hc_scripts_test.go`, `readonly_allowlist_test.go`, `role_guards_test.go`, `role_guards_rt1111_test.go`, `zz_rtaudit_test.go`, `zz_r2fix_test.go`, `hc_prepare_diff_test.go`, `shell_ast_red_test.go`, `AGENTS.md`).** `peelWrappers` drops the `sbx` peel branch and the `screenSbxEnvValue`/`--env` comma-split screen; `peelWrappersAndSbxAliases` is renamed `peelWrappersAndWrapperAliases` and only re-peels heads resolving to real wrapper bases (env/timeout/…). `hcGateUnsafePeelBase`, `isPeeledWrapperBase`, `isStructuralGuardHead`, `sameStructuralFamily`, `isAllowedSystemReadonlyAlias`, `readonlyCanonicalHeads`, `findTrustedHcExecutable` and `readonlyPrePeelHitExempt` all drop the `sbx` arm/name. `envHookVars`/`isEnvHookVar` no longer screen `PI_SANDBOX_INTERNAL_CHILD_SPEC`, `PI_SANDBOX_GO_EXEC_DIR` or the `PI_SANDBOX_*` prefix (the sandbox that consumed them is gone). The `ns-hc-*` isolation scripts and `TestMain` outer gate enumerate/mask only `hc`/`herdr-core`; the FIFO enumeration regression now plants `hc` instead of the deleted third name. `AGENTS.md` drops the `go/sandbox` sync invariant and the third-name stand-in description; the `TestGuard_PeelSbxWrapper`/`mustSbxBin` fixture is removed and the generic `nice -> sbx` provenance-surrender test re-targets `nice -> exec` (an unsafe peel base that still exists).

## [6.1.1] - 2026-11-22

### Fixed
- **Per-request working directory isolation in role guards**: Replaced shared global working-directory state with immutable per-call state, preventing concurrent `ctx_run` requests from polluting each other's path resolution.
- **Hardened directory-change and `$(pwd)` tracking**: Propagated `env -C` / `--chdir` directory changes across wrapper peeling, stdin program detection, and `xargs` / `find -exec` screening (failing closed on empty or dynamic operands), and tightened `$(pwd)` / `PWD` / `OLDPWD` taint tracking on directory switches and dynamic commands.
- **Hardened path-form and symlink-alias screening**: Blocked relative dot-path `hc` invocations and non-system toolchain symlink shadows, and ensured non-`ssh` alias heads in `xargs`, `find -exec`, and stdin pipelines are screened under both typed and resolved names.
- **Order-independent multi-target shell preparation**: Fixed multi-target `bash -c` and shell stdin preparation so evaluation order cannot alter rewrite or blocking decisions.

## [6.1.0] - 2026-11-20

### Added
- **Read-only `cccc` support in `ctx_run`**: Admitted `cccc` to the strict read-only allowlist (including over SSH) for `--help` / `--version` queries and analysis runs that pass both `--no-cache` and `--no-config` with safe, whitelisted formatting, filtering, and threshold options. Disallowed or write-capable flags (`--cache`, `--cache-file`, `--config`, `--print-cache-file`, and unknown options) fail closed.

### Changed
- **Clarified `ctx_report` guidance**: Updated tool descriptions and server instructions to state that dispatch bindings (`DISPATCH_REPORT_*`) are read server-side and the output path and receipt are returned directly, removing misleading hints to inspect environment variables beforehand.
- **Clarified `ctx_git` and `ctx_fs` schemas**: Documented `ctx_git` field scopes (`diff`-only vs. `log`-only flags, `n` > `limit` > `max_count` precedence, and literal single-pathspec handling) and clarified that `ctx_fs` only accesses mounted workspace directories.

## [6.0.0] - 2026-11-17

### Breaking Changes
- **`ctx_report` is now the sole sub-agent report delivery tool**: Removed `dispatch-report` from the `ctx_run` read-only allowlist and blocked all `dispatch-report` invocations (including `execute`, `execute_file`, `batch`, `run_task`, and interpreter/package-runner spawns) for role-guarded sub-agents. Use `ctx_report(content="...")` instead (`hc send <parent> <msg>` remains as a short-notice fallback if `ctx_report` errors). The main agent is unaffected.
- **Upgrade prerequisites**: Requires `dispatch-agent` v0.9.0 or newer, an updated Pi extension exposing `ctx_report`, and draining pre-upgrade in-flight role sessions before upgrading.

### Changed
- **Unified report-delivery error hints and spawn detection**: Updated blocked-command hints across all `ctx_run` actions (including `execute_file`, `custom` `run_task`, and inline code execution) to point callers to `ctx_report`, and extended process-spawn detection to cover C#, Go, Rust, and Elixir launch APIs.

## [5.6.0] - 2026-11-17

### Added
- **`ctx_report` MCP tool**: Added a dedicated seventh MCP tool (also exposed in the Pi extension) for delivering dispatch reports via the trusted system `dispatch-report` helper, with live `DISPATCH_REPORT_*` validation, 1 B–1 MiB UTF-8 body limits, and full delivery-receipt reporting.

### Changed
- **Trusted path canonicalization in role guards**: Allowed absolute paths in `/usr/local/bin/` and `~/.local/bin/` for `dispatch-report` and `hc` when strict read-only guards are active, while rejecting untrusted path-form executables across all sub-agent roles.
- **Actionable report-delivery hints**: Guard errors on blocked `dispatch-report` invocations now include a self-heal hint pointing callers to `ctx_report`.

### Fixed
- **AST-based `--hc-gate` rewrite and execution**: Sub-agent and strict read-only `ctx_run` paths now rewrite eligible dynamic `hc send` calls into `ctxmode --hc-gate`, enforcing UTF-8, control-character, and sensitive-content checks before invoking the trusted `herdr-core` binary directly with no `PATH` fallback.
- **Hardened shell, wrapper, and multi-target gate screening**:
  - Restricted `--hc-gate` authorization strictly to AST-verified rewrites across a single shell `-c` or local shell stdin stream, rejecting raw or dynamic `--hc-gate` flags, glued `-c` flags (e.g., `-ce`), unsafe wrappers (`env -S`, `env -i`), and remote SSH or non-shell stdin targets.
  - Audited multi-target shell commands under each target's own `PATH` configuration and ensured `batch` fails closed if a prepared script is missing.
  - Blocked `hc` / `herdr-core` invocations nested inside `xargs`, `find -exec`, and top-level interpreter scripts, as well as multi-word array/parameter expansions in guarded options.
- **Background zombie process cleanup**: Ignored zombie (`Z`) and dead (`X`) child states when checking background process groups, preventing orphaned zombie children from causing spurious cleanup failures.

## [5.5.0] - 2026-11-16

### Changed
- **`githooks/commit-msg`: non-ASCII check no longer depends on PCRE.** The English-only gate now detects leftover bytes after `LC_ALL=C tr -d '\001-\177'` instead of `grep -qP '[^\x00-\x7F]'` — an equivalent-on-valid-inputs non-ASCII-byte test that is stricter on malformed non-ASCII byte sequences, with zero PCRE dependency, so the hook also works where `grep -P` is unavailable. Error text, exit code, and the accept/reject set (newline/tab/printable ASCII pass; multi-byte/UTF-8 rejected) are unchanged.
- **`AGENTS.md`: cross-repo protocol lockstep invariants pinned under `## 关联仓库`.** The `dispatch-agent` entry now lists the four hardcoded protocol invariants both repos implement independently and must change in lockstep: the report root `/tmp/herdr-role-reports`, the parent-terminal address shape `^t[0-9a-z]+$` (`hcSendAddrOK` / `config.ReportAddrRe`), the sole role-side spelling `dispatch-report <t-addr>` (`--flush` reserved for the dispatcher), and the ack line `报告已写入 path=<abs> bytes=<n> sha256=<hex>` (empty `CTXMODE_EXTRA_WORKDIRS` counts as unset).
- **`.agents/notes/`: stale observation-shard cleanup.** Removed pure audit-residual/follow-up shard files (`20261007-175eb05-audit-residuals.md`, `20261009-ctxstats-keptout-residuals.md`, `2026-11-04-ssh-peel-followups.md`, `20261109-v531-hc-allowlist-followup.md`) and stripped the discharged `观察项登记` sections plus the `[pattern:...]` frequency-statistics block from `20261115-report-channel-security-r1.md`; per the zero-inbox rule, closed/discharged audit-residual shards and observation sections are removed while core architecture decisions, fix facts and explicit non-goal records are kept; discharged observation items remain in Git history rather than in-tree copies, and historical CHANGELOG entries are left unrewritten.
- **Orphan `ctxmode_*` temp-file reap threshold raised from 24h to 7 days (`executor.go`).** The init-time sweep deletes unprotected `ctxmode_*` files in `os.TempDir()` only when their mtime is older than `orphanTempReapTTL = 7 * 24 * time.Hour` (was `24 * time.Hour`), so a 1–6-day-old leftover is no longer treated as a dead-process orphan. The sweep body moved verbatim into `sweepOrphanTempFiles` so the boundary is unit-testable under an isolated `TMPDIR`. Regression coverage: `TestSweepOrphanTempFiles_TTLBoundary` (`executor_reap_test.go`) asserts under-TTL retention (was reaped under the old 24h threshold), over-TTL reaping and protected-file retention.

### Fixed
- **`githooks_test.go`: `hookUsesGrepP` no longer misses merged short options.** The guard used to match only the literal substring `grep -P`, so `grep -qP`/`grep -Pq` (and `--perl-regexp`) went undetected — including the real `grep -qP` the commit-msg hook carried. It now reports a bare `grep` word followed by an option containing `P` in a single-dash group or `--perl-regexp` on non-comment lines; `-e` pattern operands, comments, `egrep`/`fgrep` and non-PCRE options (`-qE`, `-F`) do not count. Regression: `TestHookUsesGrepP`.
- **CI: fix stale `TestCommitMsgHook` expectation for Chinese subjects (`githooks_test.go`).** The `chinese subject in conventional commit passes` subtest predated the English-only commit-message rule and expected a Chinese subject to pass; the hook now rejects non-ASCII messages, so the run failed (`TestCommitMsgHook/chinese_subject_in_conventional_commit_passes`, run 43712658279). The subtest now expects the message to be rejected, matching the existing `non-ASCII message is rejected` case.
- **Baseline fixes unblocking `make check`: per-test process isolation and linear fetch chunking (`executor_reap_test.go`, `executor_kill_test.go`, `fetch.go`, `main.go`, `fetch_test.go`).**
  - Reaper/kill tests no longer run global `pkill -f` on `"sleep 120"`/`"sleep 37"`/`"slee[p] 120"` or `/proc` cmdline matches on bare `sleep` names: each test now launches its sleepers through a unique `t.TempDir()` symlink (`newTestSleeper`, e.g. `.../ctxmode-test-sleeper`) to the system sleep binary, and cleanup (`killTestSleepers`) plus `waitForNoProc` match only that unique path — concurrent sessions and foreign `sleep` processes can no longer be killed by or fail the suite.
