The process-tree/session/pgid/stdio topology of the commands under test is unchanged; production reaping logic is untouched. `TestFindProcCmdline_IgnoresForeignSleeper` pins the isolation: a foreign `sleep` is never matched by the test marker.
  - `splitLongText` (`fetch.go`) no longer calls `truncateUTF8` on the remaining string per cut — an O(n²) rescan on multi-megabyte single-line bodies. Each cut is now a bounded rune-boundary back-off of at most `utf8.UTFMax-1` bytes (O(1) per cut, O(n) total), keeping lossless reassembly and the 1-byte invalid-input progress guarantee — on invalid UTF-8 at the window tail the cut simply backs off the bounded run and, if the whole window is continuation bytes, emits the first byte verbatim (each piece still ≤ maxBytes). `truncateUTF8` (`main.go`) only validates the `s[:maxBytes]` window instead of the whole string, so its cost is O(maxBytes); truncation-boundary semantics (no half runes, longest valid prefix on mid-window invalid bytes) are unchanged, and bytes beyond the window are returned untouched — a RuneError at the window tail still walks back at most `utf8.UTFMax-1` bytes per DecodeLastRune step.
  - `indexContentLocked` (`fetch.go`) drops the duplicate per-chunk `checkSensitiveContent` loop: the full-content check already refused the payload and `Store.ReplaceExactAndChunks` re-checks every chunk before any mutation (prior to opening its transaction), so the middle scan was pure duplication doubling the pattern cost on 10MB fetches. The sensitive-content gate is unchanged.
  - Regression: `TestSplitLongText_LargeSingleLine` (10MB single line, ≤30s bound, lossless join). Under `-race`, `TestToolFetchAndIndex_ReportsBodyTruncated` dropped from >300s (hung on the quadratic scan) to ~227s; the residual cost is single-transaction FTS5 trigram indexing of 2622 chunk rows — orthogonal schema territory, not touched here.

### Security
- **Role guards: claude-family nested-agent names, `dispatch-report` allowlist entry, and report/socket env-hook interception (`role_guards.go`, `readonly_allowlist.go`).**
  - `blockedAgentExecutableList` gains `claude`, `claude-code`, `claude-pty`, `codex`; a new `isBlockedAgentHead` adds the len≥5 prefix-derivative rule — `stem-…`/`stem.…` spellings (`claude-x`, `codex-cli`, `dispatch-agent-lite`) block as the launcher itself while short stems (`pig-*`, `su-*`, `at.*`) and look-alikes (`clauder`, `piglet`) stay exact-match. The derivative check runs in `screenNestedDispatch`'s default branch so a resolved alias the switch owns (`hc -> herdr-core`) is not re-blocked by a coincidental stem match; npm/npx package-spec checks and the conflicting-structural-alias screen use it too. `codeAgentNames` derives from the list as before, so unresolved code spawns naming `claude`/`codex`/`claude-code` also block.
  - `readonlyAllowlist["dispatch-report"]` admits exactly `dispatch-report <t-addr>` (two static words, `hcSendAddrOK` operand) plus `--help`/`-h`/`--version`/`-V`; every other spelling fails closed for strict read-only and for sub-agent callers (`screenNestedDispatch` case).
  - `envHookVars` gains `DISPATCH_REPORT_PATH`, `PI_SANDBOX_GO_EXEC_DIR`, `HC_TERMINAL_ID`, `HC_SOCKET` — KEY=VAL prefixes, `env` wrappers, `export`/`declare`/`typeset`/`local`/`readonly`, `for`-loop iter vars, `unset`, arithmetic/parameter-expansion writes and ctx_run `args.env` all block (the existing `isEnvHookVar` fan-out reaches `shell_ast.go`'s hook checks unchanged).
  - New `codeEnvOverrideHit` (`pyEnvOverrideHit`/`jsEnvOverrideHit`) screens Python/JS env-rewrite paths under the sub-agent guard: `os.environ['K']=v`, `os.putenv`, `environ.update`/`setdefault`, `process.env.K=v`, `process.env['K']=v`, `Object.assign(process.env, …)`. Static keys are judged by `envOverrideKeyVerdict` (hook/taint/sentinel names block, benign names pass); unverifiable dynamic keys fail closed.
- **Report-channel hardening round 2 (`role_guards.go`, `executor.go`, `main.go`, `instructions.go`, `role_guards_test.go`, `main_test.go`).**
  - `maskAssignAfterBracket`/`maskAssignOpAt` now recognise EVERY compound write operator after a subscript or JS dot member — `+=`, `-=`, `*=`, `/=`, `//=`, `%=`, `**=`, `<<=`, `>>=`, `>>>=`, `&=`, `|=`, `^=`, `@=`, `&&=`, `||=`, `??=` — where the old check matched only a bare `=`, so `os.environ['DISPATCH_REPORT_PATH']+='/x'` / `process.env.HC_SOCKET+='/x'` silently wrote protected env vars past the guard. Comparisons (`==`, `!=`, `<=`, `>=`, `===`, `??` alone) remain reads. Regression: `TestGuard_CompoundEnvOverride`.
  - **Lexical env screen whitespace/continuation closure (oracle `oracle-ctxmode-r1` M1/S1, `oracle-ctxmode-r2` S-A/S-B):** `skipMaskSpace` now crosses `' '`, `'\t'`, `'\r'`, `'\n'`, `'\v'`, `'\f'` and Python backslash line continuations (`\` + optional blanks + `\n`; `pyLineContBefore` accepts the same blank set), so writes split across physical lines can no longer slip the screen: `os.environ['HC_SOCKET'] \\\n = '/x'`, `(os.environ['K']\n) += '/x'` (the `]`-close path now also skips outer `)` before the write op), `os.environ\n.update({…})`/`environ\n.pop('K')`/`environ\n.clear()` (new `pyEnvironWriteMethodAfter` covers `update`/`setdefault`/`__setitem__`/`pop`/`popitem`/`clear`; `environ.get` stays a read), `process\n.env\n.\nHC_SOCKET\n+='/x'`, `process.env['K']\n='/x'`, `(process.env.K)\n='/x'`, `delete process.env.\nHC_SOCKET`, and `del (\nos.environ['K']\n)`/`del \\\nos.environ['K']` (the del screen now walks statement boundaries with bracket depth so newlines inside an open group or behind `\\` stay inside the `del` statement). The `Object.assign`/`Object.defineProperty`/`Reflect.set` call sinks now match via `hasMaskQualCall` — receiver and method with identifier edges and mask whitespace (`' '`–`'\f'` incl. `\v`/`\f`) around the dot — so `Object .assign(process.env,…)` and `Object\n.assign(process.env,…)` block like the glued spelling. `jsDeleteEnvHit` no longer fires on a `delete` identifier that follows a `.` after mask whitespace: `api.delete\n<newline stmt>` is a member name, not the unary operator, while a genuine cross-line `delete\nprocess.env.K` still blocks. `unquoteCodeKey` returns `("",false)` for a backtick template containing `${` — ``process.env[`K${x}`]=v`` fails closed as `env override dynamic` while a static `` `FOO` `` still extracts `FOO`. The screen still covers only direct `environ`/`process.env` key writes and the known write methods — receiver aliasing (`e=os.environ; e['K']=v`), bracket-receiver spellings (`process['env'].K=v`), whole-object rebinding and non-env `__dict__` manipulation remain out of scope (same-uid lexical screen, not a sandbox).
  - **Lexical env screen round-3 closure (oracle `oracle-ctxmode-r3` F1/F2/F3):** `jsDeleteEnvHit`'s member-access exclusion now only shields an IMMEDIATELY adjacent `.` — `x.delete` is still a member name, but `let q=1.\ndelete process.env.DISPATCH_REPORT_PATH` is a numeric literal followed by a real unary `delete` statement and blocks (the previous cross-whitespace `.` scan let it through). New `maskDestructAssignTarget` screens destructuring element writes: `[process.env.HC_SOCKET]=['/x']`, `({p:process.env.HC_SOCKET}={p:'/x'})`, `[a,process.env.HC_SOCKET]=[1,'/x']`, `[os.environ['HC_SOCKET']]=['/x']` and the Python bare-tuple unpack `os.environ['HC_SOCKET'], a = '/x', 1` all write the env slot through the target literal — the receiver's own subscript/member never takes a direct `=` so the old check stayed silent. Operand-position negatives stay free: `d[os.environ['K']]=1`, `o[process.env.K]=1`, `const o={p:process.env.K}`, `const a=[process.env.K]` and JS comma constructs (`const a=process.env.K, b=1` is a multi-declarator read, never a destructuring target; the bare-tuple shape is Python-only). Env-clear sinks close out: `os.unsetenv('K')`, `os.environ.__delitem__('K')` and `Reflect.deleteProperty(process.env,'K')` join `del environ['K']`/`delete process.env.K` as screened clears. The screen still covers only direct `environ`/`process.env` writes and the listed primitives — receiver aliasing, bracket-receiver spellings (`process['env'].K=v`, `Object['assign'](process.env, …)`), non-`=` binding targets (`for (… of/in …)`, `with … as …` — the loop/with head binds the env receiver without a plain `=`), whole-object rebinding and non-env `__dict__` manipulation remain out of scope (same-uid lexical screen, not a sandbox).
  - **Lexical env screen r5b mixed-tuple/statement-head closure (main-branch review):** `maskDestructAssignTarget` now closes two boundary gaps — a peeled wrapper followed by `,` instead of `=` (`[os.environ['HC_SOCKET']], a = ['/x'], 1` writes the env slot through the bare-tuple unpack; new `pyBareTupleAssignAfter` finds the depth-0 `=` after the comma, Python only), and `[…]`/`(…)` openers that used to pass the operand-position check behind `)`, `]` or an identifier — statement-head `if (1) [K]=v` bodies, `if (0); else [K]=v` (keyword `else` now maps to operand head like `do`/`return`/`throw`/`del`/`not`/…), and the JS ASI line break `let x\n[K]=v` (a `\n` before `[` starts a new statement there, never subscripts) all destructure-write. `(…) = rhs` groups are targets, never calls. Call-argument and subscript reads stay free: `o[process.env.K]=1`, `d[os.environ['K']]=1`, `[String(process.env.K), a]=[…]`, `fn()[process.env.K]=1`, `a[0][K]`.
  - **Lexical env screen r6 closure (oracle `oracle-ctxmode-r4` S1/O4):** Python's byte-keyed environment view `os.environb` is now screened to the same standard as `os.environ` — the receiver match tries `environb` first (longer prefix, `envLen=8`) then `environ` (`envLen=7`) with an identifier right-boundary check (`environment` stays out), and `unquoteCodeKey` peels Python non-interpolated literal prefixes (`b`/`B`, `r`/`R`, `u`/`U` and `br`/`bR`/`Br`/`BR`/`rb`/`rB`/`Rb`/`RB`) only before a `'`/`"` quote — never an `f`/`F`/`fr`/`rf` interpolation prefix and never before a backtick (a JS tagged template `tag\`...\`` is not a static key), while escapes like `\x48` still fail closed. `os.environb[b'HC_SOCKET']=b'/x'`, `[os.environb[b'HC_SOCKET']]=[b'/x']`, `del os.environb[b'DISPATCH_REPORT_PATH']` and `os.environb.pop(b'HC_SOCKET')` (plus `environb.update`/`setdefault`/`__setitem__`/`__delitem__` primitives) all block like their `os.environ` spellings; a benign `os.environb[b'FOO']=b'bar'` stays free. Two more JS qualified write primitives join the sink list: `Reflect.defineProperty(process.env,'K',{value:v})` and `Object.defineProperties(process.env,{K:{value:v}})` write env members through descriptor objects and now block like `Object.assign`/`Reflect.set`. The screen still covers only Python and JS/TS direct `environ`/`environb`/`process.env` writes — non-Python/JS languages (`ruby`/`perl`/`php` — strict read-only roles already block those interpreters wholesale via `strictReadonlyBlockLegacyScripting`), a JS non-strict `with (process.env) { K = v }` scope-chain write, receiver aliasing, bracket-receiver spellings, whole-object rebinding and non-env `__dict__` manipulation remain out of scope (same-uid lexical screen, not a sandbox).
  - `envHookVars` gains `DISPATCH_REPORT_PARENT` and `CTXMODE_EXTRA_WORKDIRS`; `isDeniedEnvKeyForCaller` denies the full report-channel set (`DISPATCH_REPORT_PATH`, `DISPATCH_REPORT_PARENT`, `CTXMODE_EXTRA_WORKDIRS`) for role-guarded `ctx_run` `args.env` calls so the parent-id binding and the extra-workdir list cannot be re-targeted through the allowlisted env channel either. For an UNGUARDED caller only `CTXMODE_EXTRA_WORKDIRS` continues through `filterExecEnv`'s `CTXMODE_` prefix passthrough — `DISPATCH_REPORT_PATH`/`DISPATCH_REPORT_PARENT` are not on `envAllowlist` and are refused there; the dispatcher itself exports them to children outside `ctx_run args.env`. `screenDispatchReportReadonly` keeps `dispatch-report <t-addr>` as the only role spelling — `--flush` is a dispatcher-side verb and stays blocked (paired with the dispatch-agent same-batch `DISPATCH_REPORT_PARENT`/`--flush`/`^t[0-9a-z]+$` implementation). Extended coverage in `TestGuard_EnvHookReportVars` / `TestGuard_DispatchReportReadonly`.
  - `-workdir` is now repeatable (`stringListFlag`): the first explicit value stays the primary workdir (its `<sha8>-<base>` database key is unchanged for a single `-workdir`), later values extend the workspace set, and the existing ordered dedup is unchanged — previously a repeated `-workdir` was last-wins, silently dropping every earlier root (the Claude MCP config emits repeated `-workdir`s and lost all but the last). Historical repeated-`-workdir` configurations shift primary from last to first, so their database directory changes from `<sha8(last)>-<base>` to `<sha8(first)>-<base>`; the old directory is left on disk and is not migrated automatically, and there is no startup hint about the switch (when `CTXMODE_DB` is unset — an explicit `CTXMODE_DB` still pins one database for every workdir). An empty `-workdir ""` value is skipped like the old last-wins semantics ignored it, never absolutized into a workspace root. New `CTXMODE_EXTRA_WORKDIRS` (JSON `[]string`) appends dispatcher-supplied extra dirs after flag/config roots; each entry must be an absolute normalized path and is rejected when it is `/`, bare `/tmp`, `/var/tmp` or inside the `/tmp/herdr-role-reports` report tree (checked again after symlink resolution); an invalid value fails startup, never a silent skip. The narrow-root deny applies ONLY to `CTXMODE_EXTRA_WORKDIRS` startup vetting — `-workdir` is the host's explicitly trusted configuration and is not subject to the denylist. Regression: `TestCLI_WorkdirFlagsAndExtraEnv` (real CLI `index`/`search` probes).
  - `instructions.go` documents the single report delivery entry (`argv=["dispatch-report","<t-id>"]` + stdin body, no flags/retries) and the `报告已写入 path=/bytes=/sha256=` notify line (matching the dispatch-agent helper) the parent compares against; `.sent` is dispatcher-side dedup only.

## [5.4.0] - 2026-11-13

### Changed
- **`make check`: raise the `go test` watchdog to `-timeout 30m` (`Makefile`).** On this arm64 dev host the full-package `go test -race ./...` run takes ~674s, over Go's default 600s test watchdog, so the gate timed out before the suite finished; the `check` recipe now passes `-timeout 30m`. No test logic or assertions changed.

### Fixed
- **CI: skip local-only audit probe tests when their fixtures are absent (`zz_r7adv_test.go`, `zz_r7reg_test.go`, `zz_r8adv_test.go`, `zz_r8legacy_test.go`, `zz_r9adv_test.go`, `zz_r10adv_test.go`).** The R7/R8/R9/R10 adversarial probes read fixture files under `/root/r7in`, `/root/r8in`, `/root/r9in`, `/root/r10in`, which exist only on the dev host; on the GitHub runner `/root` is unreadable, so `TestR7Adv`, `TestR7Reg`, `TestR8Adv`, `TestR8Legacy`, `TestR9Adv` and `TestR10Adv` failed with `permission denied` (run 43388728182). A missing fixture now skips the test (`t.Skip`, the same pattern `zz_rtaudit_test.go` already uses) instead of failing it; when the fixtures are present the probes run unchanged.
- **MCP `tools/call`: tolerate JSON-string-encoded `params.arguments` (`main.go`, new `mcp_argunwrap.go`).** Some LLM hosts deliver `arguments` as a JSON-encoded *string* instead of the object the MCP spec requires — observed in the wild as both `"{\"a\":1}"` (one extra string layer, from `call_mcp_tool`-style lazy tool bridges whose `Arguments` param is declared `type: string`) and `"\"{\\\"a\\\":1}\""` (two layers, produced when the upstream model double-stringifies). The SDK's wire-level unmarshal into `CallToolParamsRaw` accepts either (its `Arguments` is `json.RawMessage`), so the request only failed later inside the tool's own `map[string]any` unmarshal (`json: cannot unmarshal string into Go value of type map[string]interface {}`). `srv.AddReceivingMiddleware(argUnwrapMiddleware)` now rewrites `params.arguments` before tool dispatch: `unwrapArgString` decodes while the value is a JSON string that itself parses as JSON (cap `maxArgUnwrapDepth` = 3, logged to stderr when triggered), leaves non-string and non-JSON-string values untouched so downstream still reports the real type error, and only runs for `tools/call`. Regression coverage: `mcp_argunwrap_test.go` — `TestUnwrapArgString` (object/1-layer/2-layer/plain-string/non-JSON-string/number/null/array passthrough matrix), `TestUnwrapArgStringDepthCap` (4 layers leaves a string after the cap), `TestArgUnwrapMiddleware` (end-to-end params rewrite visible to the handler), `TestArgUnwrapMiddlewareOtherMethods`.
- **Stdio transport: drain in-flight responses before reporting stdin EOF (`main.go`, new `stdio_drain.go`, `[pattern:mcp-cli-immediate-eof-race]`).** A CLI client that writes its request(s) and closes stdin immediately raced the SDK: `ioConn`'s decoder goroutine could deliver `io.EOF` to `readIncoming` before the in-flight handler wrote its response, so the response write hit `shuttingDown`, failed with `ErrServerClosing` ("server is closing: EOF"), `conn.Wait()` surfaced the write error, `srv.Run` returned non-nil and the process exited rc=1 — usually without the response reaching stdout at all. `main` now serves through a stock `mcp.IOTransport` whose byte streams are wrapped by a drain pair (NOT a `mcp.Connection` wrapper, which would break `Server.bind`'s unexported `serverConnection` type assertion on `*ioConn`): `stdinGate` line-buffers stdin and registers every inbound JSON-RPC request (non-empty `"method"` plus present non-null `"id"`; notifications are not tracked; batch arrays and concatenated values on one line are scanned per object, an unterminated tail line is completed with `\n` before EOF is deferred), while `stdoutTracker` passes writes through and scans completed outbound lines AFTER the write for responses (`"id"` present, no `"method"`; `"id":null` releases one outstanding request as a fallback). Once stdin reaches EOF the gate withholds `(0, io.EOF)` until the in-flight count returns to zero — bounded by `stdioDrainMaxWait` (5s), `ctx` cancellation, and `Close` — so `ioConn` only sees EOF after every response byte is on the wire and `srv.Run` exits cleanly. `deploy.sh` drops the `; sleep 1` workaround only in the new-binary verify pipeline (the `$TMP_FILE` it checks already contains this fix); the rollback verify keeps the sleep because `$PREV` is the pre-deploy backup binary which may predate the drain fix and still needs the workaround to avoid the EOF race (bounded 3-attempt retries kept as a safety net in both). Regression coverage, stated separately: (a) baseline control — at `76c042c` the test file does not compile (`undefined: newStdioDrainTransport`), so a same-tree red→green claim does not apply; (b) `TestStdioDrain_BaselineReproducesRace` is a negative control that exercises the ungated `mcp.IOTransport` and passes only when the old path fails with `server is closing` (it `t.Skipf`s if the race does not reproduce that run) — it pins the failure mechanism and is not the fix gate; (c) the three positive fix-path tests `TestStdioDrain_NoTrailingNewlineInitialize`, `TestStdioDrain_ConcurrentInitializeImmediateEOF` (×50 subruns) and `TestStdioDrain_PipelinedImmediateEOF` pass on this snapshot, 12 `--- PASS` lines under `go test -race -count=3` across the drain test file; (d) an entrypoint-level dual-snapshot comparison — 50 initialize pipes with no sleep: the old entrypoint failed 24/50 with rc=1 (`server is closing: EOF`), the new entrypoint went 50/50 rc=0 with a complete `serverInfo` response, plus 10/10 for the no-trailing-newline form. The drain hold remains bounded by `stdioDrainMaxWait` (5s), `ctx` cancellation and `Close`; the bounded samples above do not establish that no response can ever be lost under arbitrary inputs, timeouts or cancellation.
- **Fetch: recheck TTL cache inside `singleflight` and preserve force refetch (`fetch.go`, `e58dd93`).** Concurrent identical fetches share one in-flight result via singleflight; the Do body re-checks a still-fresh cache entry before performing network work, and a `force` refetch that would otherwise join a non-force shared result uses a distinct `|force` key so force semantics stay intact. Index refill / refuse-then-delete-cache behavior from the R13 fetch port is retained. Scope is the fetch cache path only.

### Security
- **Role guards: rt1111 red-team batch — 12 findings fixed (`role_guards.go`, `readonly_allowlist.go`).**
  - A-1: `isPureRemoteStdinVehicle` now only exempts a pure remote SHELL stdin vehicle (`sh`), not `node -`/`python3 -`; `screenSSHHead` blocks piped stdin and `StrictHostKeyChecking=no|accept-new` without `UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null|none` for read-only callers (local known_hosts write + file exfiltration); `guardCtxRunCode` counts a raw `child_process` identifier (JS/TS) and a masked `open` identifier without an `open(` call site (Python `f = open` rebind) as spawn/write evidence.
  - A-2: `journalctl --lines` requires the glued `--lines=N` form (new `longEqOnly` allowlist field); a bare `--lines` no longer smuggles `--rotate`/`--vacuum-size`. **BREAKING:** the bare `journalctl --lines 50` spelling (value as a separate word) moved from allow to block — migrate to `journalctl --lines=50` or `journalctl -n 50`.
  - A-3: `git tag`/`git branch` `--sort`/`--format` are display options only — they no longer mark the invocation a listing, so `git tag --sort refname pwned-tag` blocks as a create while operand-less queries stay free.
  - A-4: `hc send` rejects control characters (`\x00-\x08`, `\x0b-\x1f` incl. `\r`/`\x03`/`\x1b`, `\x7f`) in the text operand and any dynamic word in either operand (unquoted `$x` and double-quoted `"$x"`/`"$(…)"` alike); multiline `\n`/`\t` text stays free.
  - A-5: new `isStdinDevicePath` recognises `/dev/stdin`, `/dev/fd/0`, `/proc/self/fd/0`, `/proc/thread-self/fd/0` as stdin program sources for shells, interpreters, `awk -f` and `sed -f` (incl. glued/`--file=` forms).
  - A-6: `guardCtxRunStdinForCommand` scans EVERY segment — segments with their own stdin (pipe, `<`/`<<`/`<<<`) are skipped, and a clean stdin-program segment no longer ends the scan early (`python3 -; sh` + stdin screens the `sh` segment).
  - A-7: wrapper option/value slots fail closed on dynamic words (`timeout $(…)`, `nice -n $(…)`, `command -$x`, `watch -x -n $(…)`, `timeout "$T"`): `skipExecArgs`/`skipTimeoutArgs`/`skipStdbufArgs`/`skipIoniceArgs`/`skipTasksetArgs`/`skipChrtArgs`/`skipStraceArgs`/`skipWatchArgs`/`skipToolGlobalFlags` report a fail-closed flag instead of silently skipping the word; `stdinProgramHeadWithEnv` honours `peelWrappers`' `exhausted`. **BREAKING:** `timeout "$T" cmd` (a variable in the duration slot) moved from allow to block — resolve the duration to a literal first (compute `T` in an outer step and pass the number, e.g. `timeout 30 go test ./...`); every shell-expansion/variable form (`$T`, `${T:-30}`, `$(…)`) in the duration slot stays fail-closed, so the only viable spelling is a literal.
  - A-8: symlink alias handling rewritten — new `isPeeledWrapperBase` re-peels a resolved wrapper name (`myenv -> env`, `mytimeout -> timeout`), new `sameStructuralFamily` keeps same-family aliases (`sh -> dash`, `python3 -> python3.13`, `awk -> mawk`, `hc -> herdr-core|sbx`) while a cross-family structural alias (`python3 -> dash`, `sed -> env`) returns `conflicting structural alias`; `-c` payloads parse under the resolved shell dialect; `canonicalResolvedBase`'s `g`/`gnu` prefix strip is restricted to the real Homebrew GNU set (`gnuCanonicalSet`) so `gps`/`ghc` no longer alias to `ps`/`hc`, and typed `genv`/`gfind` normalize to `env`/`find`.
  - A-9: new `screenNodeRunnerSubagent` vets npx/bunx/pnpx — `-c`/`--call`, `-p`/`--package` package specs (version-stripped), and a blocked executable as the first positional all fail closed; `npx tsc`/`npx -y -p typescript tsc`/`bunx vitest` stay free.
  - A-10: `isEnvHookVar` adds `CTXMODE_AUDIT_LOG`, `CTXMODE_ENV_PASSTHROUGH`, `CTXMODE_DB`, `CTXMODE_CONFIG` — env prefixes, `export` and `sbx --env` can no longer rewrite a sub-agent's identity/audit path.
  - A-11: `herdr`/`herdsman` join `blockedAgentExecutableList` and their dedicated screens/whitelists are removed (dead code); every spelling now blocks on the bare name.
  - W-5: `screenPathHeadTrust` resolves relative-PATH hits through `EvalSymlinks` — a `.venv/bin`/`node_modules/.bin` symlink escaping the resolved cwd must land in a trusted system/linuxbrew/user-toolchain dir passing `isTrustedExecutableOnDisk`, else `untrusted path binary` (`pathIsUnsafeForSubagent` unchanged, so ordinary relative-PATH toolchains stay free).
  - R4 follow-ups (oracle second pass): `hasHcControlChars` now rejects the C1 set (U+0080–U+009F — 0x9B is ANSI CSI) on the decoded **rune** so legal multi-byte UTF-8 (continuation bytes ≥ 0x80) is untouched; `isStdinDevicePath` compares the trailing fd number **numerically**, closing the `fd/00`/`fd/000` zero-padded spellings that `filepath.Clean` does not fold while keeping `fd/1` free; `screenNestedDispatch` re-dispatches `npm`/`pnpm`/`yarn` `exec`/`dlx` package specs through the sub-agent whitelist and scans ALL non-flag operands (`npmFamilyExecArgs` skips value-taking option operands), closing `npm --prefix /tmp exec pig` — `npm install`/`run`/`view` and `npm exec tsc` stay free for a worker sub-agent.
  - R5 follow-ups (closure review): `npm exec`/`dlx` `-c`/`--call` (both glued `--call=…` and separate `-c …`/`--call …` spellings) now fails closed like `npx -c` — a glued `--call=…` previously dropped through the `--flag=value` rule and let `npm exec --call=pig` run unscreened; and the exec/dlx verb is now the FIRST non-flag operand only (new `npmFamilyVerbIndex`), so `npm view exec pig`'s `exec` reads as the `view` field name it is and the metadata query stays free.
  - R6 npm argv model (finite accept grammar): `parseNpmFamilyArgv` replaces `npmFamilyValueFlags`/`npmFamilyExecArgs`/`npmFamilyVerbIndex`/`npmFamilyExecSub` with one B/V/S option table per manager. The first positional locks the verb — only `exec`/`dlx` enter the exec state (manager options are consumed up front — each manager's option zone ends at its own boundary: npm's own `--` ends its zone even after a child word appeared, while pnpm/yarn's zone ends at the first child word — and once the zone ends every later word incl. `--` is child data passed through verbatim); `-c`/`--call`, every unlisted option (glued `--x=y` or short cluster letter), a separated `true`/`false` after a bare boolean, a missing/dynamic/`-`-leading option value and an exec with no child all Reject. The WHOLE exec child recurses through `peelResolvedSegDepth` at `depth+1` — `npm exec -- npm exec pig`, `npm exec -- npx pig` and `npm exec -- sh -c pig` are now screened, and the shared `peelChainMaxDepth` budget bounds `npm→npx→npm` chains; `screenNestedDispatch`/`screenNodeRunnerSubagent` take `(depth, caller)` and every call site passes them through, while `-p`/`--package` specs are checked against `blockedAgentExecutables` so `npm exec --package=pig -- tsc` cannot hide behind `tsc`. The strict-readonly twin path (`screenReadonlyWriteAlias`) reuses the same parser — a Reject fails closed instead of misreading an option value as the verb. **BREAKING:** unlisted npm/pnpm/yarn options now fail closed where they were silently skipped, so `npm --strict exec tsc` moved from allow to block — the accepted option set is the fixed table in `npmFamilyOptTable`/`npmFamilyExecOptTable` (`--tag`, `--prefix`, `--workspace`, `--registry`, `--cache`, `--userconfig`, `--globalconfig`, `--loglevel`, `--yes`, `--global`, `--parseable`, `--help`, `--version` for npm; `--dir`/`-C`, `--filter`/`-F`, `--workspace-root`/`-w`, `--recursive`/`-r`, `--help`, `--version` for pnpm; `--cwd`, `--help`, `--version` for yarn). Inside exec/dlx the package-spec option differs per manager: npm's `-p` stays the boolean `--parseable` (it is NOT a `--package` alias), pnpm has no short `-p` so `pnpm exec -p …` Rejects as an unlisted option and only `--package` takes a spec, while yarn's `-p` IS the `--package` spec taker. A `-`-leading word is refused only in a SEPARATED value slot (`--prefix -x` reads as a missing value and Rejects); a glued inline value (`--prefix=-x`) is taken verbatim by `takeValue` and stays free. Gate-review follow-ups: the child's FIRST positional is now version/tag-stripped like the `-p`/`--package` specs (`npm exec pig@latest`, `yarn dlx pig@latest`, `npm exec @scope/pig@1.2` hit the agent blacklist again — a regression versus the pre-parser model — while a non-scoped spec containing `/` stays a verbatim path word), and a `--` appearing AFTER the first child word is child data — for pnpm/yarn the locked child ends the option zone so `pnpm exec npm exec -- tsc --noEmit` keeps its separator, while npm's zone ends at its OWN `--` so `npm -- exec tsc --noEmit` stays free too (child options are the child's data; the fixed-A reject set still applies to the pre-child option zone).
  - R9 npm option-zone boundary correction: npm's exec/dlx option zone ends at its own `--`, NOT at the first child word — the previous childLocked-only model let `npm exec tsc --package=pig` and `npm exec tsc --call=pig` hide a package spec / call string behind the locked command head. npm now keeps parsing manager options (`--package`, `-p`, `--call`/`-c`, unknown-option Reject) until its own `--` appears; pnpm/yarn keep the verified first-child-word lock. `pnpm exec npm exec -- tsc --noEmit`, `npm -- exec tsc --noEmit` and `npm exec -- tsc --call=pig` keep their previous verdicts.
  - R10 oracle second-opinion fixes: (M1) the `--` separator branch now excludes npm's FIRST `--` from verbatim child retention even when a child word already locked — `childLocked || managerDD` became `(childLocked && base != "npm") || managerDD`, so npm's own `--` is always removed, `managerDD` set, and the tail joins the child. The nested bypass `npm exec npm exec -- --call=pig` (outer kept its `--` verbatim, inner consumed `--call=pig` as a child word → ALLOW) now BLOCKs on the inner `option --call` Reject; `pnpm exec npm exec -- tsc --noEmit`, `npm -- exec tsc --noEmit`, `npm exec -- tsc --call=pig` and `npm exec -- npm exec --call=pig` keep their verdicts. (S1) `takeValue`'s separated-value branch now rejects an empty operand (`--package ''`) with `empty option value`, matching the inline `--package=`/`=`-glued spelling — `isDynamicArg("")` is false, so the empty word previously entered `r.packages` unscreened and the exec sailed through on the child head.
- **Round-13 merge (`merge/rt1111-r13-20261113`): strict-readonly lexical allowlist + retained official npm model (`role_guards.go`, `readonly_allowlist.go`, `082a722`).** Official main (`8f3cb64`) is the base. Integration keeps the official finite npm accept model and its later fixes (`--call` / option zone / child spec / `--` passthrough / empty value) listed above; R13 did not replace that model. Under **strict-readonly roles only** (allowlist path), `strictReadonlyBlockLegacyScripting` blocks **perl / ruby / php / R / Elixir** wholesale — this is not a global ban for non-readonly or worker execution. Lazy Node-permission helpers and Landlock-related code remain present but are **not** wired onto the production executor path. Verification already recorded on integrated tree `082a722` (GUARDS-REPORT; the docs commit did not re-run tests): full suite skipping `TestAlFix7_FuzzArgvDiff` → `ok … 232.395s` (pass); fuzz alone with `TMPDIR=/root/gotmp-merge` → `ok … 160.937s` (pass); `go test -count=5 -run '^TestStressSingleflight$'` → pass; full `-race` suite was **not run** at that time (do not treat that earlier record as a pass). After docs commit `3976763` (no implementation changes), race was run on the same tree: default TMPDIR `go test -race -count=1 -timeout 30m -skip TestAlFix7_FuzzArgvDiff` → `ok … 660.988s` (rc=0); `TMPDIR=/root/gotmp-merge go test -race -count=1 -timeout 20m -run '^TestAlFix7_FuzzArgvDiff$'` → `ok … 14.125s` (rc=0); HEAD remained `3976763`, official main remained clean `8f3cb64`. Separate pre-guards fetch retest numbers (RETEST2, after `e58dd93` fetch change and before guards landed) were full-skip-fuzz `225.199s` and fuzz-disk `138.495s`; those are not the final integrated-tree acceptance.

## [5.3.3] - 2026-11-10

### Removed
- **Role guards: retired `herdr`/`herdsman` strict-readonly allowlist entries (`readonly_allowlist.go`).** The `readonlyAllowlist["herdr"]`/`readonlyAllowlist["herdsman"]` entries and their `checkHerdrReadonly`/`checkHerdsmanReadonly` checks are dead code now that `hc` is the only sanctioned terminal-control surface for sub-agents; strict-readonly roles (`scout`/`planner`/`researcher`/`reviewer`/`oracle`/`CTXMODE_READONLY=1`) now get the default-deny `unknown command herdr`/`unknown command herdsman` verdict like every other unlisted command. The sub-agent screens (`screenHerdrSubagent`/`screenHerdsmanSubagent` in `role_guards.go`, driven by `screenNestedDispatch` under `subagent_nested_agent`) are unchanged: non-readonly sub-agents still get `herdr`/`herdsman` write verbs blocked and read-only queries free. `readonlyToolsDescription` (surfaced in the `ctx_run` tool description) now lists `hc` instead of the retired `herdr`/`herdsman` names, keeping `TestReadonlyAllowlist_ToolsDescriptionSync` in sync. Test updates: strict-readonly allow expectations for `herdr`/`herdsman` moved to block in `readonly_allowlist_alfix7_test.go` and `readonly_allowlist_alfix6_test.go`, and allow-list spots in `readonly_allowlist_test.go`/`readonly_allowlist_alfix4_test.go`/`readonly_allowlist_alfix5_test.go`/`readonly_allowlist_alfix6_test.go`/`readonly_allowlist_alfix7_test.go` now cover `hc send`.

## [5.3.2] - 2026-11-09

### Changed
- **L2 baseline: consolidate repository checks into `make check` (`Makefile`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `AGENTS.md`).** New root `Makefile` `check` target is the single check entry: `gofmt -l .` (fails on unformatted files — baseline was clean), `staticcheck`, `go vet ./...`, `go test -race ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, and `node --experimental-strip-types --test integrations/pi/*.test.ts` when `node` is on PATH; with `CI=true` and no `node` the target fails loudly instead of silently skipping the Pi extension tests, while a zero-node maintenance host skips them with a printed note. `ci.yml` keeps its checkout/setup-go/setup-node/ripgrep/staticcheck-install preparation steps and now calls `make check` as a single step. `AGENTS.md` declares `档位：L2`, documents the `make check` boundary, and replaces the verbatim global commit-rule restatement with a reference to `/root/.pig/agent/SYSTEM.md` §4. `.gitignore` gains a `!/AGENTS.md` negation to override the local global ignore (`~/.config/git/ignore`), so `AGENTS.md` is tracked in version control as the L2-required deliverable.

### Fixed
- **Role guards: peel the `sbx` sandbox wrapper and screen its `--env` assignments (`role_guards.go`, `readonly_allowlist.go`, `[pattern:sbx-wrapper-unwrap-asymmetry]`).** `peelWrappers` now understands `sbx` (Go `flag.FlagSet` spelling: `-preset`/`--preset`, `-flag=val`/`--flag=val` and two-slot `-flag val`/`--flag val`, `--` terminator, first non-option operand as inner head), mirroring the real `sbx` `parseArgs` flag table: the eight value options `preset`/`workdir`/`ro`/`rw`/`exec`/`reviewer`/`report-to`/`env` are consumed (the phantom `tmpfs` was dropped), `deny-net`/`best-effort` are peeled as booleans, and `sbx --check`/`-version` plus `-h`/`--help`/`-V`/`--version` stay the `sbx` head as pure queries. `--env`/`--env=` values are comma-split like the real `stringSlice.Set` and each segment must be a static `KEY=VAL` literal — they land in `envAs` for the execution-hook env screen (`A=1,PATH=/tmp` now blocks like `PATH=/tmp`), while `sentinelTrustVars` keys (`HOME`, `PWD`, `TMPDIR`, `IFS`, ...) and empty segments fail closed at peel time since the env screen only gates hook vars. Missing value slots, dynamic option/operand words and any unrecognised `-` flag return `exhausted` (fail closed) rather than guessing an arity that could hide the inner command. Because the peel is generic, `stdinProgramHeadDepth`, `peelToSSHHead`, heredoc and here-string models see the inner head too — `echo pig | sbx --preset reviewer-agent -- sh` now blocks as `piped code to shell`, and `sbx -- hc kill t1` / `sbx --preset ... -- pig` route through `screenHcSubagent`/`screenNestedDispatch` instead of running the inner command unscreened. A PATH symlink resolving to `sbx` (`foo -> sbx`) gets the same treatment: `peelResolvedSegDepth` re-peels a promoted `sbx` head, and `stdinProgramHeadDepth` recurses through the rewritten head — its alias resolution now runs under the PATH the peeled env ops install (`stdinProgramHeadWithEnv` carries the accumulated `envAs` down every env-inheriting recursion so `echo pig | PATH=dir foo -- sh` still hits the `sbx` unwrap, while ssh remote heads resolve fresh). The same PATH-aware alias resolution reaches the sibling helpers: `replTokenInExecSlot`, `xargsFeedsCodeFlag` and `peelToSSHHead` (via `sshTunnelsStdinProgram` / `xargsDerivedSeg`) take the effective `pathEnv`, resolve `foo -> ssh`/`foo -> sbx` aliases under the peeled PATH and re-unwrap a promoted `sbx` head once — so `PATH=<dir> xargs ssx dmit sh -c` hits `dynamic code payload`, `find . -exec ssx dmit sh -c {} ;` hits `find -exec dynamic code payload`, and a piped `xargs ssx dmit sh` keeps the `piped stdin to ssh` tunnel verdict (remote recursions resolve under the remote login env, not the local PATH). The tunnel probe and stdin-head classifier now seed PATH resolution with the CALLER's PATH instead of replaying the merged `envAs` over an empty base (S-R4-1/S-R4-2): `guardCtxRunStdin`/`guardCtxRunStdinForCommand` pass the process `PATH` to `sshTunnelsStdinProgram` (`xargs ssx dmit 'PATH=/nope sh'` no longer lets the remote `PATH=` op poison the local `ssx -> ssh` resolution and drop the strict-readonly upgrade), `screenSegmentHeredocsDepth` computes the tunnel verdict on the unseeded `caller.pathEnv` and classifies heads via `stdinProgramHeadWithEnv` on the same base, and `stdinProgramHeadWithEnv` carries an explicit `basePath` so a `PATH=$PATH:<dir>` prefix expands `$PATH` like the runtime instead of collapsing to `:<dir>` and skipping the stdin payload screen entirely (ssh remote recursions in `stdinProgramHeadWithEnv`/`replTokenInExecSlot`/`xargsFeedsCodeFlag` seed with the process PATH for the same reason). `PI_SANDBOX_*` joins the env screen (`PI_SANDBOX_INTERNAL_CHILD_SPEC` in `envHookVars`, the prefix in `isEnvHookVar`) so a forged sbx internal child spec cannot reach the sandbox through a `KEY=VAL` prefix, `env`, or `sbx --env`. Strict read-only roles keep the exact pre-peel verdict: `readonlyPrePeelHit` returns `unknown command sbx` for the `sbx` head so `sbx --preset ... -- echo ok` never unwraps into an allowlisted inner. Regression coverage: `readonly_allowlist_test.go` `TestGuard_PeelSbxWrapper` (worker block/allow matrix including comma-split `--env` interception, `PI_SANDBOX_INTERNAL_CHILD_SPEC` interception, a `PATH=`-peeled `foo -> sbx` alias in a piped stdin head, `--exec`/`--reviewer`/`--deny-net` peeling and the `--check` pure query, plus reviewer first-layer block, and `checkSbxSSHStdinTunnel` covering the `PATH=/nope` remote-op-leak strict upgrade and the `PATH=$PATH:` self-reference collapse). Reviewer R1 follow-up: the seven non-env two-slot sbx value options (`preset`/`workdir`/`ro`/`rw`/`exec`/`reviewer`/`report-to`) now screen `argv[i+1]` for dynamic words before consuming it — `sbx --deny-net --workdir $(printf '/tmp sh -c pig') echo ok` previously skipped the slot and let runtime field-splitting shift the inner command out of place, and now fails closed like the `--env` operand screen. The `foo -> sbx` alias rewrite in `replTokenInExecSlot`, `xargsFeedsCodeFlag` and `stdinProgramHeadWithEnv`'s xargs inner-head unwrap moved to the shared bounded helper `peelWrappersAndSbxAliases`, which repeats the peel under each level's own effective PATH until the head stops resolving to sbx (exhaustion fails closed) — a second nested alias layer (`xargs -IXX foo --deny-net -- foo --deny-net -- sh -c XX`) can no longer hide the fed/exec code slot; both regressions covered by new `TestGuard_PeelSbxWrapper` rows (verified red before, green after).
- **Background log file names are scoped per process instance (`executor.go`, `[pattern:ctxmode-bg-log-collision]`).** `registerBackground` renamed the `CreateTemp` log to a fixed `ctxmode_bg_<id>.log` and job IDs restart at `bg-1` in every process, so two concurrent `ctxmode` instances — or an inner `go test` suite sharing `os.TempDir()` — overwrote and deleted each other's live logs (`/tmp/ctxmode_bg_bg-1.log` collision). A new package-level `bgInstanceID` (`<pid>_<8-hex>` from `crypto/rand`, nanosecond-time fallback) makes the final path `ctxmode_bg_<instance>_<id>.log`; the `init()` >24h orphan sweep still matches the `ctxmode_` prefix and needs no change. Regression coverage: `TestBackground_LogPathInstanceScoped` (`executor_test.go`).
- **L2 baseline follow-up: propagate `gofmt` exit code, pin `staticcheck` precheck, root-anchor `!AGENTS.md` (`Makefile`, `.gitignore`, `AGENTS.md`).** The `check` target previously ran `files="$$(gofmt -l .)"` where the command substitution swallowed `gofmt`'s own exit code: a file carrying a syntax error hidden behind a non-local `//go:build` tag (e.g. `//go:build ignore`) reports diagnostics on stderr, prints nothing to stdout, and exits 2 — the assignment discarded that status and `make check` sailed on while `go vet`/`staticcheck`/`go test`/`go build` all skipped the tag-shielded file. The recipe now propagates the substitution's failure (`files=... || exit $$?`). A `staticcheck` executable precheck at `$(go env GOPATH)/bin/staticcheck` fails fast with the pinned install command (`go install honnef.co/go/tools/cmd/staticcheck@2025.1.1`, matching CI) instead of make's opaque "command not found"; `AGENTS.md` records the pinned version so local and CI check the same staticcheck release. The `.gitignore` negation is root-anchored to `!/AGENTS.md` so same-named files in subdirectories are not unintentionally un-ignored.

## [5.3.1] - 2026-11-09

### Fixed
- **Role guards: register `hc` in the strict-readonly allowlist and close the `herdr-core` direct-call bypass (`readonly_allowlist.go`, `role_guards.go`).** P11 live acceptance found the second layer of the sub-agent `hc` gate missing: `screenHcSubagent` already whitelisted `hc send <addr> <text>` plus the pure query spellings, but strict-readonly callers (`scout`/`planner`/`researcher`/`reviewer`/`oracle`/`CTXMODE_READONLY=1`) still failed at `screenReadonlyAllowlist` with `unknown command hc`. `readonlyAllowlist["hc"]` now reuses `screenHcSubagent` as its check; `isAllowedSystemReadonlyAlias` admits the two live symlink layouts — `hc -> herdr-core` (host `/root/.local/bin`) and `hc -> sbx` (inside `sbx --preset reviewer-agent`) — so `screenReadonlyAliasTarget` no longer rewrites the head into an unlisted binary, while `herdr-core`/`sbx` themselves stay OUT of the allowlist (direct spelling still blocks). `screenNestedDispatch` and `isStructuralGuardHead` now cover `herdr-core`, so a non-readonly sub-agent naming the symlink target directly (`herdr-core run ...`) is routed through the same `screenHcSubagent` whitelist instead of bypassing the `hc` interception. Regression coverage: `readonly_allowlist_test.go` (`TestGuard_HcReadonlyAndSubagent`, verified red before the fix).

## [5.3.0] - 2026-11-08

> **Known issue:** v5.3.0 fails CI on GitHub-hosted runners (run 42444290259: PATH-trust checks rejected runner toolcache/GNU-prefixed binaries). Fixed in 5.3.1 — use 5.3.1 or later.


### Security
- **Role guards R12: tighten user-writable PATH trust boundary and alias resolution (`role_guards.go`, `[pattern:path-trust-user-writable]` / C10).** `isSafePathDirEntry` now anchors user toolchain directories (`~/.local/bin`, `~/.local/share/mise/shims`, `~/.cargo/bin`, `~/go/bin`, etc.) to `trustedHomeDirs()` (or `currentScreenCwd()` for project-local `.venv/bin` / `node_modules/.bin`), narrows `/home/linuxbrew/` to `/home/linuxbrew/.linuxbrew`, and verifies on-disk directories via `isTrustedPathDirOnDisk` (owner UID must be `0` or caller `euid`, group/world write bits `0022` must be clear, and `EvalSymlinks` must not resolve into `/tmp|/var/tmp|/dev/shm|/run`, while non-existent directories pass if their nearest existing ancestor is safe). `screenPathHeadTrust` blocks planted binaries in user-writable toolchain directories that shadow OS/guard core commands (`ls`, `cat`, `sh`, `git`, `ssh`, `find`, `xargs`, `sed`, `grep`, `awk`, etc.), `transparentShimBins` (`mise`, `asdf`, `rustup`, `pyenv`, `volta`, `fnm`, `corepack`) preserves the typed command identity across shims, `peelResolvedSegDepth` screens `typedHead` before any unwrap early return without overwriting structural heads, and `screenReadonlyAliasTarget` enforces the strict-readonly allowlist on non-distro symlink targets. Production `HOME=/root` sessions no longer append a `/home/*` trust wildcard in `trustedHomeDirs()` (cross-user paths such as `/home/otheruser/.local/bin` are rejected); coverage: `TestGuard_R12_RootHomeRejectsForeignHomePath`. Lexical system dirs (`/usr/local/bin`, ADAPT2) skip owner-uid checks but still reject group/world-writable `0022` and scratch symlinks.
- **Role guards R12: route static code spawns of `ssh` through `parseSSHInvocation` and fix `execv`/`execl` argv alignment (`role_guards.go`, `[pattern:code-static-spawn-single-layer]`).** `guardCtxRunCode` now evaluates `subagent` static spawns before the blanket `strictReadonly` spawn primitive fallback using a receiver-aligned `callerIdentity()`, deduplicates `argv[0]` in Python `os.execv*`/`os.spawnv*` calls so `os.execvp('ssh', ['ssh', 'dmit', 'pig'])` cannot shift `<destination>`, extracts variadic string literals in `os.execl*`/`os.spawnl*`, handles `shell=True` list arguments and `args=`/`cmd=` keyword arguments in `staticSpawnArgv`, and propagates `roSSHWriteHit` through inline interpreter invocations (`python3 -c`).
- **Role guards R12: preserve `shellSegment` stdin context and execution-slot placeholder checks across indirect wrappers (`role_guards.go`, `[pattern:indirect-wrapper-seg-stdin]`).** `screenSSHHead` unifies the `vehicleOnly` gate across `strictReadonly` and read-only sub-agents (`verifier` / `CTXMODE_READONLY=1`) so piggybacked remote writes (`ssh dmit "rm -rf /tmp/x; sh"`) and bare `find . -exec ssh dmit sh \;` are no longer exempted by `!stdinIsProg`; `flock`, `watch -x`, `xargs -a`, and `find -ok` propagate the enclosing `shellSegment` stdin state, `xargs` without `-a` models piped argument injection into `ssh` remote stdin-program heads and `-c` code flags, and `replTokenInExecSlot` blocks `{}` / `-I` tokens in command-head or inline-code positions while leaving data operands free. Regression coverage: `role_guards_r12_test.go`.


### Docs
- **SH11 round-8 `consider` archived**: user-writable PATH dirs (`/home/linuxbrew/`, `~/.local/bin`, toolchain shim dirs) are trusted by `isSafePathDirEntry`/`headBaseAliasHits`; a planted same-name binary there resolves as an already-allowed name. Trust-boundary tradeoff, not introduced by this diff — archived in `.agents/notes/2026-11-01-role-guards-non-sandbox-boundaries.md` (C10, `[pattern:path-trust-user-writable]`).

### Added
- **Role guards: `hc` terminal-control whitelist for sub-agents (`role_guards.go`).** `hc` (herdr-core CLI) is a terminal-control surface — `run`/`exec`/`file write`/`keys`/`attach` drive programs in any live terminal — so a sub-agent caller (`CTXMODE_ROLE` non-empty or `CTXMODE_READONLY=1`) now gets the `screenHerdrSubagent`-style whitelist `screenHcSubagent` under audit rule `subagent_nested_agent`: only `hc send <addr> <text>` (exactly two operand words, `<addr>` matching `^t[0-9a-z]+$` — the report channel), `hc --help`/`hc -h`, `hc --version`/`hc -V` and `hc <sub> --help` pass; `send` with options or extra operands, every other verb (`run`, `attach`, `read`, `ls`, `get`, `wait`, `keys`, `exec`, `file`, `kill`, `rm`, `label`, `server`, `machine`, `connect`, `update`, unknown verbs) and a bare `hc` block. The dynamic-derivation checks recognise `hc` alongside `herdr`/`herdsman`: `programNamesBlockedAgent` (dynamic awk command text) and the unresolved interpreter-spawn fallback in `guardCtxRunCode` fail closed on the bare identifier (`code drives hc mutation`). The main agent (no role env) is unaffected. Regression coverage: new rows in `TestGuard_SubagentNestedArgv` (all block cases returned `""` before the guard existed — verified red on baseline, green after).

### Fixed
- **`TestVersionAligned` expects `5.2.1` (`fixes_test.go`).** `a480142` bumped `main.go`'s `Version` to `5.2.1` without updating the assertion, leaving the baseline suite red; the literal now matches.

### Fixed
- **Role guards SH11 review round 8: function bodies model the unknown call environment, dup source normalization, bash move syntax (`shell_ast.go`).** A `FuncDecl` no longer captures the DECLARATION-site environment as a closure — the body now walks a fresh `stdinFeed{piped:true}` base with only the decl's own redirects replayed at call time, so `{ f() { sh; }; } </dev/null; echo pig | f` and `{ f() { sh 0<&9; }; } 9</dev/null` stop inheriting the enclosing group's file exemption / fd table while `f() { sh; } </dev/null` (or `9<f`/`9<&-`) still binds provably. `feedThrough`'s `n<&m`/`n>&m` source word normalizes leading zeros before the origins lookup — bash reads `0<&00` as `dup2(0,0)`, so `"00"` now hits the `"0"` slot instead of defaulting to a file origin — and a trailing `-` on a digit word is the bash move syntax `n<&m-`/`n>&m-`: the source origin copies into `n`, then the source closes (a later `0<&m` reads a closed descriptor), never a `>&word` file target (`sh 9<&0 0<&9-` blocks; `isFdDupWord` keeps `>&9-` out of `redirectFile`). Descriptor keys normalize on write too (`09<f` lands on fd9). Regression coverage: `shell_ast_red_test.go` `TestAST_MustRed_Round18` (11 probes).

- **Role guards SH11 review round 7: dynamic dup targets keep taint, function bodies inherit the provable descriptor table (`shell_ast.go`).** An unparseable `n<&m`/`n>&m` word no longer earns the file exemption — `echo pig | sh 0<&$(printf 0)` and `sh 9<&$(…) 0<&9` keep a tainted (possibly-pipe) origin because the real dup2 binds whatever the expansion yields (`n<&-` closes and static non-digit `>&word` write targets are unchanged). A `FuncDecl` body now walks with the declaration's descriptor table replayed over its own redirects — `{ f() { sh 0<&9; }; f; } 9<&0` resolves fd9 through the group feed exactly like a non-function statement — while the new `fdUnvetted` flag makes every dup of an unproven fd inside a body fail closed (`f() { sh 0<&9; }; f` blocks): the body's stdin is the CALL's stdin and the call-site descriptor set is unknowable. Coverage honesty: R16_M2_03's "sequential statement" claim is narrowed — the row exercises `stmtFeed`'s group-redirect replay into `walkStmtsAll`'s shared feed; `walkStmts`' i>0 `fds` propagation is dead code today (every list-level feed carries `fds=nil`) and is kept only defensively. Regression coverage: `shell_ast_red_test.go` `TestAST_MustRed_Round17` (12 probes).

### Fixed
- **Role guards SH11 review round 6: output-side fd duplication, conditional/pipeline descriptor table, perl/ruby numeric optional flags (`shell_ast.go`, `role_guards.go`).** `feedThrough` runs the same dup for `n>&m` as `n<&m` — dash dup2s both, so `echo pig | sh 0>&0` is a no-op that keeps the pipe open instead of masquerading as a file bind, `sh 9>&0 0<&9` smuggles it through fd9, and `0>&1`/`0>&-`/unresolvable targets still resolve to file-bound (`>&word` write targets keep the `redirectFile` path). The right side of `&&`/`||` now inherits the operator's whole feed (fds included — `echo pig | { true && sh 0<&9; } 9<&0` blocks), a pipeline right keeps the inherited descriptor table while only fd0 becomes the upstream stream (`{ cat | sh 0<&9; } 9<&0` blocks), and a compound body's shared feed reaches the same table into every inner statement (`{ cat; sh 0<&9; } 9<&0` blocks via walkStmtsAll — see round 7 for the sequential-claim narrowing); a conditional right's env/`staticVars` stays closed via `allowBind`. `interpCodeSlots` splits optional-glued flags into arbitrary-tail (`perl -i/-F/-x/-7`, ruby `-i/-x` — tail ends the cluster) and restricted numeric-tail (perl `-0`/`-l`, ruby `-0`): after the legal digits (or `-0`'s `xHH` hex) the cluster scan resumes, so `perl -0e 'code'`/`perl -le 'code'`/`ruby -0e 'code'` can no longer hide `-e` inside the numeric value. Regression coverage: `shell_ast_red_test.go` `TestAST_MustRed_Round16` (13 probes).

### Fixed
- **Role guards SH11 review round 5: operator-scoped default fd, inherited compound fd origins, perl/ruby optional-glued clusters, recursive env branches, sed -f argv advance (`shell_ast.go`, `role_guards.go`).** `feedThrough` now returns the whole per-descriptor origin table and resolves the missing-N default by operator — fd0 for stdin-side ops, fd1 for `>`/`>>`/`&>`/`&>>`/`>|`/`n>&m` — so `echo pig | sh >/dev/null` no longer masquerades a stdout redirect as an fd0 rebind, and `applyRedirs` writes the final fd0 origin back to `seg.pipedStdin` too (an explicit `0< f`/`0>f`/`0<&9`-file rebind clears the piped mark). `stdinFeed` carries the enclosing compound's descriptor snapshot (`fds`): `{ sh 0<&9; } 9<&0` resolves fd9 through the group's own dup table exactly like the flat `9<&0 0<&9` spelling. `interpCodeSlots` splits cluster letters into required-value / optional-glued-value / boolean classes shared by `unwrapInterpreterInline` and `xargsFeedsCodeFlag`: perl `-i`/`-F`/`-l`/`-0`/`-x` and ruby `-i`/`-0`/`-x` are optional-glued flags (a bare `-i` eats no argv word), so `perl -i -e 'code'` and `xargs -I{} perl -i -e '{}'` can no longer hide the `-e` code operand behind the `-i` slot. `stdinProgramHeadDepth` merges the accumulated `envAs` into the `flock`/`watch`/`xargs` direct recursions and the xargs fed-code inner-wrapper env (`xargs -I{} env HOME=-exec sh -c '{}'`); the heredoc and here-string consumers switch to `stdinProgramHeadEnv` so payload text is screened under env ops peeled at every recursion level (`flock L env HOME=-exec sh <<EOF` taints `$HOME` in the body). `sedScripts` decides argv advance by the real word length, not the placeholder boundary — `sed -nf"$PWD/x.sed" -e 'e pig'` treats the dynamic tail as the glued script-file operand and still screens the `-e` payload. The vacuous `read -s` probes now bind a legal head first (`x=/bin/echo; read -s x; "$x"`) so the taint actually decides the verdict. Regression coverage: `shell_ast_red_test.go` `TestAST_MustRed_Round15` (15 probes) and the extended `TestGuard_CtxRunStdinEnv` xargs fed-code case.

### Fixed
- **Role guards SH11 review round 4: fd-dup stdin origins, flag-model interpreter clusters, recursive API-stdin env, sed dynamic clusters, narrowed PATH exception (`shell_ast.go`, `role_guards.go`).** The collector now tracks every fd's stdin ORIGIN through the redirect list: `n<&m` copies the source fd's binding instead of unconditionally marking stdin file-bound, so `echo pig | sh 0<&0` and `sh 9<&0 0<&9` still block as `piped code to shell` while `sh 9<f 0<&9`/`sh 0<&-` correctly mark stdin bound; non-fd0 heredocs no longer pollute `heredocText`, and a compound statement's own redirects are replayed onto a `stdinFeed` threaded through `walkStmt` — `(sh) <<'EOF'` hands the payload to inner commands for screening, `{ sh; } <<EOF` and `if …; fi <<EOF`/`while …; done <<EOF`/`for …; done <<EOF`/`case … esac <<EOF`/`(…)|…` feed it the same way, and `(sh) < f` marks inner stdin file-bound. `interpCodeSlots` (now the single option model shared by `unwrapInterpreterInline`, `xargsFeedsCodeFlag` and the sed slot logic) distinguishes GLUED letters (python -c/-m, perl -eE, ruby -e, php -r) from FLAG-model code letters (sh/bash/dash -c, node -e/-p): `sh -ce 'x'`/`node -pe 'x'` resolve the code operand AFTER the whole cluster scan (a trailing -o/-O still consumes `bash -co vi 'x'`'s option name first), while ambiguous glued spellings (`-cpig`, `-eCODE`) keep being screened as code. `xargsReplToken` extracts the replace token under xargs' own arity model — clusters like `-rI{}`, deprecated `-i`/`-iSTR`, and GNU bare `--replace` (defaults to `{}` without eating the command word). `stdinProgramHeadEnv` accumulates the peeled env ops at EVERY recursion level so `argv=["sh","-c","env HOME=-exec sh"]`+stdin screens the payload under the consumed position's env (the `env HOME=-exec` inside the -c text now taints `$HOME` in the stdin program). `screenSedSubagent`/`sedScripts` locate dynamic cluster values by their static prefix (`-nf<(…)` → `dynamic code payload` like `-f<(…)`), `sedDynamicOptValue` classifies e/f/l tails with the same stdin/data-path rules, and unvettable dynamic option words (`sed -$X`) fail closed as script payloads instead of being skipped as checked positionals. The relative-PATH exception shrinks to the four authorized spellings (`node_modules/.bin`, `./node_modules/.bin`, `.venv/bin`, `./.venv/bin`) — `venv/bin`/`./venv/bin` were never approved and reject again. Regression coverage: `shell_ast_red_test.go` `TestAST_MustRed_Round14` (23 probes incl. read -s ordinary-bind evidence) and the extended `TestGuard_CtxRunStdinEnv` recursive-env/read -s cases.

### Fixed
- **Role guards SH11 review round 3: fd0 stdin tracking, xargs flag clusters, API-stdin env context, `read -s`, `--file=` glue (`shell_ast.go`, `role_guards.go`).** `applyRedirs` sets `stdinRedir` only for the LAST fd0 input redirect — `sh 3</dev/null` / `sh 3<<EOF` no longer pretend stdin was rebound while the pipe still feeds the program. Conditional `&&`/`||` right branches inherit the operator's shared stdin (`echo pig | (grep p && sh)` blocks) while staying `allowBind=false`, and every executable sub-statement inside `$(...)`/`<(...)` inherits the enclosing stdin stream (`echo pig | cat $(true; sh)` blocks), not just the first. `xargsFeedsCodeFlag` re-resolves the inner command through `peelWrappers` and locates code operands with a real per-interpreter option model (`interpCodeSlots`): flag clusters (`sh -lc`, `python3 -uc`, `node -pe`, `perl -pe`), glued code tails, wrapper heads (`xargs -I{} env sh -c {}`), bare `-i` (deprecated `-I`, default token `{}`), and fed-code language now propagates (`piped code to python`/`javascript`). `xargsTargetIndex` stops consuming a separate operand for `-i` (glued-optional like `-e`). Both ctx_run stdin entry points (`guardCtxRunStdin`, `guardCtxRunStdinForCommand`) screen the stdin program text through `guardCtxRunShellEnv`, seeding the caller with the peeled `envAs` ops so `argv=["env","HOME=-exec","sh"]`+stdin parses `$HOME` as tainted, never the trusted abs-dir grant. `read -s` is boolean in both rule copies (preScanVarMutations + `screenEnvWriteTargets`): `read -s GIT_PAGER` writes and blocks instead of eating the target as an option value. `screenSedSubagent` recognises `--file=`/`-f` value slots BEFORE the dynamic check so `--file=<(…)`, `--file=$X` and glued `-f<placeholder>` can no longer masquerade as the first positional script — proc-subst/dynamic script files hit `dynamic code payload`. Regression coverage: `shell_ast_red_test.go` `TestAST_MustRed_Round13` (14 probes with `want` asserting the hit reason) and `role_guards_test.go` `TestGuard_CtxRunStdinEnv`.

### Fixed
- **Role guards SH11 review round 1: env operand bounds, GNU `-S` escapes, dq-path grading, feed-scope restructure and unified hook table (`shell_ast.go`, `role_guards.go`, `readonly_allowlist.go`).** `skipEnvOperands` no longer slices past the end on a missing operand (`env -u`, `--chdir`, `--argv0`, `-a`, `-S` all fail closed instead of `i+=2` panic). `splitEnvSPayload` matches GNU exactly — `\_` is a word separator, `\t\n\r` are literals, `\c` truncates, `#` opens a word-start comment, every other `\X` is invalid — so `env -S 'pig\_x'` exposes the `pig` head and `env -S 'x\qy'` fails closed. `isSafeDynamicDataOperand` drops the `"$X/"`-with-`/` clause: only `__ctx_abspath_dq__` or a safe static prefix may start a data slot, so `find "$X/y"`/`node "$X/app.js"` refuse. `sedDynamicScriptSafe` requires an absdq-graded or literal-safe substitution (`litVars` for `-`/`!`-free binds) and bounds the dynamic span strictly before the flag zone. `xargsFeedsCodeFlag` grades every code slot (`-e`/`-c`/`--eval`/`{`/`exec`/`spawn`/`system(` args and the trailing `-c`/`{`/`-e` operand): a `-I` token *inside* a code word feeds it (`xargs -I{} sh -c 'x{}y'`). The collector restructured for feed scope: the pipe's `pipeIn` now penetrates `Subshell`/`Block`/`If`/`While`/`Until`/`Case`/`For` bodies, every `CmdSubst`/`ProcSubst`, and only fd0-`Redirect`s and here-docs/here-strings (which consume the pipe); `Stmt.Background` snapshots `pipeIn` and never binds parent vars (a `Background`'s left subtree runs in a child collector). `emitDecl`/`assignToPrefix`/`withLhsUntainted` record `envAs` ops (`export`, `declare -x`, prefix `NAME=v` order) and `callerIdentity.seededWith(envAs)` threads them into every nested parse (`sh -c`, heredoc, here-string, `eval`, `xargs`, `watch`, `ssh` remote, `find -exec`), so `env -u PWD sh <<EOF`/`bash <<<` refuse a `$PWD` grant that the outer env cleared. `forEnumSubst` splits enum words but refuses glob/`?`/`[` items for cmd-position use (`for s in "git *"; do $s`). `wordToArgWithSubst` grades `litVars` vs `staticVars`/`enumVars` (a bare `"$VAR"` head never resolves lit binds — `BUN=echo; "$BUN"` stays dynamic). The `"*"` give-up taint poisons every bind (`command $(true) PWD` clears `staticVars`/`litVars`/`sentinelTrustVars`) while PATH keeps its own channel (`taintedVars["PATH"]`+`ssaBlocked`, not `isTainted`). `guardCtxRunStdinForCommand`'s ssh stdin-program upgrade now also fires on a *local file* `< target` (`ssh dmit sh < /tmp/x` is a remote program read from a local file). `envUnknownLongPrefix` carries `"missing operand "` so reviewers see why; `awk -f -`, `sed -f -`/`--file=-`, `awk -f <(...)`/`sed -f <(...)` refuse (script-from-stdin/process-subst is an unvetted script). `isEnvHookVar` gains `PS4`/`SHELLOPTS`/`BASHOPTS`/`GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`/`GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`/`GIT_DIR`/`NODE_PATH`/`PYTHONSTARTUP`/`PERL5LIB`/`GIT_PAGER`/`PAGER`/`VISUAL` — and `guardCtxRunEnv` now runs *every* key through that table so `ctx_run`'s env API stops duplicating the inline-screen list (identity keys still deny in `filterExecEnv` first). `journalctl` moves `n` into the boolean short set (`-n`/`-n 5` pass for `ro`). Regression coverage: `shell_ast_red_test.go` `TestAST_MustRed_Round12` (53 probes, one per R1–R9/M1–M5 finding plus S1–S4) and `role_guards_test.go` `TestCtxRunEnvAPI_HookTable`.

### Fixed
- **Role guards SH11 shell hardening (C-tier): graded dynamic placeholders, env -S re-parse, env-op replay (`shell_ast.go`, `role_guards.go`).** The sub-agent AST now emits *graded* placeholders for `$(...)` substitutions: a word-start double-quoted placeholder carries the abs-dir variant `__ctx_abspath_dq__` only when its expansion is guaranteed absolute (`$PWD`/`$OLDPWD`/`$HOME`/`$(pwd)`/abs-path `staticVars`/`$(mktemp -d)`); every other word-start dynamic word refuses positions where it could become an option (`find` path zone, interpreter script operand, `git -c` option zone, `ssh` option/destination slots). Static-safe data operands are admitted via `isSafeDynamicDataOperand` so `find "$d" -name x`, `python3 "$script.py"` and `ssh host "$r"` style uses still pass. Sentinel-trust is structural (no value-compare): `$HOME`/`$PWD`-style grants live in `sentinelTrustVars` and degrade to plain dynamic the moment the same script writes them (`HOME=/x`, `IFS=x read`) or an outer env op overrides them (`env -i`, `env -u PWD`, `HOME=/x sh -c`, `export -n PWD`) via `callerIdentity.seededWith(envAs)` + `envTaint` propagation. `env -S`/`--split-string` payloads are re-split by `splitEnvSPayload` and fed back through env's own operand parse inside `peelWrappers` (now returning the residual argv), so `env -S 'pig --help'`, `-vS` clusters and `env -S 'env agy'` resolve like GNU env itself. `export -n`/`declare +x`/`typeset -n` are screened (un-exporting `CTXMODE_*`/hook vars or nameref writes refuse); `unset` additionally refuses execution-hook and sentinel-trust names; `read`/`getopts`/`printf -v` write targets are screened for role/hook/dynamic names. `PATH=` values get a per-segment trust table (system + toolchain dirs, `/tmp`/`/var/tmp`/`/dev/shm`/`/run` refused, empty segment = "." refused, relative `node_modules/.bin`/`.venv/bin` only for toolchain heads incl. versioned `python3.x` aliases), `IFS=` only allows whitespace/`:`/`,`. `xargs` learns the code-slot feed model (`xargs sh -c`, `-I{} sh -c {}`); `stdinProgramHead` peels `eval`/`flock`/`script`/`watch`/`xargs` wrappers and respects `seg.stdinRedir`/`backgrounded`; `ssh` remote commands are re-screened unconditionally under strict read-only unless the remote is a single pure stdin-program vehicle fed by a static local payload, and multi-stage remote stdin programs refuse. `sed` gets `sedDynamicScriptSafe` (static-delimiter `s///` with an e-free flag zone may stay dynamic) plus operand-modelled screening; `awk`/`tar`/`ssh`/`find`/`git -c`/`headReadsStdinProgram` adopt the data-vs-code slot split so dynamic input files pass while dynamic code/option slots refuse. Regression coverage in `shell_ast_red_test.go`, `role_guards_test.go`, `readonly_allowlist_alfix7_test.go`.

### Fixed
- **Readonly shell allowlist alfix7: real dash argv differential + Must Fix 1–6.** Deleted `stripLineContinuations` pre-rewrite; fold `\\n` inside the lexer via `peekAfterLineCont` (comments never continue). Fixed `readHeredocDelim` `\\r`, `"${...}"` single-quote rules + nested `${}`, `scanInlineSubstitutions` backtick unescape, `"$(...)"` case/heredoc in `skipBalanced`, and GNU `env` short clusters/`-a`/`--argv0`/bare `-`. Should Fix: ctxmode `help`/`-- -h` blocked; trailing NL/blank/comment lines; `find -prune`/`-newermt`; git tag/branch `--sort`; herdr/herdsman readonly queries; rg `-a`/`-g`. New independent `testdata/argvprinter` + `readonly_allowlist_alfix7_test.go` (AUDIT3 32 + MF samples + 2000/20000 fuzz, race-safe).

### Fixed
- **Readonly shell allowlist alfix6: dash-aligned lexer + shared-parser fixes.** `checkReadonlyShellSyntax` rewritten as byte allowlist + two-state lexer (ban bare `\\r`/controls, unquoted `~/*/?/[`, unquoted backslash, quoted command heads, glued redirects). Shared `splitShellSegmentsDepth` drops `\\r` from blanks, deletes quoted-digit IO_NUMBER wipe, tightens `$((` pairing, strips line continuations, unescapes backtick bodies, handles `${...}`, heredoc delimiter without `TrimRight(\\r)`, GNU `env` unique long-opt prefixes, herdr global flag whitelist, git branch/tag optional-value dash swallowing. Adds dash argv differential + fuzz tests (`readonly_allowlist_alfix6_test.go`).

### Added
- **Role guards: three ssh stdin/ordering edge holes closed (`role_guards.go`).** `stdinProgramHead` now peels an ssh head through `parseSSHInvocation` and asks `stdinProgramHead` recursively (bounded by `shellSplitMaxDepth`) whether any remote-command head reads its PROGRAM from the forwarded stream, with symlink-resolved heads covered via `resolveHeadBase` — so `ctx_run argv=["ssh","dmit","sh"]` (or `command: "ssh dmit sh"`, or `argv=["ssh","dmit","python3","-"]`) no longer skips `stdin` screening: the payload is remote code and gets `guardCtxRunShell`/`guardCtxRunCode`. The new `sshTunnelsStdinProgram` helper upgrades a read-only-but-not-strict caller (`verifier`, bare `CTXMODE_READONLY=1`) to the strict read-only screen for ssh-tunnelled stdin, ending the local `/tmp` exemption at the wire. `screenSSHHead` restores the entry-point order "sub-agent first, then strict read-only": under `kind == 's'` the nested-dispatch screen runs before `guardShellReadonlyDepth` on both the remote command and the here-string/heredoc bodies (screened via `screenStdinProgram` when a remote head reads its program from the stream), so `ssh dmit "rm -rf /tmp/x && pig"` reports `subagent_nested_agent` rather than `roSSHWriteHit`. `screenOneSegmentDepth` defers the generic piped-stdin block to `screenSSHHead` when the head is an ssh invocation whose remote command reads its program from stdin, preserving the `piped stdin to ssh` / `roSSHWriteHit` verdicts. Covered by new `TestGuard_SSHPeel` subcases (`guardCtxRunStdin`/`guardCtxRunStdinForCommand` under worker/verifier, mixed write+dispatch precedence for all read-only roles).
- **Role guards: `ssh` moves from a blanket sub-agent ban to transparent peel + remote-command guard inheritance (`role_guards.go`).** `blockedAgentExecutables` drops `"ssh"`; instead `peelResolvedSegDepth` routes an `ssh` head through `screenSSHHead`, which parses the option run with real OpenSSH semantics — short clusters (`-qT`/`-tt`/`-vvv`), value-taking letters in glued (`-p2222`/`-oBatchMode=yes`/`-i/path`) and separate (`-p 2222`) form, `--` terminator, long options — before the first non-option operand is taken as `<destination>` and the rest joined as the remote command (`strings.Join`, matching OpenSSH). Banned surfaces still block: `-F` (config file can arm ProxyCommand/LocalCommand), `-J` (ProxyJump), `-S`/`-M`/`-O` (control socket), `-L`/`-R`/`-D`/`-W`/`-w` (forwarding/tunnels), `-N`/`-f`/`-G`/`-Q`/`-s`, `-E` (local log write), every unvetted long option class, and any unknown short letter (fail closed). `-o` values run through a key whitelist (`sshSafeOKeys`: batchmode, connecttimeout, stricthostkeychecking, userknownhostsfile, identitiesonly, identityfile, port, user, hostname, serveralive*, loglevel, connectionattempts, addressfamily, requesttty, compression, pubkey/password/kbdinteractive auth, numberofpasswordprompts, forwardagent, …) with `controlmaster=no`/`controlpath=none` value-gated — `proxycommand`, `proxyjump`, `localcommand`, `permitlocalcommand`, `remotecommand`, `match`, `include` and every other exec/forwarding key refuses by name. A missing `<destination>` or an empty remote command blocks (`ssh dmit` alone would drop into an unaudited interactive login shell). The remote command then re-enters the caller's own guard: `guardShellSubagentDepth` for every sub-agent (remote `pig`/`pi`/`agy`/`dispatch-agent`/`grok`/write-`herdr`/`tmux`/`sudo pig` still blocks as `subagent_nested_agent`, including multi-statement and nested-`ssh` forms within `peelChainMaxDepth`), and `guardShellReadonlyDepth` for every read-only caller — verifier included, whose `/tmp` exemption ends at the wire — so a remote `rm`/`git pull`/`systemctl restart`/redirect blocks as `readonly_write_blocked` while `ssh dmit "git -C /repo status && systemctl is-active svc"` stays free. Remote stdin: a pipe's bytes fail closed only when the remote command reads its program from stdin (`echo x | ssh host sh` → `piped stdin to ssh`; `cat list | ssh host 'xargs touch'` stays free, matching the local piped-code model), and here-strings/heredoc bodies on an ssh segment are screened as shell text when the remote head is a stdin-program reader. Read-only remote hits report through the `roSSHWriteHit` sentinel so `guardCtxRunArgv`/`guardCtxRunShell` attribute them to `readonly_write_blocked` even when they surface inside the sub-agent (`'s'`) pass. `screenOneSegmentDepth` now passes the owning `shellSegment` into `peelResolvedSegDepth` so the ssh stdin surface is visible; every other caller keeps the zero segment. Covered by `TestGuard_SSHPeel` (~50 subcases over worker/scout/reviewer/oracle/verifier) and the updated `TestGuard_SessionAndEscalationExecs` entries.

- **Role guards enforce the dispatch role model (`role_guards.go`, new).** `CTXMODE_ROLE`/`CTXMODE_READONLY` were audit annotations only; they now drive two hard-block rules evaluated after `startAudit` (so a blocked call is still audited with `screen_verdict`=`block`, `screen_rule` set, `exit_code=-1`). `subagent_nested_agent` applies to every sub-agent caller (any non-empty `CTXMODE_ROLE`, or `CTXMODE_READONLY=1`) and blocks nested dispatch of agent executables (`pig`, `pi`, `agy`, `dispatch-agent`, `dispatch-agent-go`, `grok`) — matched only at command position after `env`/`command`/`exec`/`nohup`/`timeout`/`nice`/`setsid`/`stdbuf`/`busybox` peeling, recursive `sh -c` unwrapping, and compound-statement segmentation (`;`, `|`, `&&`, `||`, `&`, newlines, `(...)`, `$(...)`, `` `...` ``) — plus `herdr`/`herdsman` mutations (`herdr tab create|close|rename|move|focus`, `pane run|split|close|rename|move|swap|send-keys|focus`, `workspace create|close|rename|focus`, `agent start|prompt|send-keys`, `server stop|reload-agent-manifests`, `herdsman on|off`) while `herdr`/`herdsman` read-only queries stay open. `readonly_write_blocked` applies to read-only roles (`CTXMODE_READONLY=1`, or `CTXMODE_ROLE` ∈ `scout|planner|researcher|reviewer|oracle|verifier`): all of them lose `ctx_kb index|purge` (search/fetch/stats/doctor stay open); the strict set (all except `verifier`, which keeps `/tmp` scratch capability) additionally loses `ctx_bg kill`, `ctx_run action=run_task`, filesystem-write/kill command heads, in-place editors (`sed -i`/`-i.bak`/`-ni`/`-Ei`, `perl`/`ruby -i`, `gofmt -w`), git write subcommands (`git status|diff|log|show|worktree list|remote -v|tag -l|config --get` pass), `systemctl`/`go`/`cargo`/`npm`/`pnpm`/`yarn`/`bun` write verbs, and output redirects to real files (`2>/dev/null`, `2>&1`, `>&-` pass). Wired through `toolExecute` (argv + command), `toolExecuteFile` (shell language), `toolBatchExecute` (per command), `toolRunTask` (whole action for strict roles + argv guard), and the `ctx_kb`/`ctx_bg` routers.

- **Role guards hardening: bypass surfaces and read-only false positives closed.** `screenGitWrite` now uses an explicit read-only subcommand whitelist plus dual-mode flag precision — bare `git worktree`/`worktree list`/`-h` pass while every other worktree verb blocks; `git branch --contains/--merged/--points-at/--sort/--format/-l` are queries while `-d/-m/-c/-f/-u/--set-upstream-to/--edit-description` block; `git tag -d foo -n` no longer slips through on `-n` (any of `-d/-a/-s/-u/-f/-m/-F/--annotate/--sign/--local-user` blocks, only list/verify flags pass); `git config` write ops (`--unset/--add/--replace-all/--remove-section/--rename-section/-e`) block while `--get/-l/--show-*` pass; `git remote`/`git stash` are read-only only for `-v`/`show`/`get-url` and `list`/`show` respectively; every non-whitelisted subcommand (`notes`, `replace`, `update-ref`, `symbolic-ref`, `write-tree`, `hash-object`, `archive`, `bundle`, `format-patch`, `maintenance`, …) blocks, as does `--output` on any subcommand. Wrapper handling fixes: `command -v/-V` is a read-only builtin query while `command -p`/`command --` peel to the real head; `--` option terminators are honoured across `env`/`timeout`/`nice`/`nohup`/`setsid`/`stdbuf`/`exec`; `env -S`/`--split-string` payloads are screened as shell text instead of skipped; glued `sh -c"code"`/`bash -lc"code"` spellings unwrap; `eval` args are screened as shell text; `xargs` option arity is peeled so the target command is screened (`xargs pig`, `xargs rm`, `xargs sed -i` block, `xargs grep`/`wc` pass); `find -exec/-execdir/-ok/-okdir` inner commands recurse into the guards and `-delete`/`-fprint*` block for strict roles. Shell parser fixes: dynamic output-redirect targets (`> $(...)`, `` > `...` ``) record a blocking placeholder and their substitutions are scanned; `[[ ... ]]` and `(( ... ))`/`$(( ... ))` comparison operators are no longer read as redirections; `<<`/`<<-` heredoc bodies are consumed to their terminator (a body line like `rm = 1` is no longer mistaken for a command), bodies fed to shell/interpreter heads (`sh <<EOF`, `python3 - <<PY`) are screened as code, and unquoted bodies still get `$(...)`/`` `...` `` scanning. New `guardCtxRunCode` screens non-shell interpreter code: spawn+blocked-agent blocks under `subagent_nested_agent`; spawn primitives, file-write primitives (mode-aware `open(`/`os.open(`, `os.remove`, `shutil.*`, `pathlib` mutators, `fs.writeFile*`/`rmSync`/`createWriteStream`, `Deno.write`, `Bun.write`, `os.WriteFile`, `file_put_contents`, `FileUtils.`, …; plain `open(p)`, `fs.readFile*` pass), and compile-to-disk languages (`go`/`rust`/`csharp`) block under `readonly_write_blocked` — wired into `toolExecute` (non-shell languages), `toolExecuteFile` (now guarding `args.Code` so `FILE_CONTENT` data cannot trip the guard), and inline `python -c`/`node -e|--eval|-p|--print`/`ts-node|tsx|bun -e`/`perl -e|-E`/`ruby -e`/`php -r` argv spellings (plus `python -m pip|venv|ensurepip|compileall` for strict roles). `awk -i inplace`, `curl -O`/`-o FILE`, and `wget` default disk downloads block for strict roles.

- **Role guards hardening, second pass: nesting/exec surfaces closed.** `blockedAgentExecutables` gains the detached-session managers `tmux`/`screen` and the privilege/namespace/scheduler/remote wrappers `sudo`, `doas`, `su`, `pkexec`, `nsenter`, `unshare`, `chroot`, `systemd-run`, `crontab`, `at`, `ssh`; `codeAgentNames` adds `pi`/`tmux`/`screen`. Wrapper peeling now bounds at 32 steps (bare `KEY=VAL` prefixes do not consume a step), peels compound-statement keywords (`if`/`then`/`elif`/`else`/`while`/`until`/`do`/`{`/`}`/`!`/`coproc`/`time [-p]`; trailing `fi`/`done`/`}`/`esac` are no-ops) and the scheduler wrappers `ionice`/`taskset`/`chrt`/`watch`/`strace`/`ltrace`; `flock` peels options+lock operand to its inner command (strict read-only blocks `flock` outright), `script -c` screens its payload as shell text (strict blocks `script`), `trap` screens a non-print action argument, and `unset` of `CTXMODE_ROLE`/`CTXMODE_READONLY` blocks for sub-agents. Execution-hook env vars (`GIT_PAGER`/`EDITOR`/`LD_PRELOAD`/`BASH_ENV`/`PROMPT_COMMAND`/`NODE_OPTIONS`/`PYTHONSTARTUP`/`PERL5OPT`/`RUBYOPT`/`JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS`/…) block for sub-agents and strict read-only via `KEY=VAL` prefixes, `env` operands and `env -u`/`--unset` — with `PAGER`/`GIT_PAGER`/`MANPAGER` keeping the `""`/`cat` disabling spellings and `GOOS`/`CGO_ENABLED`/`TZ`/`LC_ALL`/`TMPDIR`/`PATH` untouched. `$(...)`/`` `...` ``/`(...)` nesting past `shellSplitMaxDepth` and eval/xargs/find recursion past `peelChainMaxDepth` emit a marker that both guards block (`exceeded max shell nesting depth`) instead of silently dropping inner commands; `<<-EOF` honours a tab-indented terminator. `git -c`/`--config-env`/`--exec-path=<dir>` exec-arming config blocks (`core.pager` — `cat`/`""` exempt — `core.sshcommand`, `core.fsmonitor`, `core.editor`, `sequence.editor`, `core.hookspath`, `core.askpass`, `diff.external`, `credential.helper`, `pager.*`, `alias.*` incl. `!`-values, `filter.*`, `difftool.*`, `mergetool.*`; bare `git --exec-path` stays free) for sub-agents and strict read-only alike. `ctx_run` `stdin` payloads feeding a shell/interpreter head (`sh`, `python3`, …; `sh -c`/`python3 -c`/`node -e`/`perl -e`/`php -r`/`python -m` inline spellings mean stdin is data) are screened with the matching guard. Python `-cCODE`/`-ucCODE`/`-mMODULE` glued spellings unwrap.
