Strict read-only additionally blocks: bare shells/interpreters running script files or stdin (`sh script.sh`, `python3 app.py`, `node app.js`, `argv=["sh"]`; `-n`/`--version`/`-V`/`--help`/`-h` stay free), `.`/`source` (worker keeps `. ./env.sh`), the expanded `fsWriteHeads` table (editors `ed`/`ex`/`vi`/`vim`/`nvim`/`nano`/`sponge`, `split`/`csplit`/`fallocate`/`chattr`/`setfattr`/`setfacl`, `mount`/`umount`/`mknod`/`mkfifo`, `uudecode`/`zip`/`7z*`/`rar`/`cpio`/`ar`/`ranlib`/`strip`/`objcopy`, compilers, `make`/`cmake`/`ninja`/`meson`, `pip*`/`pipx`/`uv`/`poetry`/`conda`/`gem`/`composer`, `flock`, `script`), archive write spellings (`tar` except `-t`/`--list`/`--help`/`--version`/`-x` with `-O`; `unzip` except `-l`/`-t`/`-p`/`-v`/`-Z`/`-h`; `gzip`/`gunzip`/`bzip2`/`bunzip2`/`xz`/`unxz`/`zstd`/`unzstd`/`lz4` except stdout/list/test/version/help spellings), sed `w`/`W` script-file writes (`/word/p`, `s/a/word/` pass), awk `system(`/`| getline`/`print >` escapes (`$1 > 5`, `/a|b/`, `-f` operands pass; `-f progfile` blocks), and `ctx_kb fetch` for every read-only role except `researcher` (whose tool-cache exception stays). `codeSpawnPrims` adds `__import__`/`importlib`/`ctypes`/`cffi`/`eval(`/`exec(`/`Function(`/`process.binding`/`process.dlopen`/`System.cmd`/`Port.open`/`system2(`; perl/ruby/php backtick-`qx`/`%x` spellings count as spawn primitives; `open(p, m)`/`mode='r'+'w'`/non-literal modes fail closed while `open(p)`/`open(p,'r')`/`open(p,'rb')`/`open(p, encoding='utf-8')`/`Path(p).open('r')` stay free.

- **Role guards hardening, third pass: command queries, interpreter flags, code primitives and KB-write parity.** `command` and `builtin` are split: `command` followed by a pure query option run (`-v`/`-V`, `-p` allowed, covering `-pv`/`-Vp`/`-p -v` clusters) is kept as the head and allowed as a read-only builtin query, while argument-reversed (`command pig -v`) and `-p`-only spellings still peel to the real command; `builtin` has no query semantics and always peels. The perl/ruby in-place `-i` check now runs for strict read-only roles only (a worker's `perl -i -pe 's/a/b/' file` is a normal write task, not a blocked head) and uses `hasInterpInPlaceFlag`, which stops at `--` and skips `-e`/`-E`/`-M`/`-m`/`-I` operands so script text can never be read as an `-i` flag. `unwrapInterpreterInline` honours the `--` terminator (the next operand is a script file, not inline code) and covers the remaining glued short-option spellings (`node`/`nodejs` `-pCODE`, `perl` `-ECODE`, `ts-node`/`tsx`/`bun`/`ruby` `-e`, `php` `-r`). `guardCtxRunCode` closes the cross-language spawn/write gaps: Python method-call `.open(` treats its first positional (or `mode=`) as the open mode (`Path('/tmp/x').open('w')` blocks, `p.open('r')`/`p.open(encoding='utf-8')` pass), `urllib.request.urlretrieve` and `sqlite3.connect` without a `file:`-URI `mode=ro` + explicit `uri=True` block; `codeSpawnPrims` gains `pcntl_exec` and perl/ruby/php get a string/comment-masked lexical pass so paren-less `system 'pig'` / `exec 'pig'` / `shell_exec('pig')`, `qx`, `%x`, backticks, and perl pipe `open` count as spawns (agent names inside the still-visible backtick/`%x`/`qx` bodies trip `subagent_nested_agent`, while quoted words like `print 'unlink'` stay free); strict read-only additionally blocks perl `unlink`/`mkdir`/`rmdir`/`rename`/`chmod`/`chown`/`truncate`/`symlink`/`link`/`sysopen` and write-mode `open` (`>`/`>>`/`+<`/`+>` — pure-read spellings pass), ruby `FileUtils.`/`File.write`/`File.binwrite`/`IO.write`/`IO.binwrite`/`File.delete`/`File.unlink`/`File.rename`/`File.chmod`/`File.chown`/`File.truncate`/`File.symlink`/`File.link`/`Dir.mkdir`/`Dir.rmdir`/`Dir.unlink` and write-mode `File.open` (`File.open('f')`/`('f','r')` pass), and php `file_put_contents`/`unlink`/`mkdir`/`rmdir`/`rename`/`chmod`/`chown`/`touch`/`copy`/`symlink`/`link` and write-mode `fopen` (`fopen('f','r')` passes; the former unconditional `fopen(` token was replaced by the mode-aware check). `ctxmode index` gets KB-write parity with `ctx_kb action=index`: `runCLI` refuses every read-only role — verifier included — and `ctx_run` argv/shell screening of a `ctxmode` head (`ctxmode [flags] index`) blocks for the same roles while `ctxmode search` stays open. `env -i`/`--ignore-environment` (including `-i` inside a short-option cluster like `-i0`) is recorded as clearing the inherited environment and blocks for sub-agents unless the same `env` call re-passes a non-empty `CTXMODE_ROLE=` — the same identity-shedding rule as `env -u`/`--unset` on the taint vars (`-uVAR`/`--unset=VAR`/`-CDIR`/`--chdir=DIR`/`-SSTR`/`--split-string=STR` bundled forms were already parsed).

### Fixed
- **Role guards ssh peel, review round 2: post-`<destination>` option parsing, ASCII-only `-o` trimming and `-I` ban (`role_guards.go`).** `parseSSHInvocation` now tracks `sawDest` instead of treating the first non-option word as the remote-command start: matching OpenSSH `ssh.c` (`optind = optreset = 1; goto again`), when no `--` terminator appeared the words AFTER `<destination>` are still ssh's own options until the SECOND non-option word — so `ssh dmit -q pig` names remote command `pig` (the old code joined `-q pig`, hiding the `pig` head), `ssh dmit -o ProxyCommand=evil uptime` now hits `ssh -o proxycommand`, and a trailing `--` after `<destination>` is still a terminator. When `--` precedes `<destination>`, the very next word already starts the remote command. `screenSSHOpt` (and the remote-command emptiness check) now trims ASCII blank only (`strings.Trim(…, " \t\r\n")`) matching OpenSSH `readconf.c`: a non-ASCII blank like U+00A0 stays part of the value, so `UserKnownHostsFile=none ` is a real local filename OpenSSH would create and gets refused as `ssh -o userknownhostsfile` instead of being trimmed into the `none` sink. `sshBannedShorts` gains `'I'` — `-I pkcs11_provider.so` `dlopen`s a local shared library and runs its initializers. Regression coverage added in `TestGuard_SSHPeel`.
- **Role guards ssh peel, review round 1: `-o` value gates and fail-closed long options (`role_guards.go`).** `sshOValueOK` now value-gates the two known-hosts keys (`userknownhostsfile`/`globalknownhostsfile` accept only `/dev/null` or `none`, case-insensitive — an arbitrary path paired with `StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new` gave a read-only sub-agent a local file-write side effect) and `enableescapecommandline` (only `no` — `yes` arms the interactive `~C` local-command escape). `parseSSHInvocation` drops the long-option value table (`sshValueLongs` removed): OpenSSH `ssh` has no legitimate double-dash long options at all, so every `--name` now fails closed — banned names report `--<name>`, everything else reports `ssh <literal>` — and an unknown `--long` can no longer slip past the option run to be read as skipped. Regression coverage added in `TestGuard_SSHPeel`; remaining non-blocking observations archived in `.agents/notes/2026-11-04-ssh-peel-followups.md`.
- **`writeStubBinary` pins the stub's mode with an explicit chmod, so deploy fragment tests no longer depend on the process umask.** `os.WriteFile`'s 0755 is masked by the umask — under a strict 0007/0027 umask the stub lands 0750, and the unprivileged (setpriv to nobody) second deploy in `TestDeployFragment_SecondDeployOverReadonlyPrevSucceeds` then fails with `install: cannot open '.../ctxmode-build2' for reading: Permission denied` even though the test hands the directory itself to that uid. The chmod makes the intended mode deterministic on any machine; on a stock umask-0022 runner nothing changes.

- **Role guard name screens no longer go blind where interpreters resolve to versioned or shim targets.** `peelResolvedDepth` substitutes the symlink-resolved basename for the command head before the name-keyed screens run, but stock layouts resolve `php` to `php8.3` (Debian alternatives), `ruby` to `ruby3.2` and `npx` to npm's `npx-cli.js` — none of which match the canonical names the interpreter inline-code table (`unwrapInterpreterInline`) and the `npx`/`bunx`/`pnpx` package-runner branch are written against, so `php -r`, `ruby -e` and `npx <agent>` slipped both guards on exactly the machines where the tools are installed that way (stock CI runners), while machines without them screened fine and the suite stayed green. The resolved name now passes through `canonicalResolvedBase`: version-suffixed `php`/`ruby`/`perl` targets and the npm `*-cli.js` shims map back to the canonical name, and every other target name passes through unchanged, so a disguised alias still surfaces under its real target name. The existing `TestGuard_InterpreterInlineArgv`, `TestGuard_HardGuardsRound3` and `TestGuard_InterpValueOptClusters` cases lock it in: they fail pre-fix when the `php`/`ruby`/`npx` found on PATH are symlinks to `php8.3`/`ruby3.2`/`npx-cli.js` and pass post-fix.
- **`integrations/pi/install.sh` skips the TypeScript sync when a native Go extension owns `ctxmode` (zero-node crash fix).** `e265167` moved the default `DEST` to `~/.pig/agent/extensions/ctxmode.ts`, so on zero-node hosts — where `~/.pig/agent/extensions/ctxmode` is a symlink to a native Go extension directory containing `go.mod` — every `deploy.sh` run wrote `ctxmode.ts` next to it; `pig` scans `*.ts` extensions at startup, tried to spawn the nonexistent `node` (>= 22.13), and crashed before any session could start. When the destination is the default path (`PI_CTXMODE_EXT` unset) and `${DEST%.ts}/go.mod` exists, install.sh now removes any stale `ctxmode.ts` file/symlink (self-heal), prints `skipped: native Go extension active at <dir>`, and exits 0. Explicit `PI_CTXMODE_EXT` installs and the md5 idempotency path are unchanged. Also aligned with the codegraph-go authoritative template: `PI_CTXMODE_EXT` ending in `/` is rejected before the directory check, and `mkdir -p` / `install` failures now report via `fail` instead of relying on `set -e` alone. Covered by `TestPiInstallScript_NativeGoExtensionGuard` in `deploy_test.go`.

- **Role guards review round 5: `typeof`/`==` `require('child_process')` regression locked and deploy.sh edge cases hardened.** `jsScanRequire` already required the token left of `require` to be `=` (and the token left of that an identifier), so `typeof require('child_process')`, `const x = typeof require('child_process')` and `x === require('child_process')` never bound `typeof`/`x` into the spawn-alias map and correctly fell through to `unresolved` — regression subcases now lock that behaviour in `TestGuard_CodeStaticSpawns` (allResolved=false) and `TestGuard_CtxRunCodeAliasAndFallback` (blocked on agent/herdr/herdsman co-occurrence, free without it). `deploy.sh` hardens two operational edges: the atomic-deploy fragment now also installs `cleanup_tmp` on `EXIT` (covering `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM` exits that bypass the `ERR` trap, so a signal mid-deploy cannot leave the staged temp file or the live binary unlocked — rollback's `cleanup_roll_err` gains the same re-lock for its `unlock_immutable "$BINARY"` window), and `rm -f -- "${BINARY}.prev"` now runs between `unlock_immutable "${BINARY}.prev"` and `cp -- "$BINARY" "${BINARY}.prev"` so a second deploy under a non-root user no longer dies on `Permission denied` against a read-only 0444/0555 `.prev` left by a prior run (covered by `TestDeployFragment_SecondDeployOverReadonlyPrevSucceeds`, which re-runs the fragment as an unprivileged uid when the tests run as root). The oracle-ctx-r2 `consider` observations C6–C8 (`batch` has no `stdin` field — all inline payloads live in `command` text screened by the shell guards; `run_task` takes only kind/target/args/env with argv+env still screened for sub-agent and hook surfaces; `withScreenCwd` relies on the MCP server's serialized pre-flight while real concurrency rides on per-`exec.Cmd.Dir`) are archived in `.agents/notes/2026-11-01-role-guards-non-sandbox-boundaries.md`.

- **Role guards review round 4: sub-agent code-screen alias resolution and partial-parse short-circuit closed.** `codeStaticSpawns` is rewritten as `codeStaticSpawnsDetailed(language, code) (argvs, shellCmds, allResolved)`: Python `import subprocess|os|pty as <alias>` and `from <module> import <sym> [as <alias>]` (comma lists and parenthesized multiline forms, covering `run`/`Popen`/`call`/`check_*`/`getoutput`/`getstatusoutput`/`system`/`popen`/`exec*`/`spawn*`) and JS `require('child_process'|'node:child_process')` name/destructure bindings plus ES `import` clauses now add their bound names to the static keyword scan — so `import subprocess as sp; sp.run(['herdsman','status'])` screens at the argv position and passes while `sp.run(['herdsman','daemon','start'])`/`sp.run(['herdr','pane','close','w1:p2'])`/`sp.run(['pig'])` block with the concrete command detail. Partial-parse short-circuit is eliminated: `allResolved` is true only when at least one spawn call was statically resolved AND no spawn-capable site resisted parsing — a variable/computed argv (`subprocess.run(cmd)`), an unclosed call, `from subprocess|os|pty import *`, a dynamic or expression-escaped `require(...)`/`import('child_process')` (`x(require('child_process'))`, `global.cp = require(...)`, `await import('child_process')`), and every non-call use of a module root or bound spawn name (`getattr(subprocess,'run')`, `f = subprocess.run`, `x = cp.execSync`, `g(subprocess)`) all flip `allResolved` false, so one harmless literal call can no longer silence the fallback for an adjacent dynamic spawn. Receiver precision keeps ordinary methods out of the spawn set (`platform.system()`, `os.path.join('system','x')`, `m.execSync('ls')` stay free) while spawn-bearing receivers (`subprocess.`, `os.`/`pty.` member chains, bound aliases, `require('child_process').`) still claim their calls. The `!allResolved` fallback now blocks on the bare presence of `herdr`/`herdsman` (the `programNamesBlockedAgent` rule) instead of the old verb co-occurrence list that missed `daemon`/`wake`; literal string-form commands (`os.system("cat f | pig")`, `subprocess.getoutput('herdsman daemon start')`, `execSync('a | b')`) are screened as shell text through `guardShellSubagentDepth` rather than silently dropped. Regression coverage added in `TestGuard_CodeStaticSpawns` and `TestGuard_CtxRunCodeAliasAndFallback`; the `CTXMODE_ROLE=worker` + `CTXMODE_READONLY=1` strict-precedence rule is now documented in the `role_guards.go` classification header, and the round-1 oracle `consider` observations are archived in `.agents/notes/2026-11-01-role-guards-non-sandbox-boundaries.md`.

- **Role guards review round 3, final pass: strace/xargs operand arity, sed truncation bounds and the sqlite3 URI contract closed.** `skipStraceArgs`/`straceArgHasValue` drop `--output-append-mode` and `--seccomp-bpf` from the two-slot set — both are GNU boolean switches, so `strace -f --seccomp-bpf pig` peels to `pig` and `strace --output-append-mode -o /tmp/t ls` still hits `strace -o` — while `-X`/`--const-print-style` and `-U`/`--summary-columns` (which carry separate values) are added in their place, keeping the two tables at parity. `xargsTargetIndex`'s short-option cluster loop now stops at lowercase `e` (GNU `-e[eof-str]` consumes the rest of the cluster as a glued optional operand, never the next argv slot), so `xargs -en pig` resolves to `pig` while `xargs -n 1 pig` still consumes the `1`. `sedExecCommands` and `sedScriptHasWrite` no longer panic on truncated scripts: the pattern/replacement scans bound-check `\` escapes and bail on a missing middle/closing delimiter (`sed 's/'`, `'s/a'`, `'s/a\\'`, `'s/a/b\\'` all terminate cleanly instead of slicing past the end). `sqlite3.connect` read-only detection is rewritten around Python's real contract: only a static `file:`-URI literal carrying `mode=ro` in the `database` position (or `database=` kwarg) TOGETHER with an explicit `uri=True` keyword exempts the call — `sqlite3.connect('file:/tmp/x.db?mode=ro')` (no `uri=True`), `sqlite3.connect('/tmp/x.db?mode=ro')`, `uri=False`, `*args`/`**kwargs` unpacking and `'file:...' + suffix` concatenations all fail closed. Bare `from os import` spellings of `execv`/`execvp`/`spawnl`/`spawnv` already hit `codeSpawnPrims`; regression assertions added.

- **Role guards review round 3: `scanInlineSubstitutions` index/recursion defects closed.** The double-quote inner loop consumed a `\\` escape one byte at a time (`i++`), stopping on the escaped byte, so `\"` inside `"..."` re-terminated the quote and let an enclosing `'...'` span swallow a `` `...` `` substitution (`echo hi > "\"'`pig`'"` bypassed `subagent_nested_agent`); the escape now skips the whole pair (`i += 2`). The in-quote backtick branch advanced `i += j`, landing on the closing backtick itself so the outer `i++` skipped it — adjacent `` `a`x`b` `` groups re-paired the closer as an opener and emitted a spurious segment for the gap text; it now advances `i += j + 1`. All four substitution branches appended the `depthExceeded` marker at the cap but still recursed into `splitShellSegmentsDepth`; they now mirror `scanInner` and stop at the marker. Regression coverage added in `TestGuard_InlineSubstitutionEscapes`.

- **Role guards review round 2: sed/awk/watch operand-model gaps, arithmetic empty words and depth-marker false positives closed.** A new shared `sedScripts` scanner feeds both `screenSedSubagent` and `screenSedScriptWrite`: it tracks `sawExplicit` (any `-e`/`--expression`/`-f`/`--file`, glued or cluster form) and `endOpts` (`--`), walks short-option clusters letter-by-letter (`-ne'1e pig'` glue, `-nf`, GNU `-l N`), and only treats the first positional as a script when no `-e`/`-f` appeared — so `sed -ne'1e pig' /etc/hosts`, multi-`-e` runs, and `sed -- '1e pig' f` are screened while an input file named `warm.txt` after `-e '1p'` is no longer misread as a script. `awkProgramFrags` honours `--` too (`awk -- '-1 {system("pig")}' f` parses the operand as program text, not an option cluster). `peelResolvedDepth`'s `watch` branch drops the single-word `peelResolvedDepth` shortcut: default-mode operands always screen through the shell-text path so `watch 'pig;id'` / `watch 'echo>/tmp/x'` / `watch '$(pig)'` hit, and `skipWatchArgs` walks clusters (`-dn 1`, `-dx`, `-n1`) so `-x` in a bundle still switches to the exec path. `scanInlineSubstitutions` only emits the `depthExceeded` marker when a `$(...)`/`` `...` `` is actually present at the cap (a max-depth `>/dev/null` no longer fails closed) and now scans backticks inside `"..."`. `$(( ... ))` at word position writes a `0` placeholder so a standalone `$((1+1))` no longer produces an empty head that `filepath.Base("") == "."` misread as `source`.

- **Role guards review round 1: shell-parser tearing, option-cluster arity, module opens and wrapper-chain writes closed.** `splitShellSegmentsDepth` no longer tears the enclosing command line at a word-position `$(...)`/`` `...` ``: the substitution's commands are staged as sibling segments that flush with their owner while a `__ctx_cmdsub__` placeholder keeps the outer argv whole — `git -C $(pwd) status` stays one command (the prior torn `[git -C] [pwd] [ status]` shape let the empty head trip `filepath.Base("") == "."` as a `.`/source write) and `A=$(true) pig` keeps the KEY=VAL prefix attached to the `pig` segment; a placeholder left in the resolved command HEAD fails closed as `dynamic command head` for both guards while placeholders in argument positions stay inert. Pure `(...)` subshell groups flush the enclosing segment and append their parsed commands directly; the redirect scans (arithmetic groups, redirect targets, unquoted heredoc bodies) stage into the same owner slot and never touch the outer word. A digit INSIDE a word is no longer an fd prefix (`python3>/dev/null -c ...` keeps its `python3` head; a bare `2>` still reads as an fd redirect). Interpreter option clusters stop at value-bearing letters: `python -Wmodule`/`-Xutf8` (glued or separate) ends the cluster scan so `-Wmodule`'s 'm' can no longer mask a later `-c`, and `inlineArgs` value letters (`-r`, `-M`, `-I`, `-d`, ...) break the cluster in every position so `ruby -rtime -e` / `perl -Mstrict -e` cannot misread the operand's letters as code flags; `hasInterpInPlaceFlag` adds `r`/`x`/`F` operand letters so `ruby -rtime` is not mistaken for `ruby -i`. `pythonWriteModeHit` covers the stdlib module-level opens (`gzip`/`bz2`/`lzma`/`tarfile`/`wave`/`aifc`/`sunau` — mode at positional 2, default read), `shelve.open` (default `flag='c'` creates the shelf), and `dbm.open`/`dbm.*.open` flag-aware mode screening (`'r'`/default read; `'w'`/`'c'`/`'n'`/non-literal write). `git --config-env=core.pager=VAR` now blocks like every other exec-arming config key (the env-sourced value cannot prove `cat`; `--no-pager`/`-c core.pager=cat` stay free). `wrapperWriteOpHit` walks the same wrapper/prefix chain `peelWrappers` peels (KEY=VAL, `env`, `command`, `builtin`, `exec`, `nohup`, `setsid`, `nice`, `timeout`, `stdbuf`, `busybox`, `ionice`, `taskset`, `chrt`, shell keywords, `--`) instead of stopping at the first non-strace head, and `straceArgHasValue` reaches parity with `skipStraceArgs` (`-a`/`-b`/`-O`/`-S`/`--columns` two-slot operands), so `env strace -o /tmp/t ls`, `FOO=1 nice time -o /tmp/t ls` and `strace -a 40 -o /tmp/t ls` all hit. `watch` leaves `peelWrappers` and resolves in `peelResolvedDepth`: with `-x`/`--exec` the operands are a direct exec argv; otherwise the joined operands are screened as `sh -c` text (GNU watch's real execution model — `watch 'pig --help'` / `watch -n 1 'rm -rf /tmp/x'` hit). `xargs` option arity drops lowercase `-e` from the separate-value set (it takes only a glued optional eof-str), so `xargs -e pig` resolves to `pig`. sed and awk argument scans support combined short-option clusters: sed `-ne`/`-nf`/`-rn` walk letters (`sed -ne '1e pig'`, `sed -ne 's/a/b/w /tmp/x'`, `sed -nf f.sed` hit) and awk gains `-e PROG`/`-ePROG`/`--source`/`--source=` fragments (joined with `\n`), `--file=FILE` and cluster-tail `f`/`e`/`F`/`v`/`i` operand consumption (`gawk -e 'BEGIN{system("pig")}'` hits; `-f` program files still block for strict read-only and are skipped for sub-agents). `awkProgramWrite` detects `system (` through the lexer (identifier + `openParenAfter`), so `system  (` and `system\t(` hit while a `/system\(/` regex literal cannot trip it. `heredocInterpLang` maps `bun` to javascript, and `codeSpawnPrims` adds the bare exec-family/spawn-lv entry points (`execv(`/`execve(`/`execl(`/`execle(`/`execlp(`/`execvp(`/`execvpe(`/`spawnl`/`spawnv`).

- **Mode-aware write screens now run on byte-aligned lexical masks, closing string-literal false positives and `ident (` misses.** `pythonWriteModeHit` and `jsFsOpenWriteHit` previously inspected `normCallSpace(code)` — the RAW text — so `print("open('/tmp/x','w')")` and `print("urlretrieve('http://x')")` were blocked, `os.open('/tmp/O_WRONLY_name', os.O_RDONLY)` fired on the path literal, and `open ('/tmp/x','w')`/`fs.openSync ('x','w')` spacing was handled only through a normalization that broke byte alignment whenever a preceding string contained `foo (bar)`. Both now match on the byte-aligned `pythonMask`/`jsMask` output (`len(masked) == len(code)`, no `normCallSpace` before slicing), locate the `(` via the new `openParenAfter` helper, split top-level arguments with `splitCallArgsOffsets` on the masked span, and decode mode/URI literals from the same span in the raw code — so string literals can neither fake nor hide a call, `os.open ('/tmp/x', os.O_WRONLY)` and `sqlite3.connect ('/tmp/db')` block while `os.open('/tmp/O_WRONLY_name', os.O_RDONLY)` passes, and the bare `openSync` entry checks its left identifier edge (`myopenSync` stays free). The single-argument `.replace(` rename check now runs on `normCallSpace(masked)` for python/javascript/typescript only — `Path(p).replace ('/tmp/y')` is caught while a Ruby string containing `.replace('x')` is not. A subprocess-level test compiles the binary and asserts `ctxmode index` under `CTXMODE_ROLE=reviewer|verifier` exits non-zero with the guard error on stderr before creating the `CTXMODE_DB` directory or touching `.git/info/exclude`. `role_guards.go` also drops a stale `//nolint:unused` on `guardTaintVars` (used by `screenUnsetArgs`).

- **`TestAudit_ReopenAfterRotationFailsKeepsRecord` no longer flakes under `-race` on CI.** The test forced the post-rotation reopen failure by tightening `RLIMIT_NOFILE` to one spare descriptor; FD churn from the race detector and other tests' finalizers could free a descriptor between the probe and the reopen, so the reopen succeeded and the assertion on `<path>.1` failed (`rotated generation = [{"seq":1}]`). `appendAuditLine` now opens through package var `auditOpenFile` (same pattern as `auditRotateBytes`); the test swaps it so the second open returns `EMFILE`, exercising the keep-writing-to-`.1` branch without process-wide FD limits.

- **Category tools tolerate the parameter spellings hosts actually send.** `ctx_run`'s `action` is now optional in the inferred MCP JSON Schema (`omitempty`) and the router normalizes a missing `action` — or the legacy `"run"` — to `execute`, including for the `background` guard, instead of rejecting the call with `missing properties: ["action"]`. `ctx_fs` accepts `action="list"` as an alias for `ls`. `ctx_git` accepts `limit` and `max_count` as aliases for `n` on `action=log` (`n` still wins when set), so those calls no longer fail schema validation with `unexpected additional properties`.

- **Removed three `staticcheck` `U1000` unused symbols so CI `staticcheck ./...` is green again.** `isDeniedEnvKey` (`executor.go`, superseded by `isDeniedEnvKeyForCaller` at every call site), `pythonModuleWrites` (`role_guards.go`, superseded by `pythonModuleHit`), and `tokenSet.hasAnyIdent` (`role_guards.go`, no remaining callers) are deleted with their comments; no behaviour change.

## [5.2.0] - 2026-10-22

### Fixed
- **`-workdir` always takes precedence as `workdirs[0]`.** `main.go` previously skipped prepending `-workdir` when the path already appeared later in `config.yaml`'s `workdirs` list (e.g. `/tmp` at a non-first index), leaving `workdirs[0]` (and therefore the per-workdir database and default `cwd`/`.` resolution) bound to the config's first entry instead of `-workdir`. `absWd` is now unconditionally prepended and deduplicated in order so `-workdir` is always `workdirs[0]`.
- **`ctx_stats` (`json=true`) serializes `audit_session` counters.** `auditAgg` in `stats.go` had unexported fields, causing `json.Marshal` to emit `"audit_session": {}`. Exporting `Calls`, `Output`, `Indexed`, and `Truncated` with `json:"calls"|"output"|"indexed"|"truncated"` tags restores the session audit breakdown in JSON mode.
- **`ctx_kb action=purge` (`scope=project`) rebuilds FTS5 indexes and truncates WAL on vacuum.** `Store.PurgeAll` (`store.go`) now runs `INSERT INTO documents_fts(documents_fts) VALUES('rebuild')` and `INSERT INTO documents_trigram(documents_trigram) VALUES('rebuild')` inside the delete transaction so FTS5 shadow-table tombstones are cleared before `Store.Vacuum` runs `VACUUM`, and `Store.Vacuum` follows `VACUUM` with `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` so `-wal` is truncated to zero and `freed_bytes` reflects the reclaimed space.
- **`toolExecuteFile` reuses `sensitiveInodes` across both hardlink checks.** When the target file has `Nlink > 1`, the initial `Stat` check and the post-`O_NOFOLLOW` `fdInfo` check now share a single `collectSensitiveInodes()` scan per call, and `collectSensitiveInodes` skips `skipWalkDirs` plus `.cache`, `.cargo`, `.rustup`, `.npm`, and `.pnpm-store` (while still walking credential-bearing directories such as `.ssh`, `.aws`, `.gnupg`, `.kube`, `.gemini`, `.pi`, `.grok`, `.config`); `README.md` (`Security model — NOT a sandbox`) documents this scan trade-off.
- **Baseline test & hook alignment.** Updated `TestVersionAligned` (`fixes_test.go`) to `5.1.0` (and fixed the double period in `README.md:7`), and restored the two `sk-` secret regex lines in `githooks/pre-push` to match `githooks/commit-msg`.

### Changed
- **`ctx_bg` accepts `status` and `output` as backward-compatible action aliases.** `toolCtxBg` (`router.go`) routes `status` to `list` and `output` to `log` at runtime so callers using legacy rule wording succeed without error (kept out of the public JSON Schema enum to avoid steering models toward legacy names).
- **Audit read-only role set includes `verifier` and Pi adapter forwards role env.** `readonlyAuditRoles` (`audit.go`) and both role lists in `README.md` add `verifier`; `integrations/pi/ctxmode.ts` exports `resolveRoleEnv(argv, baseEnv)` to infer `CTXMODE_ROLE` (and `CTXMODE_READONLY=1` for `scout|planner|researcher|reviewer|oracle|verifier`) from `--name role-<role>-<8hex>` when unset and passes it to the spawned `ctxmode` process (covered by a standalone `test()` in `integrations/pi/ctxmode.test.ts`).
- **Extended sensitive credential filename deny list.** `isSensitiveCredentialBase` (`main.go`) and `rgSystemBounded` (`fs_tools.go`, using trailing `*` globs including `!hosts.yml*` and `!hosts.yaml*`) now also block `antigravity-oauth-token`, `auth.json`, `logins.json`, `plugin-auth.json`, `hosts.yml`, and `hosts.yaml` (plus backup-suffixed variants).
- **Schema, host documentation, and gate closure note for `agy` (Antigravity CLI) and `ctx_kb.scope`.** Added `jsonschema` description on `ctxKbArgs.Scope` (`router.go`), documented `agy` MCP host invocation in `router.go`, `instructions.go`, and `README.md` (with Ultra/Pro `ctx_kb.json` and `instructions.md` host caches synced after `./deploy.sh` deploys the new binary), and recorded the 9-item audit summary, SF-4 exemption, and C-1–C-5 closure in `.agents/notes/2026-10-21-agy-support-audit-and-gate-closure.md`.

## [5.1.0] - 2026-10-18

### Fixed
- **`fix(githooks): harden installer safety and sync repo hooks`.** `scripts/install-hooks.sh` no longer treats `.git/hooks/`, `.git/hooks/.` or `.git/hooks//` as a different directory from `.git/hooks`: trailing slashes and a trailing `/.` are stripped before `core.hooksPath` is resolved, and the comparison uses `realpath` (falling back to `readlink -f`, then `pwd -P`). `.git/hooks` being a symlink is rejected on the undecorated path, and the destination is resolved again immediately before the copy and refused unless it is still the repository `.git/hooks`. A hook name that is itself a symlink is removed (the link, not the file it points at) before `cp -P`, so the copy can no longer follow the destination symlink and overwrite a file outside the repository. `githooks/pre-push` now fails closed when the sibling `commit-msg` is missing or not executable, instead of skipping format, noise-word and in-message secret checks (and instead of falling through to a global `commit-msg`). `CTXMODE_COMMIT_MSG_FALLBACK` may name a validator used only when the sibling file is absent, for tests.

### Changed
- **`ctx_fs action=resolve|related` is exposed in the Pi extension.** `integrations/pi/ctxmode.ts` updates `ctx_fs`'s `action` enum and description from `ls|glob|stat|rg` to `ls|glob|stat|rg|resolve|related` (matching `router.go`'s `ctxFsArgs` and tool description; `path` and `limit` were already present on the Pi parameter schema), and `README.md` / `integrations/pi/README.md` remove the former boundary note that `resolve` and `related` were omitted by the Pi bridge. One test in `integrations/pi/ctxmode.test.ts` asserts the six-action enum, description, and `tools/call` forwarding for both actions (measured red before the change, green after).
- **Drop retired `prism` from the related-repositories list in `AGENTS.md`.** The local, gitignored `AGENTS.md` removes the archived `prism` bullet (`- `prism`：同属 AI 基础设施`) from `## 关联仓库`, leaving `codegraph-go` as the active related repository; historical notes under `.agents/notes/` stay untouched (recorded in `.agents/notes/20261016-drop-prism-related-repo.md`).
- **Close the retired `prism.ts` extension-sync backlog item.** Add `.agents/notes/20261016-close-prism-backlog.md` (clause-level `supersedes` on `.agents/notes/20261004-pi-ext-deploy-sync.md:37`) recording that `prism` has been removed from `/root/workspace/pi-extensions` (`21411bd`), its deployed copy `/root/.pi/agent/extensions/prism.ts` deleted, and its local repository and runtime artifacts purged, while keeping the historical note file untouched.

## [5.0.0] - 2026-10-09

### Changed
- **BREAKING: `ctx_kb action=stats` reports `kept_out_bytes` instead of `saved_estimate_bytes`.** The old JSON field name is no longer returned, so an external consumer that reads it now sees a missing key. The old field was `totalOutput - totalInput`, i.e. the raw byte counters of the commands this server ran, so an output that was returned to the caller verbatim was still counted as "saved" almost in full. The field is now the session's kept-out total — the same figure `ctx_stats` reports as `total_kept_out` — and is named `kept_out_bytes`. `total_input_bytes` / `total_output_bytes` are kept (they are legitimate raw quantities) with their descriptions now stating that they are command input/output bytes, not a context-saving measure.
- **kept-out accounting moved into the `Store` (every KB write path is now covered).** `Store` gains an optional `onIndexedBytes func(int)` callback invoked after `Store.Index` and `Store.ReplaceExactAndChunks` commit successfully (`Index` passes the input `content` length, `ReplaceExactAndChunks` the sum of the chunk lengths, both measured before `neutralizeDefMarker` rewrites them); `main` installs it via the new `server.attachStore` so it feeds `statRecordKeptOut("store", n)`. The `statRecordKeptOut` call inside `storeIndexLocked` is removed to avoid double counting. Two paths that previously counted nothing are therefore counted now: `ctx_run action=batch`'s direct `Store.Index` writes and `ctx_kb action=fetch`'s `Store.ReplaceExactAndChunks` chunked writes. Only successful writes are counted, the pseudo-tool row stays `store`, and a nil callback stays nil-safe.
- **`ctx_stats` is registered in the Pi extension.** `integrations/pi/ctxmode.ts` now returns `ctx_stats` from `getTools()` and registers it via `pi.registerTool` (label, Chinese description, `promptSnippet`, `promptGuidelines`, `parameters: { json?: boolean }`, `execute` → `run("ctx_stats", params, signal)`), matching the other five tools. The tool was already advertised by `instructions.go` and `README.md` but was unreachable from Pi.

### Fixed
- **`ctx_stats` no longer advertises an exemption that no longer exists.** The tool description in `router.go`, the `instructions.go` playbook line, the `README.md` `ctx_stats`/`batch`/`ctx_kb stats` sections, the `stats.go`/`store.go`/`main.go` comments and the `integrations/pi/README.md` boundary paragraph all stated (or implied) that kept-out bytes were counted only where output went through `storeIndexLocked`, and that `ctx_run action=batch` and `ctx_kb fetch` were not counted. They now describe the full coverage above.
- **Regression tests for the two accounting gaps** (`stats_test.go`, appended to the existing file): `TestStoreLayerKeptOutCoversBatchDirectIndex`, `TestStoreLayerKeptOutCoversFetchChunks` and `TestToolStatsKeptOutBytesIsKeptOutTotal`. All three were measured red against the pre-change code (the batch/fetch probes saw no `store` row at all, and `ctx_kb action=stats` returned `saved_estimate_bytes: 99000` for a 100000/1000 output/input pair) and are green after.

## [4.3.0] - 2026-10-07

### Added
- **`ctx_stats` — per-session context-consumption statistics (new read-only MCP tool, ported from the original context-mode MCP server's `ctx_stats`, mksglu/context-mode).** The tool reports, per top-level tool (`ctx_run`/`ctx_fs`/`ctx_git`/`ctx_kb`/`ctx_bg`/`ctx_stats`), the call count, the bytes of text handed back to the caller (the actual context cost), the estimated tokens (bytes ÷ 4, the same heuristic context-mode uses), and the bytes written into the KB instead of the context window by the auto-indexing paths (oversized rg hit sets, truncated run output, `ctx_kb index` payloads), plus the overall savings ratio. Two accounting paths feed it: a generic `counted()` wrapper around every registered tool handler (router.go) sums each response's `TextContent` bytes into per-tool in-process counters (new `stats.go`; fields `statMu`/`statTools` on `server`, plus `startedAt` for the uptime header), and `storeIndexLocked` (main.go) records the byte length of every KB write that goes through it under the pseudo-tool row `store` — the choke point the auto-indexing paths funnel through, which keeps the instrumentation at one line instead of eleven call sites. That choke point is not a fence around every KB write: `ctx_run action=batch` writes through `Store.Index` directly and `ctx_kb fetch` through `indexContentLocked` → `Store.ReplaceExactAndChunks`, so neither is counted (see the `ctx_stats` section of `README.md`). A second section aggregates the existing ctx_run audit JSONL (new `aggregateAuditLog`, scoped to the current `session_id`; oversize lines are skipped, not fatal) for a lifetime view of calls / raw output / auto-indexed / truncated counts. `json:true` switches the whole report to machine-readable JSON. Read-only annotations. One behavioural nuance measured during review: the go-sdk dispatches concurrent `tools/call` requests on separate goroutines, so counters are recorded after each handler returns — a `ctx_stats` call racing an in-flight sibling may not include that sibling's row yet; sequential callers (the normal agent pattern) always see complete rows, and the smoke test confirmed correct counts under sequential requests (1 `ctx_run` call → 12B returned / 3 est. tokens).
- **`ctx_fs action=resolve` and `action=related` — fuzzy path resolution and related-file ranking (ported from the pi-fff extension's `resolve_file` / `related_files` tools, ShpetimA/pi-fff; reimplemented in pure Go with no `@ff-labs/fff-node` dependency, per the repo's zero-Node rule).** `resolve` takes an approximate path (`path`, `@path`-style references and surrounding quotes accepted) and returns fuzzy-ranked file candidates across all workdirs: exact path/basename match > suffix > basename substring > path substring > in-order subsequence (`fuzzyScore` in new `fs_resolve.go`), with boundary anchors (`/`, `_`, `-`, `.`), a contiguity bonus, and a span normalization so a scattered subsequence over a long path cannot outrank a close substring. `related` takes an existing file and returns ranked related files with a human-readable `reason`: test/impl pairs in the same language family share the strongest score (the stem comparison strips the `_test.go` / `.test.ts` / `.spec.ts` / `test_*.py` marker first, so `user.go` ↔ `user_test.go` and `user.ts` ↔ `user.tsx` pair up regardless of directory), then same-stem other-extension, then same-directory siblings, then stem-prefix matches. Both actions reuse the glob walk's existing fences — `skipWalkDirs`, `.gitignore` layers, symlink fencing via `ensureInsideWorkspaces`, the sensitive-path gate — under a 5-second wall-clock budget and a 100k-file cap (consuming the rg wall-clock budget pattern already established by `ctx_fs rg`), and both respect `limit` (default 10, max 2000) with a `truncated` flag, set both when `limit` cuts the hit list and when the 5-second budget or the 100k-file cap stops the workspace walk short, so a partial scan is never reported as a complete result. Tool description and `jsonschema` action enumerators updated to `ls|glob|stat|rg|resolve|related`. Tests in `fs_resolve_test.go` cover the scoring tiers, the ranking/fence behaviour (vendor and `.env` must not leak into results), the `@` prefix and limit handling (including the quoted-`@` spelling, which is matched literally), the test/impl pairing matrix across Go/TS/Python conventions, the sibling-over-stem-prefix tier order, the truncated flag on budget/cap exhaustion, and the stem-splitting helpers; tests in `stats_test.go` cover the counters, the wrapper, the audit aggregation session filter (mixed-session lines and non-JSON lines are handled), the text/JSON rendering (including the zero-division-free savings ratio), and the humanizer helpers.
- **The Pi extension sync in `deploy.sh` is now a standalone script with an explicit contract** (`integrations/pi/install.sh`, new; `deploy.sh` calls it in place of the old inline block). The old block `install`ed `integrations/pi/ctxmode.ts` onto `${PI_CTXMODE_EXT:-$HOME/.pi/agent/extensions/ctxmode.ts}` unconditionally — even when the deployed copy already matched the repository, so every deploy rewrote the file and touched its mtime for nothing — with no `md5sum` availability check and no validation of `HOME` or the target shape. The extracted script aligns its contract with codegraph-go's `integrations/pi/install.sh` (its `deploy.sh` and `.agents/notes/20261003-pi-ext-deploy-sync.md` are the template): md5 idempotence (an identical target md5 reports `unchanged` and does not rewrite the file, so its mtime is untouched), a `command -v md5sum` pre-check, an explicit checkable `HOME` (not `set -u`'s opaque "unbound variable"), a destination that must be an absolute **file** path (an existing directory fails explicitly instead of being silently written into), an `md5_of` helper that no longer swallows `md5sum`'s stderr or exit code, an empty source/target md5 as a hard failure, and a closing `/reload` hint. Path resolution and the override variable keep their existing `deploy.sh` semantics (`PI_CTXMODE_EXT`, default `$HOME/.pi/agent/extensions/ctxmode.ts`), so no existing invocation changes. The failure policy stays `deploy.sh`'s own: a failed sync is a hard failure that aborts the deployment — the previous block also exited 1 on a missing source, and under `set -e` any `install` failure was fatal too — which is deliberately **not** codegraph-go's non-blocking WARN (the two repositories' deployment semantics differ; reasoning in `.agents/notes/20261004-pi-ext-deploy-sync.md`). A follow-up in the same round fixes the target-resolution order: an explicit `PI_CTXMODE_EXT` is now honoured before `HOME` is required — the pre-fix order checked `HOME` first, so an explicit override was still rejected with the `HOME is not set` failure even though that very message advises setting `PI_CTXMODE_EXT` explicitly (measured before the fix: `env -u HOME PI_CTXMODE_EXT=/tmp/ext.ts bash integrations/pi/install.sh` → `install.sh FAILED`, exit 1) — matching the same fix in the other copies of the script; the directory-target failure message is reworded to `(须指定文件名而非目录本体)` for cross-repository consistency. No other semantics changed (md5 idempotence, the `md5sum` pre-check and the `/reload` hint are untouched).
- **Hook notes addendum records the second adversarial-review pass's observation items (R1–R3).** In `.agents/notes/20261003-hook-header-filter-structural.md`, R1 notes that the "repos without a repo-local `core.hooksPath` override skip `pre-commit`" wording did not add that those repos' `commit-msg` stage still scans the staged diff for secret patterns (measured: `/root/.git-hooks/commit-msg` itself rejects a GitHub-token-shaped string), so that wording must not be read as "secrets are unchecked there"; R2 records that `TestVersionAligned` in `fixes_test.go` compares a hardcoded literal and does not parse `CHANGELOG.md`, so it catches a forgotten `Version` bump but not a changed `Version` paired with an unchanged CHANGELOG heading; R3's original anchor is corrected: the `pre-commit` wording appears only in the then-unreleased `[4.2.0]` section and not in `[4.1.0]`, and the `global pre-commit` phrase is itself a product of the same earlier misreading and had already been corrected in `bd83f34`, so no historical section needs scrubbing; the principle that historical release sections are not reworded still stands. Documentation only; no behaviour change.

### Fixed
- **Audit rework on the then-unreleased `ctx_stats` and `ctx_fs resolve|related` port (verifier/reviewer/oracle pass on `175eb05`; items R1–R5).** Nothing outside those items was touched; the two features were then unreleased, so the entries above are corrected in place where they stated something the code did not do. **R1** — `fs_resolve.go` dropped the unused second return value of the `relatedPairRules` lookup (`pairExts, _ :=` → `pairExts :=`), clearing the `staticcheck` S1005 (`unnecessary assignment to the blank identifier`) that failed the first CI gate. The lookup result is unchanged. **R2** — `action=related`'s tier `switch` tested the stem-prefix tier (score 30) before the same-directory sibling tier (score 40), so a file that is both a directory sibling *and* carries a ≥4-character stem that prefixes the target's stem could only ever land in the 30 tier, leaving the documented 40 tier unreachable for it; the `dir == targetDir` case now precedes the stem-prefix case, matching the order documented in `README.md`, this file and `.agents/notes/20261007-port-features-from-originals.md`. The 100/90/70 tiers and the relative order of the remaining cases are unchanged. **R4** — `scanWorkspaceFiles` stopped on the 5-second budget or the 100k-file cap by returning `filepath.SkipAll`, which `filepath.Walk` swallows and turns into `nil`, so both callers treated a half-scanned workspace as a complete result and reported `truncated: false`; the early stop is now returned as an explicit bool from `scanWorkspaceFiles` (via a new `scanMaxFilesOverride` hook on `server`, so the branch is reachable from a test without a 100k-file fixture) and the `resolve` and `related` responses set `truncated: true` for it. Exhausting the budget stays a partial result, not a hard error. **R3** — documentation only, no write path changed: the kept-out byte counter is recorded by `storeIndexLocked`, which only the auto-indexing paths go through (oversized `ctx_run` `execute`/`execute_file`/`run_task` output, `ctx_fs rg` auto-index, `ctx_kb index`, and the legacy JSON migration re-index), while `ctx_run action=batch`'s direct `Store.Index` write and `ctx_kb fetch`'s `indexContentLocked` → `Store.ReplaceExactAndChunks` write are counted by nothing; `README.md` (the `batch` and `ctx_stats` sections), the `ctx_stats` entry above, the `router.go` `ctx_stats` tool description (the string a model actually reads when choosing the tool, which still carried the older `bytes kept out by auto-indexing (KB store)` wording with neither exemption), and the `stats.go` / `store.go` comments now agree instead of calling `storeIndexLocked` the single funnel for every indexing path. **R5** — three cases added to the existing `fs_resolve_test.go` rather than a new file: the sibling-over-stem-prefix tier order (measured red against the pre-fix switch: `same-directory sibling must take the score 40 tier, got 30 (reason "stem prefix match")`; green after), the truncated flag when the file cap cuts the walk short (measured red against the pre-fix walk: `exhausting the file cap must set truncated=true`; green after), and the quoted-`@` input spelling (the bare `@path` prefix is stripped first and quotes are trimmed only afterwards, so `"@foo"` keeps its `@` and is matched literally, hitting nothing). Verification for the rework: `go build`, `go vet ./...`, `gofmt -l .` and `staticcheck ./...` clean, `go test -race ./...` and the Pi extension suite (`node --experimental-strip-types --test integrations/pi/*.test.ts`) green.

## [4.2.0] - 2026-10-03

### Changed
- **Deployment is run by the agent, not by hand.** The build/deploy rule no longer describes `./deploy.sh` as a manual, human-only step: the agent runs `./deploy.sh` directly (build → `initialize` verification → atomic replacement of the live binary), with no hand-off, confirmation or approval in between. `README.md`'s Deployment section states the same for readers of the repository; the local, gitignored `AGENTS.md` carries the matching wording. Nothing else about the build/deploy rule changed.
- **`ctx_run`: argv is no longer path-fenced** (`main.go` `validateArgv`, new `resolveArgvExe`/`looksLikePath`). The old branch rejected any `argv[0]` that was not a bare name or a path inside a workdir (`lexicallyInside` + `ensureInsideWorkspaces`). Only an empty `argv`/`argv[0]` is rejected now; otherwise `argv[0]` is normalized: an absolute path is `filepath.Clean`ed and kept, a relative path is joined onto the resolved `cwd` and cleaned, a bare name is passed through verbatim for `exec.LookPath` at spawn time. Absolute paths and bare names now mean the same thing — anything the server user can execute — which the security model already documented; the argv fence was the last place implying otherwise. The `ctx_fs`/`ctx_git` workspace fences (`resolvePath`, `ensureInsideWorkspaces`) and the `cwd` fence are untouched and still apply to `path`/`pathspec`; `run_task`'s `custom` kind inherits the same normalization.
- **argv mode now goes through the pre-execution screen** (`shell_screen.go`, new `screenArgv`, wired into the argv branch of `toolExecute`). The rules differ from the shell-string case because argv never reaches a shell: a NUL byte in any argument is a hard block (`argv command blocked (nul_byte)` — `execve` rejects such an argument with EINVAL, so the spawn could never succeed and the old behaviour was a confusing start failure), an argv whose joined bytes exceed 64 KiB is **warn-only** (never blocked: large build/codegen arguments are legitimate and are exactly what the argv path exists for), and the three `shellWarnPatterns` are matched against the space-joined argv so shapes like `sh -c 'curl … | sh'` are still logged. A warn hit calls `logShellScreen` and its rule name goes into the audit record.
- **Documentation states the new argv contract**: the `instructions.go` usage policy and `README.md` (the `execute` and `run_task` entries plus new security-model bullets) no longer claim argv is "path-validated"; they document that argv skips the shell entirely (no shell string, therefore no injection surface), that it is not path-fenced, and that the argv screen blocks only a NUL byte and warns on an over-length argv.
- **Tests**: `TestValidateArgv` now asserts an absolute path outside every workspace is allowed and preserved (and that `../` traversal is resolved rather than rejected); `shell_screen_test.go` gains `TestScreenArgv` (NUL block, oversize warn, idiom warn, at-cap must-pass) plus the `toolExecute` wiring check; new `audit_test.go` covers the full field set for a successful argv call, the bare-name `exec.LookPath` resolution, both tag rules, the argv NUL block record, and the batch/`run_task` injection points. Its `TestMain` points `CTXMODE_AUDIT_LOG` at a throwaway temp file for the whole test binary so test runs cannot append noise to the real audit log.

### Added
- **Execution audit log** (`audit.go`, new; pure stdlib). One JSON line per `ctx_run` call, from all four entry points (`toolExecute`, `toolExecuteFile`, `toolBatchExecute`, `toolRunTask`), written from a `defer` so early validation errors, spawn failures, timeout/reaper kills, background starts and large output that was auto-indexed instead of returned are each recorded exactly once and never decoupled from the call. Records calls (`ctx_fs`/`ctx_git`/`ctx_kb`/`ctx_bg` are not audited) and never subprocesses. Target is `CTXMODE_AUDIT_LOG`, default `/root/.local/state/ctxmode/audit.jsonl` (state, not `~/.cache`); the parent directory and the file are created `0700`/`0600` **on first create only** (an existing file keeps its mode), opened `O_CREATE|O_APPEND|O_WRONLY`, each complete line appended under one process-wide mutex so concurrent batch commands cannot interleave bytes, with the directory created on demand and the file rotated to `<path>.1` at 32 MiB. Fields: `timestamp`, `pid`, `session_id`, `action`, `role` (`CTXMODE_ROLE`), `caller_readonly` (`CTXMODE_READONLY`), `command_type` (`argv`/`command`/`file`/`task`), `raw_cmd` (capped at 64 KiB with a `…(truncated)` marker, cut on a rune boundary, and replaced by a `[redacted: … len=… sha256=…]` marker when the sensitive-content check hits), `resolved_exe` (bare names resolved through `exec.LookPath`, `/bin/sh` for the command form, the runtime binary for `execute_file`), `cwd`, `background`, `background_id`, `duration_ms`, `exit_code`, `error`, `screen_verdict`/`screen_rule`, `audit_tags`, `env_keys` (caller env **key names** only), `stdin_len` (a byte count, never the payload), `indexed`/`index_label`, `commands`, `first_failed_label`, `output_len`, `truncated`. The schema is fixed — every key appears on every line (empty string / 0 / false / `[]` when not applicable) so consumers never have to treat a missing key as a third state — and **stdout/stderr bodies are never recorded**: they can be huge and are the likeliest place for secrets. The file itself is plaintext and not secret-free: `raw_cmd` is the caller's command text (or `execute_file`'s path/language/`code`) and unformatted secrets outside the patterns can still be recorded. `batch` has no single exit status, so its line carries both the per-command `commands` array (`label`, `exit_code`, `screen_verdict`/`screen_rule`, `indexed`/`index_label`) and an aggregate (0 only when every command succeeded, otherwise the first failing code; summed output size; whether any command truncated; `first_failed_label`). The target is validated (`Lstat` + `O_NONBLOCK`, before the append mutex) so a symlink/FIFO/device/directory target is refused with one log line instead of stalling the tool surface; a write failure is logged and ignored — the audit is a record, never a policy, and must not turn the execution tools off.
- **Two record-only audit tags.** `readonly_role_call` when `CTXMODE_ROLE` ∈ {`scout`,`planner`,`researcher`,`reviewer`,`oracle`} or `CTXMODE_READONLY=1` (both set by the dispatch layer, so the tag is absent until that layer sets them); `eval_interpreter` when the invoked file name (`argv[0]`, after `exec.LookPath`, or the command an `env`/`busybox` wrapper hands over to) belongs to an interpreter family (`sh`/`bash`/`dash`/`zsh`/`ksh`/`env`/`busybox`/`node`/`nodejs`/`perl`/`ruby`/`php`/`Rscript` or `python*`) and a later argument contains an explicit inline-code spelling for it (`-c`/`-e`/`-p`/`-r` including bundled short options such as `-lc`/`-ec`/`-pe`, `--eval=…`/`--print`, `php -r`, `Rscript -e`, or a leading `-i` where that means "read the program from stdin"). It is a **spelling check over argv, not a detector**: known false positives (`bash -e file.sh`, `env -i cmd`) and the absence of any coverage guarantee are documented in `README.md`. Neither tag blocks, delays or modifies an execution, and neither is a permission model.

### Fixed
- **The commit gate's arrow-adjacent residual shapes are recorded, not fixed** (global `/root/.git-hooks/{commit-msg,pre-push}` — shared tooling in the image; documentation only, no behaviour change). A second verification pass measured four more narrow bypass spellings for the assignment rule: `password -> v12345`, `password =>> v12345`, `password = > v12345` and `password => => v12345` all pass, because once the separator is consumed the value side still begins with a single symbol (`-` or `>`) that becomes the "first token" and stays under the 6-character floor. `->` is not an assignment operator in any language (Perl/Ruby's `->` dereferences, `=>` is the hash assignment), and `=>>` / `= >` are the same family of pseudo-shapes; a value that does hit a known format (`sk-` / `AKIA` …) is still caught by the pattern layer. Decision: **record only, do not fix** — extending the separator table has diminishing returns, and "take the second token when the first one is a single-character symbol" would change the value semantics. The measured shapes and their provenance live in `.agents/notes/20261003-hook-header-filter-structural.md` (second verification pass, oracle session, 2026-10-03, after `f0c6050`).
- **The commit gate's credential assignment no longer matches only the bare separator and the bare keyword** (global `/root/.git-hooks/{commit-msg,pre-push}` — shared tooling in the image, not code shipped from this repository; the in-repo `githooks/` mirror is untouched). Two narrow escapes are closed, both in the assignment rule of the two hooks: the keyword alternative now tolerates an identifier suffix (`(password|api_key|secret)[A-Za-z0-9_]*`), so a name such as `SECRET_KEY`, `PASSWORD_HASH` or `API_KEY_ID` — keyword immediately followed by `_…`, which matched neither the whitespace nor the separator — is no longer skipped; and the separator alternative covers the arrow forms (`[:=]+>?`), so an arrow assignment no longer leaves the `>` behind as the "first token" and passes the 6-character floor. What did **not** change: the first-non-whitespace-token rule, the 6-character floor, the placeholder/env-var/`none` exemptions, the awk header filter, and the known over-strict side of matching a keyword as a substring — which is also the one deliberate behaviour change here: a word that merely contains a keyword and is followed by an assignment (e.g. `secretary`) moves from pass to reject, the price of catching the real `_KEY`/`_HASH` names. The template `/root/.git-templates/hooks/pre-commit` mirrors the header filter and has no assignment scan; it is this repository's effective pre-commit (the repo-local `core.hooksPath` is `/root/.git-templates/hooks`, where `commit-msg`/`pre-push` are symlinks into `/root/.git-hooks/` and `pre-commit` is a real file, so it does run here — an earlier wording that called it overridden by the global `core.hooksPath` was wrong, corrected 2026-10-03), and it was synced to the same awk state machine in the same round (sha256 `2b8b4be7dfba7d1546c4ca766f1391522e14b5aa1d6591f3c21f7f95af517245`, backup `pre-commit.bak-20261003143116`, 25-case regression green); this entry's change does not reach it. Old-vs-new differential, the 45-case matrix re-run and the measured residual list live in `.agents/notes/20261003-hook-header-filter-structural.md`; previous hooks kept at `commit-msg.bak-20261003145322` and `pre-push.bak-20261003145322`.
- **The commit gate's diff header filter is structural, not prefix-based** (global `/root/.git-hooks/{commit-msg,pre-push}` — shared tooling in the image, not code shipped from this repository). A `+++ …` line now counts as a file header only when it directly follows a `--- …` line while still outside a hunk: `added_lines_only` became an awk state machine keyed on the `diff --git` opener and the `@@ ` hunk marker, so inside a hunk every `+` line is added content. The prefix filter it replaces (`grep -v '^+++ \(b/\|/dev/null\)'` plus the `"+++ b/"*|"+++ /dev/null"*` case arm in `commit-msg` and a bare `"+++"*` in `pre-push`) could not distinguish a header from an added line whose own text starts with `++ `, and the previous narrowing still dropped the `++ b/…` and `++ /dev/null …` spellings. Both remaining escapes are closed and both false positives are gone: a secret-shaped **file name** is no longer scanned, and skewed prefixes (`diff.noprefix=true`, `diff.mnemonicPrefix=true`) no longer make real headers survive the filter. This supersedes the "Residuals" paragraph of the *commit gate only scans added lines* entry below; the rest of that entry stands. The two credential scanners also stop re-applying a prefix skip of their own — the feeder is what removes headers now, and a prefix skip there would re-drop real added content. Second change in the same hooks: the assignment value is the **first non-whitespace token** after the separator (quotes/backticks then stripped) instead of the whole tail, so documentation prose on an `api_key` line that points at a runbook is no longer reported as a hardcoded credential; the existing 6-character floor and the placeholder/env-var/`none` exemptions are unchanged, and single-token assignments are still rejected. Measured residuals, all narrow: a first token of 6+ characters still trips the check (prose included), a diff format that omits the `diff --git` opener would over-report later files' headers, and secret-shaped file names stay unscanned. Method, old-vs-new differential and the 45-case end-to-end matrix live in `.agents/notes/20261003-hook-header-filter-structural.md`; previous hooks kept at `commit-msg.bak-20261003141636` and `pre-push.bak-20261003141636`.
- **The commit gate only scans added lines now.** The global `commit-msg` hook scanned every line of the staged diff, so removed and context lines could reject a commit that was in fact *removing* a credential-shaped line (its `"-")` case arm, meant to skip diff markers, was a typo for `"-"*`), and it dropped any added line whose content starts with `++` — diffs render those as `+++content`, indistinguishable from a file header by prefix alone — which let a real secret through. Both scanners now receive only the added lines, and the header filter is anchored on the two forms git actually emits for the added side (`added_lines_only`: `grep '^+'` plus `grep -v '^+++ \(b/\|/dev/null\)'`; the `check_credential_assignments` case arm is `"+++ b/"*|"+++ /dev/null"*`). Anchoring on `b/` and `/dev/null` rather than on a bare `"+++ "` closes the spelling that a first fix still dropped: an added line whose content starts with `++ ` renders as `"+++ …"`, so an assignment or a pattern-shaped credential in such a line — dropped whole until now — is caught. The `pre-commit` hook in this repository's configured hooks path (`/root/.git-templates/hooks/pre-commit`, a real file rather than a `commit-msg`/`pre-push`-style symlink) got the same filter (`git diff --cached -U0 | grep -v '^+++ \(b/\|/dev/null\)'`). Residuals: a line whose content starts with `++ b/…` renders as `"+++ b/…"` and is still dropped, and secret-shaped *file names* are not checked now that header lines are excluded (kept deliberately, low risk); a skewed header prefix (`diff.noprefix=true`, `diff.mnemonicPrefix=true`) makes real headers survive the filter, which can only over-report, never under-report. This is shared tooling in the image, not code shipped from this repository; the notes record the reasoning. Backup of the previous hooks: `commit-msg.bak-20260930094017`, `pre-commit.bak-20260930094017` (earlier: `commit-msg.bak-20260930082550`, `pre-commit.bak-20260930092955`).
- **Pre-commit review fixes for the two features above** (oracle/reviewer double review; items F1–F2, S1–S10, O1–O5/O8 of the fix package).
  - **F1 — the audit target is validated before it is opened.** `appendAuditLine` now `Lstat`s the target and refuses anything that is not the regular file ctxmode creates (symlink, FIFO, device, directory) with a once-per-path+reason log, `O_NONBLOCK` plus a post-open `Stat` re-check back that up, and the **`Lstat` validation** happens **before** `auditMu` is taken (the `O_NONBLOCK` open and its `Stat` re-check run inside it — they are what makes the in-lock open non-blocking) — a FIFO target could previously block inside the append mutex and queue every later `ctx_run` call behind it. This also corrects the earlier "audit problems never slow down an execution" claim: write failures still never fail a call, but the one failure that could have stalled the tool surface is now refused up front. Adds rotation to `<path>.1` at 32 MiB (S6). Tests: `TestAudit_TargetIsValidatedBeforeOpening`, `TestAudit_RotatesAtSizeCap`.
  - **F2 — a batch failure is no longer folded into 0.** The call-level aggregate reused `auditExitNotRun` (-1) as both its "no result yet" sentinel and a legitimate failure code, so a first command blocked by the screen, skipped on a shared timeout or killed at -1 was overwritten by the next command's 0. It now tracks `seen bool` separately and always keeps a failure. Test: `TestAudit_BatchFirstFailureNotFolded`.
  - **S1/S4/S5 — record shape.** Adds `pid` and `session_id`; `batch` lines carry a machine-readable `commands` array plus `first_failed_label`, replacing atomically-joined label lists (a label containing a comma used to be ambiguous); a background line records `background_id` and `exit_code: -1` instead of an `exit_code: 0` that never happened, with `output_len` documented as the start-message length.
  - **S2 — command-text redaction and inputs.** `raw_cmd` goes through the same `checkSensitiveContent` patterns used for indexing and becomes a length+digest marker on a hit; `env_keys` (key names only, sorted, capped at 64) and `stdin_len` (a count) are recorded. README drops "metadata and statistics only" and states that the file is plaintext and may contain credentials.
  - **S3/S10 — `run_task` joins the screen, `-i` false positive removed.** Every `run_task` kind (including `custom`) applies `applyArgvScreen`/`setScreen`, so no argv path is left unscreened; the `-i` "reads stdin" rule fires only as the first argument and is suppressed in `-m` module mode, so `python3 -m pip install -i URL` is no longer tagged.
  - **S7/S8 — `eval_interpreter` coverage and wording.** Handles bundled short options (`-lc`/`-ec`/`-pe`), `--eval=`/`--print`, `php -r`, `Rscript -e` and `env`/`busybox` unwrapping; README/CHANGELOG wording is narrowed to "a spelling check over argv, not a detector" with its known false positives and misses listed.
  - **S9 — uniform audit timing.** `toolExecute` and `toolExecuteFile` create the audit record before their required-field checks, so an empty `execute`/`execute_file` call is recorded like `batch`/`run_task` already were. Test: `TestAudit_EarlyValidationRecorded`.
  - **O1/O2/O3/O4/O5/O8.** `auditExeCache` is bounded (256) and only caches successes; the argv `oversized` warn logs no command head; `screen_verdict`'s unscreened value (`""`) and the `exit_code: -1` semantics (rejected call / background start) are documented; a failure on the default path logs a hint that it is root-specific; `TestMain`'s `os.Setenv` (unavoidable outside a `*testing.T`) and the literal default-path assertion are documented as deliberate; per-command screen verdicts/rules are recorded so a specific idiom rule is not hidden by another command's size block in the same batch.
  - **Known gaps (deliberate, unchanged):** audit covers `ctx_run` only; one line per **call**, never per subprocess; "append-only" means `O_APPEND`, not tamper-proof (same-uid processes can rewrite or delete the file); `readonly_role_call` stays absent until the dispatch layer sets `CTXMODE_ROLE`/`CTXMODE_READONLY`; the target check is `Lstat` + `O_NONBLOCK` rather than an `O_NOFOLLOW` open. Rationale and the full observation-item list live in `.agents/notes/`.
- **Round-2 review fixes** (oracle round 2: `R2-F1` must fix, `R2-S1`/`R2-S2`; documentation items `R2-D1`–`R2-D3`; reviewer items `R2-R1`–`R2-R3`).
  - **R2-F1 — a failed rotation no longer loses the line or wedges the log.** Rotation ran *after* `f.Close()` and returned on a rename error: that line was dropped, the file stayed over the cap, and every later append re-took the same failing path (measured causes: `<path>.1` is a non-empty directory, or the directory is not writable for the process — a same-directory rename is always within one filesystem, so `EXDEV` is unreachable; a target that is a mount point gives `EBUSY`). Rotation is now best effort and attempted **before** the write, while the descriptor is still open: a failed rename is logged once per reason and the current line is appended to the current file with the same descriptor, so no record is lost and no later append can fail permanently. The same invariant covers the second half of the rotation — if the rename succeeds but the replacement file cannot be opened, the line goes to the generation just rotated away instead of being dropped. Tests: `TestAudit_RotationFailureKeepsWriting` (an occupied `.1` directory, three appends, each recorded) and `TestAudit_ReopenAfterRotationFailsKeepsRecord` (the reopen failure forced with `RLIMIT_NOFILE`).
  - **R2-S1 — `resolveAuditExe` really shares `looksLikePath` now.** Its comment claimed a shared "what counts as a path" rule that the code did not implement (it looked for a separator only), so `.hidden`, `..x` and `a\b` were recorded as PATH-lookup results while the executor resolved them as paths. The audit side now calls `looksLikePath` itself, so both name the same file for every path-like spelling; the one remaining difference (empty-cwd fallback) is documented and unreachable from the current call sites. Test: `TestResolveAuditExeAgreesWithExecutor`.
  - **R2-D3 — env key names go through the sensitive-content check.** A key name that is itself credential-shaped (`AKIA…`, `ghp_…`, a JWT) is recorded as `[redacted]` instead of verbatim, and the list stays sorted, deduplicated and capped at 64. Values are still never inspected. README notes that names outside the pattern set are recorded as sent, so key names are potentially sensitive too. Test: `TestAuditEnvKeys`.
  - **R2-S2 / R2-D1 / R2-D2 / R2-R1 — documentation now matches the implementation.** README and the `audit.go` header state that only the `Lstat` runs before the append mutex (the `O_NONBLOCK` open plus `Stat` re-check run inside it), that only the **last path component** is checked — a symlinked parent directory still redirects the trail, a hardlink to a regular file is indistinguishable, and a hung NFS/FUSE/CIFS mount or frozen block device can still delay a write — that only the current file plus one `.1` generation are kept (≈2×32 MiB; older generations are silently overwritten, no archive, no cross-process lock, so two processes sharing a path can race the rotation) with external-archiving suggestions, and that refusing symlinks means `/dev/stdout`-style targets no longer work (containers must use a real file, a path inside a mounted volume, or a file the log collector tails).
  - **Recorded, not changed (R2-R2, R2-R3 and the reviewer's confirmations).** `O_NONBLOCK` stays set on the descriptor: on Linux it is ignored for regular files and clearing it via `F_SETFL` risks dropping `O_APPEND` for no real gain, so the NFS/CIFS/FUSE `EAGAIN` write path is a documented boundary instead. The multi-process rotation race and the single-generation window are likewise documented rather than fixed (single-process design assumption). Recorded as such in `.agents/notes/`.

## [4.1.0] - 2026-09-24

### Added
- **Agent collaboration scaffolding**: added `AGENTS.md` collaboration rules, `.agents/notes/` architecture decision records, and `scripts/notes-index.sh` indexing script.
- Align skeleton test commands with CI (glob Pi extension tests, race-enabled go tests) and unify notes-index.sh.

### Changed
- **`ctx_run` command form now uses a fixed, controlled shell.** `executor.go` gains `shellCommand(code)` (`exec.Command("/bin/sh", "-c", code)`, absolute path); `runShellOpts` and `batch.executeCommand` no longer read `os.Getenv("SHELL")` or split it with `strings.Fields` — the interpreter for shell `command` strings is no longer caller-controllable via the environment. Existing SHELL-based tests were rewritten to assert the real child interpreter (`readlink /proc/$$/exe`) is dash/sh under `SHELL=/bin/bash`, `"/bin/bash -l"`, unset, and a hostile `/tmp/evil`.
- **Graded pre-execution screen for shell commands** (`shell_screen.go`, pure stdlib). `screenShellCommand` returns `shellOK`/`shellWarn`/`shellBlock`. Blocks only two conditions: a NUL byte or a command >64 KiB. The limit is counted on the entire code segment as received by `runShellOpts` (i.e. after injection), and the check is sunk into `runShellOpts` so it covers both `execute` and `execute_file`; the empty string is still handled by the existing "command is required" error. For `execute_file` with the shell language this means the injected `FILE_CONTENT` plus the caller's `code` is measured together, so it is rejected once the two exceed 64 KiB even though the `execute_file` file-read limit is 10 MB; non-shell languages are written to a temp file and are not subject to this limit. Warns (logged, execution continues) on three heuristic idioms — `base64 -d … | sh`, `curl`/`wget … | sh`, and a redirect into `/etc/` or `/dev/sd[a-z]` — logging only the rule name, length, and first 80 bytes, never the full command. Wired into `toolExecute` (command/shell path only) and `executeCommand`, where a block fails only that one command's `batchResult` and never cascades to siblings.
- **Tool schema and docs now state the command/batch shell boundary.** `executeArgs.Command`, `ctxRunArgs.Command`, `batchCommand.Command`, the Pi bridge `ctx_run` guideline + `command` description, `instructions.go`, and `README.md` (execute/batch entries + a security-model note) all document the fixed `/bin/sh -c` semantics (SHELL ignored, no command allowlist, argv bypasses the shell and is path-validated) and that the command screen only logs heuristic warnings and is not a policy.

### Fixed
- **Pi bridge: Esc could not stop a running `ctx_run`** (`integrations/pi/ctxmode.ts`). Pi aborts the turn locally (`session.abort()` → the extension tool's `AbortSignal`), but the bridge never told the ctxmode server: the five `execute()` handlers dropped the third `signal` argument, and the hand-rolled MCP client had no `notifications/cancelled` path at all (its per-request `setTimeout` only rejected locally). The server-side kill path (`executor.go` `runCmd` `ctx.Done` → `kill(-pgid, SIGTERM)` → 3s → `SIGKILL` + descendant sweep) therefore never fired, so a `ctx_run`/`run_task` child kept running until its own default budget (execute 30s, run_task 5min, background 1h). Tools now accept and forward the signal; abort writes `notifications/cancelled` with the matching `requestId` (always after the `tools/call` line so it lands on an in-flight request) and immediately rejects the local wait instead of dangling until the client timeout; the client-side timeout path now cancels server-side too instead of orphaning the job. No server-side change — verified at the bridge level only: the bytes written to the server (`tools/call` followed by `notifications/cancelled`), the matched `requestId` and its ordering invariant (request before cancel), and local pending-request cleanup; the subprocess kill itself is the server-side `runCmd` path, not re-exercised end-to-end against the real binary here.
- **Pi bridge: an explicit `stop()` could be undone by an internal reconnect** (same file). `cleanup()` rejects in-flight calls with "ctxmode disconnected", and `callTool`'s reconnect branch then called `start()`, which unconditionally reset `this.stopped = false` — so stopping while a long `tools/call` was in flight (`/ctxmode-stop`, `/reload`, session exit) spawned a second `ctxmode` process the extension could no longer reference or stop. Reproduced: 2 spawns, the second alive. `start()` now refuses once the client has been explicitly stopped; explicit starts (`/ctxmode-start`, `session_start`) always construct a fresh `CtxmodeClient`, and crash-driven auto-restart is unaffected because `stopped` stays false there — both locked in by tests.

## [4.0.9] - 2026-09-13

### Fixed
- **Timing schema copy unified to ms**: the last three descriptions still using human units now state the bound in milliseconds — the `ctx_run` `background` jsonschema ("default max 1h" → "default max 3600000ms") and the Pi adapter's `background` parameter description and `ctx_bg` tool description ("max 1h" / "最大1小时" → "max 3600000ms"). No behaviour change: every tool-facing timing input was and remains `timeout_ms`/`ttl_ms` in ms with matching code defaults.

## [4.0.8] - 2026-09-13

### Fixed
- All 20 staticcheck findings resolved: dead assignments in the kill/drain and rg-line-drain paths removed, dead functions (superseded purges, unused runCompiled/isProbablyBinary wrappers) deleted, error strings normalized. No behavioural changes.

### Added
- **deploy.sh rollback subcommand**: the previous binary is kept as `.prev` on every deploy, and `deploy.sh rollback` verifies it through the same initialize check before atomically swapping it back.
- **KB backups**: every `context_mode.db` under the data directory is snapshotted weekly (Sunday 06:00 UTC) via `sqlite3 .backup` with per-database integrity checks, gzip, and 4-week retention into `/root/backups/ctxmode/`; restore documented in README.
- **CI quality gates**: staticcheck (pinned 2025.1.1) and the Pi-adapter TypeScript tests (`node --test`, Node 24 via setup-node) now run on every push; coverage baseline measured at 78.3% with new table-driven tests for the weakest newly-covered paths.

## [4.0.7] - 2026-09-13

### Fixed
- **migrateFromJSON rewritten**: the v4.0.6 whitelist rejected every real legacy document (the JSON era stored absolute workdir paths, and old session ids can never match the per-process random id). Legacy paths are now accepted only as absolute paths that resolve inside a workdir (EvalSymlinks + `..`-segment rejection + valid-UTF-8 + control-character checks), normalized to workdir-relative display paths, and re-checked by `isSensitiveFilePath`; the rg:/batch:/URL accept branches that only existed for hostile JSON are gone.
- **timeout branch of runCmd now bounds its final wait** (reapWaitBound, mirroring the ctx-cancel and batch branches), and `limitedBuffer` is mutex-protected, removing a data race when an abandoned Wait's copy goroutine is still writing.
- **fetch**: a fence-refused URL now deletes any pre-existing stale `fetch_cache` row (new `DeleteCached`) instead of leaving plaintext until the 7-day TTL.
- **isSensitiveFilePath false positives narrowed**: pgpass/netrc/git-credentials match exact names only (netrc.go / pgpass.md no longer flagged), private-key prefixes require a name boundary, and pure-numeric secondary suffixes are stripped (`prod.key.1`, `server.pem.20240901` now match).

### Changed
- Pi adapter stripANSI covers two/three-byte ESC forms with intermediate bytes and DCS/SOS/PM/APC sequences (verified by new TS tests).

## [4.0.6] - 2026-09-12

### Fixed
- **fetch persistence no longer bypasses the sensitive fence**: when indexing is refused, the page content is not written to `fetch_cache` (previously refused content persisted in plaintext for 7 days and could revive purged documents via cache hits).
- **migrateFromJSON hardening**: legacy JSON document paths are validated against a whitelist (session-namespace, rg/batch prefixes, fetch URL shapes, plain relative paths; no control characters, length-capped); malformed documents are skipped with a warning instead of aborting, and a corrupt migration file no longer fatals the server at startup.
- **setsid escapee reaping extended to the ctx-cancel and batch branches** (previously timeout-only): descendants are snapshotted and SIGKILLed before draining, and the final wait is bounded (5s) - a single `setsid` child can no longer hang an MCP call indefinitely.
- **killBackground fallback for unknown process start time**: entries whose /proc starttime could not be read are conservatively group-killed when the recorded pgid matches, instead of expiring never and occupying a background slot.
- **Sensitive-file name variants**: backup/secondary suffixes (`id_rsa.bak`, `prod.pem.txt`, `.pgpass.bak`, `netrc.bak`, `.htpasswd.old`, `.env~`, `pgpass`, `git-credentials.txt`) and private-key prefixes (`id_rsa_primary`, `id_ed25519_sk`) now match `isSensitiveFilePath`.
- **Pi adapter**: ctx_kb schema gains `confirm_phrase` (project purge was unusable from Pi), tool text is stripped of ANSI/OSC/CSI sequences before TUI rendering.

### Changed
- Shell execution is documented as `$SHELL -c` (not `sh -c`).

## [4.0.5] - 2026-09-12

### Fixed
